# Cross-cutting work

Part of [the plan](../PLAN.md). A unit id such as `6.E2` names a unit of the testing refoundation, an item under `## Testing refoundation units` below.

Work that belongs to no feature: CI and gates, dependencies, the test system, observability,
architecture rules, documentation and its checks.

## Work by subject (141)

### Architecture / quality

- **SAGA-UNIT-TESTS-ACCEPT-FAILED-SAGA**: The vitest saga suites tolerate a failed saga, so dropping the `return` of the command executor leaves them green; only the integration tier reads it. Line 121 accepts `"FAILED"` and line 180 accepts `currentStep >= 1 || RUNNING`. The executor is the one `SagaIntegration.registerSagaDefinitions` installs. Measured on 2026-11-10: mutation case `s01-saga-command-executor` is VACUOUS under two mutations in the vitest tier. The integration test asserts `COMPLETED` and `PUBLISHED` through `UpdatePostStatusStep`'s `result.success` (`packages/shared/src/saga.ts:1036-1038`), but the mutation tool's single-collector scope does not run that tier. Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/sagaIntegration.queue.test.ts:121,180`, `apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:289`, `apps/api/tests/integration/sagaPublishNowPromotion.integration.test.ts:102-103`, `packages/shared/src/saga.ts:1036-1038`, `docs/development/testing-refoundation/mutations/2.8.9c.record.json`. Done: The two assertions tightened to the state each case expects, and case `s01` re-run SAFE in the vitest tier. Found by: the testing refoundation (2026-11-10).
- **CORE-TESTS-TYPECHECK**: No gate typechecks the test files under `packages/core/*/tests/`. A slip such as `saved.id.value` on a possibly undefined mock call goes unnoticed, while the tests of `apps/api` have their own typecheck ratchet. Verified on 2026-11-09. Where: `packages/core/*/tsconfig.json` (includes `src/**/*` only), `packages/core/approvals/tests/unit/SubmitForReviewUseCase.test.ts:91`. Done: A typecheck over `packages/core/*/tests/**` in the battery and CI, ratcheted from the measured count with a retire condition, or the decision to keep them untyped recorded. The retire condition is that the ratchet, once at zero, becomes a hard zero; a decision to keep the tests untyped is recorded with its reason. Found by: the testing refoundation (2026-11-09).
- **AUDIT-STATS-ORDER-UNREAD**: Closed by #904. The `auditLogGroupBy` test double ignored the `orderBy` and the null filters `AuditService.getStats` sends, so no test read them (case `s02-stats-top-users-order` VACUOUS). The double now applies the `where`, keeps a null key as a group of its own and sorts by the `orderBy` it receives, and the four blocks of `getStats() - Ranking Order and Null Groups` read the count order and the null exclusion of the top users and the top resources, each shown red under a dropped `orderBy`, the user-id order of case `s02` and a dropped null filter. Re-run at its base `cc4d0d7a`, case `s02` still reads VACUOUS, because its deleted block runs there against the old double; its head side is now red. Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/auditService.stats.test.ts`, `mutations/2.8.9b.record.json`.
- **AUDIT-LOGS-RESOURCE-FILTER-UNREAD**: Closed by #904. Dropping the resource-id filter of `AuditService.getLogs` turned no test red, because the seed held a single Post log. The seed now holds a second Post log, of `post-test-2`, and the block `returns the log of the requested resource id and not another log of its resource` asserts that the filter returns the log of `post-test-1` alone, shown red with the filter dropped and under the mutation of case `a01`, which now reads SAFE. Where: `apps/api/src/audit/auditService.ts:170`, `apps/api/tests/unit/auditService.query.test.ts`, `mutations/2.8.9b`.
- **GATE-RETIREMENT-AUDIT**: Every active gate must answer whether it still detects anything; those that do not leave. 54 fitness checks, battery steps, lint rules, dependency gates and hooks are in scope. The Product Owner decided this on 2026-11-09, with the rule that a gate is needed while it detects and prevents an anomaly, and goes once the anomaly is corrected and cannot return, unless the audit shows it still can. Several gates were written for one remediation and keep running after it closed. Zero detections alone never retire a gate that prevents a reintroducible defect, such as the `@ts-ignore` class. Known candidates: the `PrismaUnitOfWork` term of #23 and the `sagaTenant` term of #40 are inert, and #1 is superseded by #21. The lint rules in scope are those at `error`. Where: fitness #1-#54, `scripts/testing/*-gate.mjs`, `.claude/hooks-py/`, `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md`. Done: One table, a row per gate (what it detects, its last detection, a retire condition), and every new gate names its retire condition from then on; owner Maintainer. Each table row also records how the last detection was found (CI history or `git log -S` of its baseline), whether an ordinary edit can reintroduce the anomaly, and one disposition from the closed list keep, narrow or retire; retire covers a redundant gate, a gone producer, an unrepresentable anomaly, or a ratchet at zero (then hard-zero or delete). Each retirement is its own pull request that proves the red path of what remains. The table is appended to `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md` as the practice "a gate ships with its retire condition and is audited against it". Where possible a new gate, gate class or exemption enforces its retire condition: one that matches nothing fails closed with "delete this entry", as the artifact-language gate's inert-exemption rule does.
- **TARGET-ARCH**: The application core is still trapped in `apps/api` and the boundary guard was partial, so the target design (a core every deployable consumes, boundaries enforced, one composition root per executable) is only partly met. In the target design the apps are deployment containers, and the core is promoted into a package that `apps/workers` and future deployables consume. The partial guard was SMELL-40: the dependency-cruiser rule did not cover `@packages/api-common`. The design's last version is kept in git history at commit `0086df48af32db3980680158e8b76ada41358dbf`. ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA and SMELL-45 wait for this item, whose design is their reference. Where: `docs/architecture/TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_CANON_ES.md` (deleted from the repository on 2026-10-17; cited path absent), `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `apps/workers`. Done: (a) audit the current state against the target map; (b) dependency-cruiser rules that enforce the core's boundaries in CI for every deployable; (c) the core promotion epic closed. Today, read on 2026-11-27: `packages/core/` holds 53 package directories (among them `accounts`, `domain`, `application` and `posts`), and the header of `.dependency-cruiser.cjs:12-21` maps `packages/core/<context>/src` to an application layer and places `packages/api-common` in the infrastructure layer that may import anything. Whether SMELL-40 is closed and how much of the core remains in `apps/api` were not checked; this does not close the row.
- **ARCH-WORKERS-PRISMA** (SMELL-26, SMELL-39): Workers use Prisma directly, with no use cases, Unit of Work or outbox. Where: not stated. Done: not stated. Depends on: TARGET-ARCH; blocks the recycling and moderation workers.
- **ARCH-ROUTES-PRISMA** (routes-prisma-03): Route handlers hold a direct PrismaClient. Where: not stated. Done: not stated.
- **EVENT-DUAL-SYSTEM** (SMELL-10, FN-002): The outbox `EventDispatcher` runs beside a parallel `EventService` over Redis, and the integration-events pipeline is dead in production. Where: not stated. Done: Classify each side as canon or drift.
- **AUTH-RL-1**: The live limiter groups by IP and route with no metrics and no per-principal quota, and the change `auth-rate-limit-integrity` has no row. The change lives in `docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/`, with no `tasks.md`; its C0 still requires `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT`, which ADR-0021 removed, and its `design.md` calls the per-principal quota an intention with no owning change. `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:71` prefers the authenticated principal to the IP. The `api_rate_limit_*` counters (`apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts:271-311`) have no producer and label by `client_ip`. The removal of the 7 `config.rateLimit` blocks and `@fastify/rate-limit` is closed (pull request #679, 2026-10-22). Decision PREG-8 (2026-10-06): the limiter gets no in-application emergency mode; attacks are answered at the edge, with an "edge under attack" runbook in `docs/runbooks/` written once a deployment target exists. The Product Owner signed the order on 2026-08-16. Verified on 2026-10-05. Where: `apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:244-249`, `docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/`, `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:71`, `apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts:271-311`, `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md:366`, `apps/api/src/auth/rbacMiddleware.ts`, `apps/api/src/monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts`. Done: Stage 1: the design refreshed against ADR-0021, the AUTH preset on the three uncapped customer credential routes and on `/admin/auth/login` and `/admin/auth/refresh`, one source of rules, a per-address cap on both password-reset emails. Stage 2: a per-account charge in `requireClientAuth` and `requireAdminAuth` with figures from the Product Owner, and each limiter decision as a metric with bounded labels and a block-ratio alert with a runbook. Stage 1 also consolidates item 9 of section 5 of `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md` into the single rule source. In stage 2 the figures are per role or plan, and metric labels carry the rule and the result, never the IP. The work also covers the intentions of `roleBasedRateLimit` (`apps/api/src/auth/rbacMiddleware.ts`, quotas per role, which closes L-26) and of `apps/api/src/monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts` (telemetry and abuse per tenant), 2026-03 code that never ran and is due for deletion. Depends on: `principal-derived-authority` (order signed 2026-08-16); N-SEC-2, N-SEC-7, AI-QUOTA-1, DEF-70, C-P1b.
- **SEC-KEK-1**: `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` names a model that does not exist and the channels' OAuth tokens live under `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY` with the rest of Class A, so key separation by purpose is undecided. The tokens live in `Channel.credentials`, encrypted under `PLATFORM_ENCRYPTION_KEY` with AAD per channel and rotation by versions (`packages/core/domain/src/security/secretCatalog.ts:71-74`). `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is still required at startup and has never had a reader. The repository already separates keys when the threat model changes (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:140`). DEF-29 is finding F1, the AAD of `Channel.credentials` between the API, the seed and the workers. ADR-0034 (`docs/technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md`, 2026-10-06) sets one key-encryption provider with one data key per record for the five encrypted families (decisions 4 and 5) and retires `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (decision 13). Where: `apps/api/src/config/env.ts:125`, `packages/core/domain/src/security/secretCatalog.ts:76-79`, `docs/technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md`. Done: An ADR decides whether tenants' OAuth tokens carry a KEK of their own (with a re-encryption path) or one KEK covers Class A, and `docs/security/SECRETS.md` is aligned. Depends on: DEF-29 (closed first); adjudicated against ADR-0034. Today, read on 2026-11-27: Outside tests, `OAUTH_ENCRYPTION_KEY` is named only by its boot schema (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:125`), its catalog entry (`packages/core/domain/src/security/secretCatalog.ts:76`) and two migrations under `infra/prisma/migrations/`. `docs/technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md` decides a data key per record (`:146`) under one provider-neutral key-encryption port (`:175`) and retires the variable (`:341`); whether it answers the question of a key of the tenants' own was not read, and this does not close the row.
- **SEC-SSRF-1**: There is no network guard on HTTP egress toward URLs that come from the tenant, and the portal image optimizer accepts any https host. A static search on 2026-11-27 finds no code under `apps` or `packages` matching `isPrivateIp`, `ssrf` or `isLoopback`. The only live control is the media host list: `makeMediaUrlSchema` over `ALLOWED_MEDIA_HOSTS` (`inputValidation.ts:174,287`). The optimizer's remote pattern is `hostname: "**"`. The URL validation "with domain restrictions" in `apps/api/src/validation/secureSchemas.ts:113-136` was never connected and relies on `includes("localhost")`; it is dead code due for deletion, not a guard. Where: `apps/api/src/security/inputValidation.ts:174,287`, `apps/client/next.config.mjs:30-33`. Done: An inventory of egress points toward tenant URLs including the Next optimizer, an outbound client that resolves DNS and rejects loopback, private, link-local and metadata ranges also after redirects, and negative tests per point.
- **AUTH-OBS-1**: There are two security alerts and no metrics for login attempts, MFA verifications, revocations or refresh failures, nor alerts for a brute-force spike or mass revocation. The two alerts are `MfaBackupCodeReuseRejected` and `AdminRefreshTokenReplayRejected`, both over `api_security_threats_total`. The gap is items E9 to E11 and item 15 of section 5 of `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md`. The work picks up the intentions of `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts` (threshold alerts over the audit trail) and of `BusinessKPITracker.trackUserEngagement` (active sessions), 2026-03 code that never ran and is due for deletion. Verified on 2026-10-05. Where: `prometheus/alerts/api.yml:35-61`, `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md:153-155,375`, `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts`. Done: `login_attempts_total{side,result}`, `mfa_verifications_total`, `session_revocations_total` and `token_refresh_failures_total` with bounded labels, and alerts with runbooks (spike, MFA burst, mass revocation, mass change by one actor). The mass change by one actor covers modification and deletion, and the runbooks include one for a compromised account. Today, read on 2026-11-27: `BusinessKPITracker.trackUserEngagement` is defined at `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/businessMetrics.ts:197`, and a search of `apps` and `packages` outside tests finds no other line that names it.

The noEmit packages move to TypeScript project references under an ADR of their own (SMELL-49). - **TEST-TYPECHECK-PERIMETER** (SMELL-68): Outside `apps/api` and `db-prisma`, no compiler opens test files: 69 packages typecheck `src` only, both portals exclude their tests, four packages have no `typecheck` script and nine run `tsc --noEmit` with no `tsconfig.json` and compile nothing. Each of the nine reports green: eight (`packages/adapters/{cache-redis,dead-letter-queue,external-apis,fallback-strategies,queue-bullmq,storage-s3}` and `packages/monitoring/{circuit-breaker,health-checks}`) resolve the root `tsconfig.json`, whose `"files": []` (`:5`) compiles nothing, as `tsc --showConfig` confirmed on 2026-10-29; `packages/core/threading` resolves `packages/core/tsconfig.json`, which compiles the two files of `packages/core/src` and none of its own. Where: `packages/core/domain/tsconfig.json:6`, `apps/admin/tsconfig.json:30`, `apps/client/tsconfig.json:43`, `apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs`, `tsconfig.json:5`, `packages/core/tsconfig.json`. Done: Every workspace with tests has a typecheck program that opens them (hard-zero or a ratchet like the `apps/api` one), the nine packages get a `tsconfig.json`, and a type error planted in one test of each group turns `turbo run typecheck` red. Each of the nine new `tsconfig.json` files opens its package's `src` and `tests`. Depends on: DEF-128 (the portals' tests) is related.

- **TENANT-PARENT-FK** (SMELL-69): Nothing ties a denormalized `accountId` to its parent project's: `Channel` and 23 other models reference `Project` by `projectId` alone. `Project` already carries `@@unique([id, accountId])` (`:689`), and only the `Post` trio has composite foreign keys (the first at `:706`). The eight enrolled `projectId`-only models are ExternalNotificationConfig, ScheduledReport, Campaign, RecurringPost, TrackedLink, GeneratedImage, ProjectMember and Channel; they rely on the create paths and the backfill assertion, and a divergent `Channel` row makes the worker's scoped lookup match nothing while the job reports success. The other sixteen include `Mention`, `MediaAsset` and `Template`; `WebhookEvent`'s `accountId` is optional, and fitness #39 counts it. The total of 24 was measured on 2026-10-29. Where: `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:862`, `:689`. Done: A divergence audit query, composite foreign keys `(projectId, accountId)` to `Project(id, accountId)` for the eight models (NOT VALID, then VALIDATE), the same or a recorded reason for the other sixteen, and an integration test that the engine refuses an inconsistent insert. The divergence audit runs before the keys are added, and the keys follow the `Post` trio's NOT VALID then VALIDATE pattern. Depends on: N-SEC-3 slice 2 does not cover it.
- **FK-CASCADE-INDEX** (SMELL-89): A foreign key whose referencing column leads no index makes every cascade delete scan the child table; the real count of such edges is unmeasured. The scan runs once per destroyed parent, and a single-column index narrowed to `WHERE "deletedAt" IS NULL` cannot serve a cascade. A backlog count of 31 edges assumed that migration had indexed 10, so the real count may be 41. The hard-delete migrations in the tree, `infra/prisma/migrations/20260830220417_*` to `20260901120100_*`, index none of these edges. Where: `infra/prisma/migrations/20260903040000_hard_delete_cascade_fk_indexes` (named as absent from every tree on main). Done: The catalog query run against a migrated database records the list, each edge is indexed or kept on a measured cascade timing, and account-erasure time at 10 000 posts is recorded before and after. Today, read on 2026-11-27: Of the migrations from `20260830220417_*` to `20260901120100_*`, only two create indexes: `infra/prisma/migrations/20260830220417_ondelete_convention_alignment/migration.sql:115-117` on `DeletionRecord`, and `infra/prisma/migrations/20260901120000_deletion_record_retention_and_partial_uniques/migration.sql:93-102` on `DeletionRecord` plus partial unique indexes on `Account` (`email`) and `Project` (`accountId`, `name`). Whether any of these leads a cascading foreign key was not checked. Open decision of the Maintainer: Should the catalog query that lists foreign keys whose referencing column leads no index be committed to the tree and named in this row, given that no living file of the tree is known to hold it?

### Defects found on 2026-10-04 — queued after N-TEST-1

- **DEF-16**: The API scheduler receives a pino logger whose `(message, object)` order drops the error context, so a scheduled task's error is logged without `taskId` or `err`. `setupServices.ts` passes `createLogger("scheduler")`, a pino logger, as the `SchedulerLogger`, whose methods take `(message, data)`. Pino reads that order as a message plus a format argument and drops the object when the message has no format specifier; the affected calls are the `error` calls at `default-scheduler.ts:139-142`, `:168-171` and `:175-178` and the `info` at `:144`. Measured with the repository's pino (10.3.1): the call writes only the message; the rest was read statically. The workers adapt the same port in pino's order with `createWorkerTaskScheduler` (`apps/workers/src/lib/workerScheduler.ts:16-26`), the pattern to follow. Where: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:175`, `packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/port.ts:17-21`, `packages/observability/background-scheduler/src/default-scheduler.ts:139-178`, `apps/workers/src/lib/workerScheduler.ts:16-26`. Done: not stated (same risk class as decision D-R10 of ADR-0033). Depends on: ADR-0033 (D-R10). Open decision of the Maintainer: What condition closes DEF-16, the API scheduler's error logs that lose `taskId` and `err`, since its row states no `Done:`?
- **DEF-34**: The alerts `ApiLatencyP99High`, `ApiErrorRateHigh` and `OutboxLagHigh` cannot fire: they query series names the API and the outbox do not export, and there is no rules test. `ApiLatencyP99High` queries `http_duration_bucket{job="api"}` and `ApiErrorRateHigh` queries `http_requests_total{status=…}`, while the API exports `api_http_request_duration_seconds` and `api_http_requests_total` with the label `status_code` and the scrape job is named `api-server`; `OutboxLagHigh` queries `outbox_pending_events`, which no registry exports. `docs/observability/SLO.md` (including `up{job="api"}`) and three runbooks repeat the wrong names. Read statically, not run: a `count(http_duration_bucket)` query against the live Prometheus confirms it. Where: `prometheus/alerts/api.yml:8`, `:20`, `prometheus/alerts/outbox.yml:10`, `prometheus/prometheus.yml:37`, `apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts:108-118`, `docs/observability/SLO.md:25-27`, `docs/runbooks/alert-api-latency.md`, `docs/runbooks/alert-api-error-rate.md`, `docs/runbooks/alert-outbox-lag.md`. Done: Rules, SLO and runbooks over series that exist, the outbox relay publishes its backlog, and a `promtool test rules` step in CI with its red path proven.
- **DEF-35**: The provisioned Grafana dashboards read series nobody feeds, and the live breaker and worker series have no panel or rule. - **API-REFERENCE-COVERAGE**: `docs/architecture/API.md` documents 31 of the 497 method and path pairs of the generated route table; the other 466 are undocumented there. DEF-169 and BOOLEAN-QUERY-OTHER-PARSES hold the other boolean query flags and neither names these two. Where: `apps/api/src/audit/auditRoutes.ts:30`, `apps/api/src/audit/auditRoutes.ts:44`. - **DEF-87**: `apps/admin/next-env.d.ts` and `apps/client/next-env.d.ts` are tracked although `.gitignore:99` lists them, so every `next dev` leaves a change in the tree. Next 16 (16.3.8) always sets the development `distDir` to `.next/dev`, so `next dev` rewrites the file to import `.next/dev/types`, while `next build` and `next typegen` write `.next/types`. Next's TypeScript guide, shipped in the `next` package, asks to ignore the file and take it out of Git. The typecheck scripts read the tracked file through each portal's `tsconfig.json`, so untracking it without running `next typegen` first breaks them. Measured on 2026-10-10: every `next dev` on a working branch left its portal's `next-env.d.ts` modified. Where: `apps/admin/next-env.d.ts`, `apps/client/next-env.d.ts`, `.gitignore:99`, `apps/admin/package.json:18`, `apps/client/package.json:20`, `:29`, `apps/admin/tsconfig.json:28`, `apps/client/tsconfig.json:35`. A real run of F-01 T5's start command measured the rewrite: `next dev` changed the reference in `apps/admin/next-env.d.ts` from `./.next/types/` to `./.next/dev/types/`. Done: Untrack both files and run `next typegen` before the typecheck scripts, in a unit of its own. Depends on: DEF-85, DEF-86 (same branch).
- **DEF-88**: `apps/admin/public/README.md` is tracked against the documentation policy and is what keeps the directory in Git; nothing detects a misplaced `.md` under `apps/` or `packages/`. The documentation policy keeps all documentation under `docs/`, with no `.md` file in `apps/` or `packages/` other than the root `CLAUDE.md` and `.claude/`; this README is the only tracked `.md` under `apps/`, `packages/` and `infra/`. Its three lines say the directory holds the files Next serves as is from `/` and must exist even when empty, because the `Dockerfile` copies it into the image and a missing directory breaks that stage; deleting the README outright breaks that copy. No fitness check and no gate of `scripts/` covers a misplaced `.md`, and the pre-edit hooks skip `.md` files and never see the files a tool writes, such as the `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` that `next dev` writes. Where: `apps/admin/public/README.md`, `apps/admin/Dockerfile:120`, `.claude/hooks-py/pre_edit_tripwire_blocker.py:273`, `.claude/hooks-py/pre_edit_planmode_guard.py:62`. Done: Move its explanation to `docs/` and keep the directory with a non-`.md` file, or make the `Dockerfile` not depend on it; then a gate with its red path proven.
- **DEF-90**: The copyleft license check, the one useful value of the deleted bundle analyzer, only ran by hand and no gate covers it. The deleted check read `pnpm licenses list --json` and flagged every package whose license expression contained `GPL-2.0`, `GPL-3.0` or `AGPL-3.0`; that substring match also flagged `LGPL-2.0` and `LGPL-3.0` but not `LGPL-2.1`, failed nothing, and returned an empty list when pnpm could not list the licenses. `pnpm quality:security` lists the licenses without judging them. The candidate homes are beside the legal dependency inventory (fitness #45) or inside `pnpm check:legal`. Where: `pnpm quality:bundle` (deleted), `docs/legal/inventories/subprocessors.generated.md`, `package.json:47`, `package.json:70`. Done: A real gate that reads `pnpm licenses list --json` and fails on a copyleft expression, red path proven; where it lives and which expressions count is a decision of its own unit. Depends on: DEF-84 (closed), DEF-75, fitness #45.
- **DEF-91**: `setupTest` of `apps/api/tests/setup.ts` logs "DB/Redis connections verified" without inspecting the `Result` of `queue.health()` or `listLogs`, and does not close the adapters when it fails. `queue.health()` and `listLogs({ limit: 1 })` are awaited together with `Promise.all` and the value is discarded (`_healthResults`), so a database or Redis error returned as `err` passes the setup; only a rejection or the time limit `TEST_CONFIG.dbTimeout` makes it fail. When it fails, the `catch` sets `globalRepo = null` and `globalQueue = null` without closing them, and the next call creates new adapters. Where: `apps/api/tests/setup.ts`. Done: Narrow both results, make the setup fail on an `err` and close what was opened.
- **DEF-92** (SMELL-157, SMELL-86): `makeFlowCleanup` discards the `Result` of each deletion and swallows rejections, and calls a `deletePost` the port does not have, so a failed cleanup leaves no trace. It discards the `Result` of `deletePost`, `deleteProject` and `deleteAccount`, its `catch` ignores rejections («Ignore cleanup errors»), and it empties the lists anyway, so the next suite inherits the rows. `RepoPort` declares `deleteAccount` and `deleteProject` and no post deletion, so `apps/api/tests/setup.ts:362` throws a `TypeError` on every recorded post and the bare `catch` at `:363` absorbs it; posts leave only through their project's `ON DELETE CASCADE`. The tests typecheck ledger records one `TS2339` for `apps/api/tests/setup.ts`. Where: `apps/api/tests/setup.ts:362-363`, `packages/ports/src/RepoPort.ts:152`, `:180`, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:706`. Done: Gather the failures and report them, and call a deletion the port has or delete the post loop.
- **DEF-93**: `initBrowserSentry` sets `replaysSessionSampleRate` and `replaysOnErrorSampleRate` but `@sentry/nextjs` 10.48.0 adds no `replayIntegration`, so the two options have no effect. It sets `replaysSessionSampleRate: 0` and `replaysOnErrorSampleRate: 1.0`. The client of `@sentry/nextjs` 10.48.0 builds its default integrations by adding `browserTracingIntegration` to those of `@sentry/browser`, and none of them records sessions. The decision is the Product Owner's because a session replay is personal data: turning it on changes the note and `dataSent` of `@sentry/nextjs` in `docs/legal/classification/subprocessors.json`. Where: `packages/observability/sentry-next/src/client.ts`, `apps/admin/instrumentation-client.ts`, `apps/client/instrumentation-client.ts`, `docs/legal/classification/subprocessors.json`. Done: The Product Owner decides: add `Sentry.replayIntegration()` with its entry in the legal register, or remove the two options. Depends on: DEF-86 (closed), DEF-102 (closed).

- **DEF-148**: Grafana links Prometheus exemplars to a `jaeger` datasource that no provisioning file defines, so the jump from a metric to its trace does not work. Where: `grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml:14-16`. Done: not stated. Depends on: the [REQUIREMENT] OpenTelemetry in every application. Open decision of the Maintainer: Does the requirement of OpenTelemetry in every application wait for the fix of the Grafana exemplar link to an undefined `jaeger` datasource (DEF-148), or does that fix wait for the requirement?
- **DEF-149**: Jaeger keeps traces in memory, so they are lost at every restart, while the requirement asks not to lose the history. Where: `jaeger/config.yaml:20`. Done: not stated. Depends on: the [REQUIREMENT] OpenTelemetry in every application. Open decision of the Maintainer: Does the requirement of OpenTelemetry in every application wait for Jaeger to keep traces across restarts (DEF-149), or does that fix wait for the requirement?
- **DEF-150**: To measure: with `TRACING_ENABLED=true` the API starts the OpenTelemetry SDK before Sentry and does not pass `skipOpenTelemetrySetup`, so Sentry's API spans may degrade. The degradation is read from Sentry 10's code and not measured: Sentry's trace provider does not stay the global one, so its API spans degrade. Where: `apps/api/src/index.ts:15-20`, `:487`, `:494`. Done: Sentry is mounted on the application's OpenTelemetry SDK instead of registering its own (the requirement demands that the two coexist).
- **(no id)**: [REQUIREMENT] OpenTelemetry in every application: the four applications are instrumented once; each deployment chooses at most one of Sentry or GlitchTip and may add an OTLP backend at the same time; errors and traces go to both; switching does not delete history. Today the API and the workers export traces over OTLP when `TRACING_ENABLED` is set, and neither portal depends on OpenTelemetry. Grafana 11.2.2, Prometheus v2.48.1 and Jaeger are defined in `docker-compose.yml`. Sentry and GlitchTip speak the same protocol. Where: `docker-compose.yml`, `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:15-20`, `apps/workers/src/telemetry/initialization.ts`. Done: The four applications (API, workers, two portals) instrumented once with OpenTelemetry; at most one of Sentry or GlitchTip per deployment plus an independent OTLP backend; errors and traces go to both; no history deleted on a switch (Maintainer, 2026-10-28). Depends on: DEF-148, DEF-149, DEF-150. Today, read on 2026-11-27: `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:165-166` names Prometheus, for the scrape of `GET /metrics` and for the alert rules of `prometheus/alerts/*.yml`, and the checklist names neither Grafana nor Jaeger.
- **DEF-154**: `docs/compliance/RETENTION_CALENDAR.md` has no row for `DeletionRecord.name`, although the calendar is the retention window per data type. The plaintext `name` of a hard-delete tombstone is kept until `retainUntil`, never earlier than `clientUntil` plus one year, and the daily `deletion-record-degrader` tick replaces it with its HMAC digest once that date has passed. The calendar names, per data type, both the window and the scheduled job that enforces it. Where: `docs/compliance/RETENTION_CALENDAR.md`, `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3740-3742`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:1003`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/retention/DeletionRecordDegrader.ts:94`. Done: One row of the retention matrix for `DeletionRecord.name`, with its window and the `deletion-record-degrader` job (proposed fix).
- **DEF-155**: Closed by #882. dependency-cruiser had no rule that kept an adapter or a provider from importing an application. `adapters-no-apps` and its twin `providers-no-apps` now hold `packages/adapters/` and `packages/providers/` away from `apps/`, each at `error`, and the known-violations baseline took no entry. Red path: an import of `apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts` planted in one adapter and then in one provider made `pnpm check:architecture` exit 1 naming the rule each time; each file was restored byte for byte and the gate passed again. Where: `.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, `apps/api/tests/unit/lint/architecturePolicies.test.ts`.
- **DEF-157**: Six code comments say the tenant guard audits or logs a `withSystemContext` reason, which it does not. Neither the guard nor `tenantGuc.ts` reads the reason (`infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:20-21` says it is no runtime audit record), as `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` states since 2026-10-28. The fix rides the change that next touches each file, because fitness #47 then holds that file to the canon's method-level JSDoc. Where: `apps/api/src/auth/customerAuthRoutes.ts:208`, `apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookJobProcessor.ts:20-21`, `apps/api/src/security/tenantContext.ts:45-47,134`, `apps/api/src/billing/billingWebhookRoutes.ts:199,253`, `infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:20-21`, `infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuc.ts`, `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`. Done: Each comment states what the reason is (a declaration the guard never reads), in the change that next touches each file (proposed fix).
- **DEF-158** (SMELL-116): `matchesWhere` of the shared Prisma test double compares an operator object it does not recognise by reference, so `{ equals: x }` matches no row; 37 test files import it. The unrecognised object reaches `recordVal !== val` at `mockPrisma.ts:143`, so `{ equals: x }`, the form `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts` sends for `category`, matches no row: a zero that looks like a correctly firing count. Each recognised operator ends in `continue`, so an object holding two, such as `{ gte, lte }`, is checked on the first alone. Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/helpers/mockPrisma.ts:72,101-143`, `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts:377`. Done: Evaluate every key of an operator object and throw on an unrecognised one, then rerun the importing suites and fix any test that passed on the old answer (proposed fix).
- **DEF-159**: Closed by #908. The duplicate-code gate ignored `**/reports/**`, which also matched ten source files (`apps/api/src/reports/`, `packages/core/reports/`, two client pages), so `pnpm check:duplicates` read nothing in them. The ignore is now `**/reports/mutation/**`, the one report output `.gitignore` names, as `.dependency-cruiser.cjs:402-405` already reads it. The 19 clones the narrowed scope found, 17 new fingerprints, are the route and use-case validation boilerplate the baseline already holds 979 of; each was read and accepted into `.jscpd-baseline.json`. A clone planted under `packages/core/reports/src/` now fails the gate, and passed it before. Where: `.jscpd.json:8`, `.jscpd-baseline.json`.
- **DEF-169**: Closed by #884. Nine boolean query flags could not be turned off because `z.coerce.boolean()` parses `"false"` as `true`. One shared schema, `BooleanQuerySchema` (`packages/api-common/src/schemas.ts`), accepts `"true"` and `"false"` and refuses anything else with the 400 each route gives any invalid query; an absent flag behaves as before. #883 applied it to the five analytics flags and #884 to the other four (`hasMedia`, `includeArchived`, `isActive`, `success`), each with a test that `false` filters. Where: `apps/api/src/analytics/analyticsRoutes.ts`, `apps/api/src/posts/postRoutes.ts`, `apps/api/src/admin/analyticsSchemas.ts`, `apps/api/src/admin/schedulingSchemas.ts`.
- **DEF-173**: The post-edit hook reports a false secret leak on every edit of an ignored file in a linked worktree that has no `node_modules`. `scan_target()` (`.claude/hooks-py/post_edit.py:68-97`) scans from the file's own checkout only when that checkout holds `node_modules/.bin/secretlint`; otherwise it scans from `PROJECT_ROOT` with the path as given, an absolute path. secretlint matches the patterns of `.secretlintignore` (`docs/**` at `:42`, `CLAUDE.md` at `:43`, `**/*.md` at `:53`) against paths relative to its working directory, so none of them matches. Measured on 2026-10-29 in a linked worktree: each edit of `CLAUDE.md` printed `BLOCKED [post-edit]: secretlint detected a possible leak … PostgreSQL connection string` for the documented dev database URL at `CLAUDE.md:87`, and the edit applied anyway, so the message trains agents to ignore the hook. `pnpm exec` is excluded because it installs the workspace. Where: `.claude/hooks-py/post_edit.py:58,68-97`, `.secretlintignore:42,43,53`, `CLAUDE.md:87`. Done: When the file's checkout holds `.secretlintrc.json` and `.secretlintignore` but not the binary, the hook runs `PROJECT_ROOT`'s `secretlint` by absolute path with that checkout as working directory and the path relative to it (never `pnpm exec`), and a test under `.claude/hooks-py/tests/` pins an ignored file edited in a second checkout.
- **DEF-175**: A local coverage run can lower a committed coverage floor: the `autoUpdate` formatter writes the measurement's decimal minus one, so a measurement under 0.1 pp above a floor lowers it. vitest's `autoUpdate` rewrites `apps/api/vitest.config.ts` whenever a run measures above a floor by any amount, and the formatter `(Math.floor(n * 10) - 1) / 10` writes a measurement less than 0.1 pp above a floor as that floor minus 0.1: floor 60 measured at 60.05 writes 59.9, floor 50.3 measured at 50.39 writes 50.2. Fitness #37 refuses the lowered literal in a pull request, but CI's step "Surface a risen coverage floor" reports such a fall as a rise. The config's own comment states the case. Where: `apps/api/vitest.config.ts:156`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:291-326`. Done: The formatter never writes below the committed floor (clamps to the literal, or formats only a measurement at least 0.1 pp above it), pinned by a test on its edge values, and the CI step's message tells a fall from a rise.
- **DEF-188**: `mutation-diff.mjs` names a run-level listing disagreement with the table's reason string, so the head arm is mislabelled and no test pins the path. When the recursive `ls-tree` of a side lists a scope file that `presentAt`'s per-file `ls-tree` says the commit does not hold, `sideOf` sets `unreadable`, and the run is recorded as `base-unreadable` on the base arm and `runner-failed` on the head arm; a disagreement on both sides shows only the base. This class stays apart from DEF-187's so that the two do not merge under one reason name. Where: `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs:580-609,875`. Done: Its own reason name for the listing disagreement, or the path deleted as unreachable with the evidence; a suite case; the headers updated. Depends on: related DEF-187 (closed).

### Routes → use cases and the composition root (ADR-0033) — queued after N-TEST-1

Where: `apps/api/src/outbox/outboxAdminRoutes.ts:73`, `SchedulingPostHandlers.ts:249,349`, `SchedulingSlotHandlers.ts:418`, `tenantTransaction.ts:55`, `tenantTransactionNesting.test.ts`, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`. ### Dead code — decisions of 2026-10-05

- **DC-1**: `performanceMonitor.ts` and its two root hooks are dead, and the metrics plugin must be wrapped with `fastify-plugin` so every HTTP series stays lit. The two root hooks are the `preHandler` that stamps `request.startTime` and the `onResponse` that calls `performanceMonitor.recordRequest`. The deletion and the wrap land in the same change: deleting the monitor without the repair leaves every HTTP series dark. `fastify-plugin` is already a dependency of `apps/api/package.json`. The audit hook is not switched on here (DEF-30). After the wrap, `recordEndpointRequest` also feeds `api_endpoint_*`, and the alert rules keep reading wrong names until DEF-34. Where: `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:455-460,477-483,563-579`, `apps/api/tests/unit/performanceMonitor.init.test.ts`, `apps/api/tests/unit/performanceMonitor.stats.test.ts`, `apps/api/tests/unit/performanceMonitor.test-helpers.ts`, `apps/api/src/middleware/metricsMiddleware.ts`. Done: Delete the monitor, its hooks and its 752 test lines, wrap the metrics plugin with `fastify-plugin`, with a test on the composed app that proves its hook runs for every route. Depends on: in series with DC-6 (DEF-30, DEF-34 mentioned).
- **DC-14**: Admin's class list `ui-safelist.ts` and the override block of `globals.css` rested on a wrong diagnosis (DEF-49) and are redundant. `ui-safelist.ts` has no importers: the directive `@source "../lib/ui-safelist.ts";` consumes it. With the `@source` path of `packages/ui` corrected, Tailwind's oxide scanner extracts the list's 124 classes from `packages/ui/src` (measured on 2026-10-06). The override block cancels the directional slide of every element with `role="dialog"` or `role="alertdialog"`, so deleting it changes those dialogs' animation; the content of Radix's Popover also carries `role="dialog"`, and Admin rendered no Popover on 2026-10-06. The comments the DEF-49 correction left in Admin's stylesheet name this item as their retirement condition. Where: `apps/admin/lib/ui-safelist.ts`, `apps/admin/app/globals.css:9-16,176-196`, `docs/development/ADMIN_TO_CLIENT_MIGRATION_GUIDE.md`, `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md`. Done: Delete the list, its `@source` directive and the override block after a visual review of Dialog, AlertDialog and Toast; the unit decides whether the animation rule goes or stays with a comment; waits for the Maintainer's approval. The same change updates what `docs/development/ADMIN_TO_CLIENT_MIGRATION_GUIDE.md` and the rows of `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md` that name the list say about it, and the visual review also covers any Popover Admin renders by then. Depends on: DEF-49. Found by: the DEF-49 correction (2026-10-06).

### Credential model — decisions of 2026-10-06

- **CRED-2**: Closed by #906. The loggers redacted differently: the API logger held a private `REDACT_PATHS` that lacked `appPassword`, `clientSecret`, `botToken`, `mfaSecret` and the nested forms, and the packages' base logger redacted nothing. `@observability/logger` now exports `REDACT_PATHS`, the previous list plus the 12 fields and 5 nested forms of ADR-0034 decision 8, and `REDACT_CENSOR`; its base logger and `apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts` both redact with them, and `redaction.test.ts` and `loggerRedaction.test.ts` write a credentials object through each factory and read the masks, shown red on the previous code. LOGGING_CANON's `packages/**` row says the packages redact. Where: `packages/observability/logger/src/index.ts`, `apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts`, `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md`.
- **REDACT-NESTED-AND-VARIANT-NAMES**: `REDACT_PATHS` masks most of its names at the top level only. A pino path masks exactly the depth it names, so `config.clientSecret`, `channel.appPassword`, `user.mfaSecret` or `payload.botToken` log raw: past the top level only `token`, `secret`, `password`, `apiKey`, `accessToken`, `refreshToken` and `credentials` are masked at depth one, `token` and `secret` at depth two, and nothing at depth three. The OAuth snake_case fields (`access_token`, `refresh_token`, `client_secret`), `x-api-key`, and the capitalised header spellings an outbound client sends (`headers.Authorization`, an axios `err.config.headers.Authorization`, `Set-Cookie`) are not in the list at all, although LOGGING_CANON and SECURITY_CANON say case variations are listed explicitly. Every gap existed before CRED-2, which kept the list ADR-0034 decision 8 names. Where: `packages/observability/logger/src/index.ts:36-84`. Done: not stated: a decision between more explicit paths per depth and a censor function over key names (pino's `redact.censor` or a serializer), with a test that writes the nested, snake_case and capitalised forms through both loggers and reads the masks. Found by: CRED-2's independent verification (2026-11-16), measured with pino 10.3.1.
- **CRED-7**: The Vault key-provider adapter (`@adapters/key-provider-vault` on `fetch`), a Vault service in `docker-compose.yml` and the CI integration job, and its real contract suite. ADR-0034 (decision 5) sets it. The adapter uses no SDK and covers `datakey/plaintext`, `decrypt`, `rewrap`, key rotation and `min_decryption_version`. The compose service sits beside MinIO, its image pinned as ADR-0018 requires, in dev mode, with the transit engine enabled by a startup step; the CI integration job gets the service under its `services:` entry. If Vault's BSL license ever matters, OpenBao (MPL-2.0, transit-compatible) is the fallback image, which the unit verifies before relying on it. Where: `docker-compose.yml`, `apps/api/scripts/run-tests.sh`. Done: About 300 lines of code: the adapter, the compose and CI services, the contract suite as a batch with `TIER=pr-integration` that fails without the server, and a battery step. Depends on: CRED-5. Open decision of the Maintainer: Where is a new test batch of the integration tier, such as the Vault contract suite, recorded?
- **CRED-9a**: `@adapters/key-provider-aws-kms`, written from the documentation with the SDK client mocked, labelled UNCERTIFIED against the live service. ADR-0034 (decision 5) sets it. The adapter uses `GenerateDataKey` and `Decrypt`, with credentials from the default chain, and the shared contract suite mocks the SDK client to the documented shapes. Where: `packages/adapters/key-provider-aws-kms` (new). Done: About 200 lines of code; the label lives in its `@file` header and in this row and is retired with the first green live run (CRED-10). Depends on: CRED-7.
- **CRED-9b**: `@adapters/key-provider-gcp-kms` with the same label and retirement condition. ADR-0034 (decision 5) sets it. The adapter generates the data key locally, calls `encrypt` and `decrypt` on a key version, and authenticates with Application Default Credentials. Where: `packages/adapters/key-provider-gcp-kms` (new). Done: About 200 lines of code; the label retired with the first green live run (CRED-10). Depends on: CRED-7.
- **CRED-9c**: `@adapters/key-provider-azure-keyvault` (RSA-OAEP-256 wrap) with the same label and retirement condition. ADR-0034 (decision 5) sets it. The adapter generates the data key locally and calls `wrapKey` and `unwrapKey` with RSA-OAEP-256, because AES wrapping exists only in Managed HSM; it authenticates with `DefaultAzureCredential`. Where: `packages/adapters/key-provider-azure-keyvault` (new). Done: About 200 lines of code; the label retired with the first green live run (CRED-10). Depends on: CRED-7.
- **CRED-10**: The live batch of the cloud adapters under its own `TIER=live-kms`, which fails when requested without credentials and is never skipped silently. ADR-0034 (decision 5) sets it. Where: `apps/api/scripts/run-tests.sh`. Done: About 80 lines of code: the tier named in `run-tests.sh`, declared as not run until credentials exist (fitness #30 and #32). Depends on: CRED-9a, CRED-9b, CRED-9c. Open decision of the Maintainer: Where is a test tier that runs only with cloud credentials, such as `TIER=live-kms`, declared as not run?
- **CRED-13**: Three credential gates (cipher only in the canonical module; secret-shaped keys in the environment schemas; the inventory of secret columns), each with its block in `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, its line in that document's inventory, its step in `fitness.yml` and its red proven. ADR-0034 (decision 12) sets the three gates; their fitness numbers are assigned at landing. (a) `createCipheriv` and `createDecipheriv` appear only in the canonical module, with a floor on its own calls; `apps/api/src/video/uploadPipeline.ts:638` is a named exception until DC-8. (b) A secret-shaped key enters the two environment schemas only as a boot key or through an exception list that only shrinks, its entries labelled with BILL-5, STOR-3 and CRED-8a to CRED-8c. (c) The gate runs over a new `SECRET_COLUMNS` registry (about 60 lines of code) that forces every column with a secret's name to declare its class. `fitness.yml` is a sensitive path. Where: `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` §Automated Compliance Checks, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`, `apps/api/src/video/uploadPipeline.ts`. Done: Each gate lands with its block, its list line, workflow step, #44 count sentence and proven red; (a) after PR 2 of DEF-29, (b) right after, (c) after CRED-5 (80 to 120 lines of evidence each). Depends on: DEF-29, CRED-5. Today, read on 2026-11-27: `createCipheriv` or `createDecipheriv` appear in `packages/shared/src/channelCredentialsCrypto.ts`, `apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts`, `infra/prisma/seed.ts`, `apps/api/src/video/uploadPipeline.ts` and `apps/api/tests/unit/security/channelCredentialsCrypto.shared.test.ts`. `docs/technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md:70-71` places the one cipher in `packages/shared/src/channelCredentialsCrypto.ts` and has a second pull request make `EncryptionService` and the seed delegate to it. Lines `:169-170` of that ADR make the kernel module the only file that may call the two functions. Open decision of the Maintainer: Where does a new or modified CI gate record its proof of red?

### Technical support documentation and the freshness of the product documents — after the legal facts register (2026-10-06)

- **SUP-1**: The support-documentation register: template, generated index and `NON_FEATURES.md` are on `main`, the first two pages are not written. The Product Owner retired the table of dated exceptions `EXCEPTIONS.md` on 2026-10-07 (ADR-0043, decision 8): `NON_FEATURES.md` absorbs it, with every candidate of the tree `documented`, `non-feature` with its reason, or `pending`, and so does the `## Support impact` grammar, which declares `none for <feature> — <reason>` in each PR. CI checks the index since 2026-10-07: `pnpm check:support` runs as fitness #46 in the `dependency-consistency` job of `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`. Where: `docs/support/_TEMPLATE.md`, `docs/support/README.md`, `docs/support/NON_FEATURES.md`, `scripts/support/`, `docs/support/classification/non-features.json`, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`. Done: Template, index and `NON_FEATURES.md` on `main`; the first two pages (SUP-4, slice 1) written with it; the regenerated index matches the committed one. Depends on: SUP-4 (the missing pages).
- **SUP-3**: The support-documentation gate `support-docs-gate.mjs` (coverage, freshness, links, index) does not exist. The suite lives in `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/`. (a) Coverage: every feature of the derived inventory has a page with all its sections, each member is claimed once, and a name that no longer exists fails. (b) Freshness: a page fails at 5 or more commits on its `paths` after its stamp or at 90 days, and warns from the first commit or at 60 days; in a PR, a change under a declared `paths` needs the page changed with its stamp, or the declaration `none for … — reason`. (c) Links: every cited code path exists, and a cited `#symbol` is there. (d) Index: the committed table matches the derivation. The thresholds live in two constants of the script, reviewed after three months against the trigger counts. Where: `scripts/testing/support-docs-gate.mjs` (absent), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/`. Done: Three PRs (a+d, b, c) on `main`, reds proven in the body of each PR, fitness green. The gate has a step in `.github/workflows/fitness.yml` and a block in `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, the #44 inventory stays contiguous, and each part's red is proven with a byte-for-byte restoration. Depends on: SUP-1.
- **SUP-4**: The initial load of 30 support pages in 14 slices, by support value, correcting the operator claims that contradict the code. The 30 pages (about 4,700 lines) include the three operator pages the Product Owner kept in the register: queues, outbox and health. Slices hold at most 400 lines each: S1 `publishing`, `provider-adapters`; S2 `channels-and-oauth`, `media-assets`; S3 billing, rate limiting; S4 customer and Admin authentication; S5 scheduling, editor; S6 inbox, mentions and listening, after the decision on `trendRoutes` (DEF-12); S7 analytics, links and UTM; S8 accounts and projects, team and permissions; S9 notifications, webhooks and integrations; S10 AI, multilingual; S11 templates and brand, campaigns, CRM; S12 approvals and tasks, audit, compliance; S13 queues, outbox; S14 health and observability, platform settings and credentials. The queues page may move to S1, since publishing tickets end in a queue. Where: `docs/support/`, `docs/support/NON_FEATURES.md`. Done: Per slice: the gate green with its candidates `documented` in `NON_FEATURES.md`, `pnpm format:check` and lychee green. Depends on: SUP-1, SUP-3 (slice S2 after DEF-37, PR 2 of DEF-29 and CRED-14), DEF-12.
- **SUP-5**: The claims register `docs/product/CLAIMS.md` for the seven product documents does not exist. The register is in English, although it indexes company documents in Spanish and in English. Each material claim carries an invisible anchor `<!-- claim:ID -->` and a row with a type (`current`, `planned`, `aspirational` or `figure`) and evidence (`ADR:<n>`, `code:<path>`, `test:<path>` or a plan row); each document carries a header `> Verified against main <sha> on <date>`. The docs role corrects the seven documents without the Product Owner's approval: each correction is factual (it aligns the claim with its plan row, the code or the ADR, or marks it aspirational), never reworks the messaging, and is listed in the PR body. Where: `docs/product/CLAIMS.md` (absent). Done: Seven headers, anchors and rows on `main`; zero claims without a row. Today, read on 2026-11-27: Besides its planning documents, `docs/product/` holds four documents: `INVESTOR_EN.md`, `INVESTOR_ES.md`, `MARKETING_EN.md` and `MARKETING_ES.md`; the row counts seven. Open decision of the Product Owner: Which documents does the claims register `docs/product/CLAIMS.md` cover: the four investor and marketing documents (`INVESTOR_EN.md`, `INVESTOR_ES.md`, `MARKETING_EN.md`, `MARKETING_ES.md`) or others as well?
- **SUP-6**: The gate `product-claims-gate.mjs` (anchor and row equality, resolvable evidence, header age) does not exist. Its rules: per document, the set of anchors equals the set of rows; every piece of evidence resolves; a `current` claim cites no open row, and a `planned` claim cites no closed row and no row that no longer exists; a document fails when its header is more than 60 days old or predates the last change of the plan rows it cites; in a PR, the gate warns which documents to re-verify. Where: `scripts/testing/product-claims-gate.mjs` (absent). Done: A PR on `main` with a fitness step, a block, a gates-table row and a red proven per rule; fitness green on the SUP-5 load. - **SUP-7**: The documentation cadence: rules written, `docs/README.md` without its hand-kept version table and with a verified header, a final editorial pass. Where: `docs/README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` §Documentation Policy. Done: Rules written; `docs/README.md` links its sources and carries a verified header; the final editorial pass done with the Product Owner. Depends on: SUP-6. Each omni-post gate is classified as holding by construction, as a generalized gate, or as omni-post only. Depends on: the start of the next application.

### Normalization roadmap

- **HOMELAB-DEBIAN-13**: The development homelab moves from Debian 12 (security support ended 2026-07-11) to Debian 13, between slices, with Postgres 16 to match CI. The Product Owner decided it on 2026-11-06. Debian 12's LTS runs to 2028-06-30 and Debian 13's support to 2028-08-09; no production image changes, since each pins its base by digest. Measured on 2026-11-06 on LXC 102: Postgres 15.19, Redis 7.0.15, Docker 20.10.24, Podman 4.3.1, Python 3.11.2, against CI's `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` and `redis:7-alpine`; Debian 13's defaults (Postgres 17, Redis 8.0, Docker 26.1, Podman 5.4, Python 3.13) widen that gap if taken as they come. The move has two steps, each with its own rollback: LXC 102 first, after a `pct snapshot`, with Postgres 16 from the PostgreSQL project's repository and an explicit choice for Redis; then LXC 101 apart, after reading what it runs, because its data cannot be recreated. Where: LXC 102 and LXC 101 (outside the repository). Done: Every hook of `.claude/hooks-py` runs under Python 3.13, the battery is GREEN on both lanes with Postgres 16, Container Security and `podman` work, the snapshot is removed.
- **TOKEN-1**: Closed by #929. The base load every agent call carries falls from about 692,000 bytes to at most 250,000 (move the audit ledgers of `SECURITY_CANON.md` out). Where: `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `MEMORY.md`. Done: The loaded files total at most 250,000 bytes; fitness #24 passes on the four children and #44 on the new file; every moved link resolves; no rule's wording changes. Progress: #917 (task T1 of the feature that closes this row) moved the dependency audit record, the whole of the canon's section "Audited audit-ignores", byte for byte, to `docs/security/DEPENDENCY_AUDIT_RECORD.md`; the canon keeps its rules and a short section "Dependency audit record"; a short path-scoped rule, `.claude/rules/dependency-audit-record.md`, points at the record when a governed file is touched; the hold-table gate reads the record; the decision is [ADR-0045](../../technical/ADR-0045-instruction-context-budget.md). Task T2a added the manifest of required instruction files, `.claude/instruction-manifest.json`, and the gate that holds the auto-loaded set to it and to a byte ceiling of 288,897 bytes, the tracked files measured at `253b2413` (`scripts/testing/instruction-context-gate.mjs`, `pnpm check:instruction-context`, the static battery step `instruction-context`; ADR-0045's amendment of 2026-11-17); the target of 250,000 bytes was owed by task T3. Task T2b added the enforcement in the session: `.claude/hooks-py/instructions_loaded.py` records the instruction files the harness reports as loaded, `user_prompt_instruction_check.py` blocks the prompt of a session that lacks a file the manifest requires, failing closed, and `pre_tool_record_guard.py` refuses an Edit, Write or MultiEdit of a file the record's rule governs, and a Bash write of one it can derive (a destination pre-bash reads, a file named beside an in-place writer, the manifest and lockfile of a pnpm command that writes them), until the session or the agent has opened the record with the Read or the Grep tool (a Bash command never counts: its text cannot say what ran); every builtin and alias of pnpm 12.6.0 is in one class, writing, not writing or running code, and a test that reads the installed pnpm's list holds the classification complete. A write the guard cannot derive passes unjudged: a script or a pnpm command that runs code, an interpreter one-liner (`python3 -c`, `node -e`, `perl -pi`), another package manager, a path built from a variable, `git apply`, and a checkout or a rebase that names no governed file. The check and the judging guard are wired with `"onFailure": "block"`, and the check refuses a harness older than 2.1.295, which ignores the key; the Maintainer's switch `MAINTAINER_DISABLE_INSTRUCTION_CHECK=yes` turns both off and says so at every prompt; the gate's rule W1 holds the wiring and refuses the switch and `AI_AGENT` in the tracked settings' `env` block, and the gate runs in the fitness workflow (ADR-0045's amendment "the enforcement"). Task T3 moved the blocks of the fitness checks, byte for byte, from `CLAUDE.md` §Automated Compliance Checks to `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, the manifest's second record, with its path-scoped rule `.claude/rules/fitness-functions.md` and the record guard's refusal reworded for any record; the section keeps its rules and one line per check, each named as its step in `fitness.yml`, and fitness #44 holds the workflow, the blocks and the list to one set; the instruction-context gate became fitness #55; `CLAUDE.md` went from 201,668 to 48,394 bytes and the tracked auto-loaded set from 288,897 to 135,623, so `budget.targetOwedBy` left the manifest and the target of 250,000 bytes holds as a hard cap (ADR-0045's amendment of 2026-11-18). Task T0 had reduced the project's local `.claude/CLAUDE.md` from 37,508 to 16,355 bytes on 2026-11-17, in the main checkout only: git ignores the file, so no pull request holds that change. Task T4 moved the five feedback files `CLAUDE.local.md` imports from the user's directory into the project, under `.claude/feedback/`, which git ignores: an import that leaves the project needs an approval this project does not have, so none of the five reached a session; the manifest's `local` list names them, and the session check requires them of a session in the main checkout (ADR-0045's amendment "the feedback files"). Task T5 closed the feature. The Product Owner decided on 2026-11-18 that the 250,000 bytes cover the project's files a session loads (the tracked set, the local files and the feedback files): they total 223,800 bytes. With the user's own instruction file (91,629) and the memory index (15,008), which are outside the ceiling, a session loads 330,437. A one-word request from the main checkout carries 166,618 input tokens on `opus`, which answers, where it carried about 290,000 on 2026-11-17 and was refused (ADR-0045's amendment "the closing measurement"). Fitness #24 passes on the four children and #44 on the reference. The same task implemented the five review notes deferred to the feature's close, one of them a false green of #44 (a numbered name nested inside a step was credited as a check no step runs). The local and the feedback bytes are held by no check: INSTRUCTION-BUDGET-LOCAL-FILES-HELD-BY-NO-CHECK.
- **CONTEXT-DIET-TRACKED-HALF**: Closed by #995 and #996. The tracked instruction text every session loads keeps only what a session always needs, and the detail is read as a file when the work reaches it: the tracked half of the context diet, whose rows the Product Owner decided on 2026-11-25. Where: `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/development/CANON_ENFORCEMENT.md`, `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, `docs/GOVERNANCE.md`, the four canon documents `CLAUDE.md` loads (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md`, `docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md`, `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md`, `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md`) and their companions. Done: `CLAUDE.md` reads as the decided rows P, every line that leaves it is in a tracked document with no change of wording or is replaced by the decided wording; each canon document keeps only its rules under a three-line header and its rationale, examples and "How to extend" live in a companion beside it, read as a file; the rules of one area live in that area's document, with one pointer line in the canon; and fitness #24 and #55 and ADR-0045 are changed with them. Progress: the pull request of rows P shortens `CLAUDE.md` from 45,484 to 30,585 bytes with its documentation rows (task T5a), and to 21,185 with the fitness inventory (task T5b): the detail of the canon exceptions and of the tripwires moves to `docs/development/CANON_ENFORCEMENT.md`, the mechanics of push grants to `docs/GOVERNANCE.md` §The hooks' grant issuer, the `/docs/` table gives way to `docs/README.md`, and the pull request template gains `## Foundation practices`; the inventory of the fitness checks, the detail of their baselines and "Extending the suite" move to `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, where fitness #44 now reads the list, and `CLAUDE.md` keeps the count sentence. The canon documents (task T3, rows H, D, S and O of its review sheet, 21,808 tokens before the walk): `ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md`, `CODING_STANDARDS.md`, `SECURITY_CANON.md` and `LOGGING_CANON.md` keep their rules; the companions `ARCHITECTURE_CANON_REFERENCE.md`, `CODING_STANDARDS_REFERENCE.md`, `SECURITY_CANON_REFERENCE.md` and `LOGGING_CANON_REFERENCE.md` hold what is read as a file; the saga rules live in `docs/architecture/SAGA_CANON.md`, the React rules in `docs/frontend/REACT_STANDARDS.md`, the client rules in `docs/standards/frontend-standards.md`, the Prisma client rule in `docs/architecture/schema-conventions.md`, and the version and patch rules in `docs/security/dependency-audit-policy.md`. The canon is not loaded by path: the Product Owner decided on 2026-11-26 that the main session keeps it by the `@`-import and the delegated writer and verifier receive it by a preloaded skill, so row P10's "load by path" is replaced by that decision. With them (task T3b): fitness #24 reads `**Owner:**` in each canon document and `## How to extend` in its companion, fitness #55 holds the manifest's budget at the measured 68,883 bytes, and the amendment of 2026-11-26 of ADR-0045 records the decision. Outside the tree: the loader skill for the delegated roles is local configuration (task T3d). The findings the four walks made outside their scope are rows of this plan, each found by the context diet, T3.
- **API-TYPES-1**: 494 of 497 operations in the generated API types type every response as `unknown`; only the three health routes are typed. The generator and its CI drift gate exist; an operation is typed only when its route declares a response schema. The figures were verified on 2026-10-28. This item blocks the typed portals of API-TYPES-2. Where: `packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts`, `scripts/generate-api-types.ts`, `openapi-ts.config.ts`, `docs/architecture/openapi-migration.md`. Done: Every route declares Zod schemas for params, query, body and response, so each operation is typed, area by area with the drift gate green.
- **API-TYPES-2**: No file of `apps/admin` or `apps/client` imports `packages/shared/src/api-generated/`, so a renamed response field breaks a portal at run time. The test is the only importer outside the package, and the break shows at run time instead of at typecheck. Verified on 2026-10-28. It needs from API-TYPES-1 at least three endpoints with typed responses. Where: `packages/shared/src/api-generated/`, `apps/api/tests/unit/security/httpRateLimitRuleCoverage.test.ts` (its only importer). Done: Typed clients in both portals consume at least three endpoints through the generated types. Depends on: API-TYPES-1.
- **QUEUE-SPLIT-1**: Split `@adapters/queue-bullmq` into job types, a client and a worker base; deferred until a second worker process exists. The package mixes the three responsibilities, which a common monorepo layout keeps in three packages; this was verified on 2026-10-28. With one worker process, three packages would triple the boilerplate for no gain. Where: `packages/adapters/queue-bullmq/src` (seven files, 867 lines), `apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts`. Done: Three packages, `apps/api` consuming the client, `apps/workers` the worker base, job types a leaf dependency (only when the trigger fires).
- **OBS-1**: The API registers no default process metrics and its gauges (memory, connections, health, queue depth) have no producer, so the saturation dashboards and the availability alert have no series. Only the workers register the default metrics (`apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:149`), and the gauges' setters have no callers. The dashboards of `grafana/dashboards/system-resources.json` and the availability alert depend on these series. This was verified on 2026-10-05. The item takes over the intent of `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts` (an alert for memory above 85 % and aggregated health, which ran with no reader) and of `apps/api/src/health/healthMetrics.ts` (never registered), 2026-03 code that cards C1 and C6 of `docs/technical/ADR-0041-dead-code-cards-and-retired-intentions.md` retire. Where: `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:149`, `apps/api/src/metrics/apiMetrics.ts:337-355`, `grafana/dashboards/system-resources.json`. Done: The process's default metrics in the API, checker health as a series, heap, RSS, event-loop lag and availability alerts each with a runbook. The alerts use the real job label (see DEF-34). Depends on: DEF-34.
- **OBS-3**: No HTTP series and no span carries the account (`rg 'setAttribute\("(tenant|account)'` = 0 in the API and workers); only `tenantHealth.ts` emits `tenant_id` gauges. It complements STOR-13 and `tenant-health-idor-01`. The Product Owner wants behaviour per customer, the intent of `PerformanceMonitor`, and `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/correlationTracking.ts` put `tenantId` in spans; both are 2026-03 code that cards C1 and C4 of `docs/technical/ADR-0041-dead-code-cards-and-retired-intentions.md` retire, and this item takes over their intent. The finding has not been reviewed yet. Where: `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/tenantHealth.ts:22-40`. Done: An `account_id` label only on chosen bounded-cardinality series, an `account.id` attribute on API and worker spans, a per-account query in OBS-2.
- **OBS-4**: The circuit breaker exports requests, failures and state changes and the workers measure duration per provider, but no rule or dashboard queries them. The breaker also exports timeouts and duration, and labels state changes with `from_state` and `to_state`. The breaker's state is an alert signal, not a readiness one. That no rule or dashboard queries these series was verified on 2026-10-05. The item takes over the intent of `@monitoring/circuit-breaker` (four rules: open, error rate, slowness, recovered) and of `CircuitBreakerHealthChecker`, 2026-03 code that never received a sample and that card C6 of `docs/technical/ADR-0041-dead-code-cards-and-retired-intentions.md` retires. Where: `packages/adapters/external-apis/src/circuitBreaker.ts:244-341`, `apps/workers/src/metrics/workerMetrics.ts:203`. Done: Alerts for an open breaker, error rate and latency per provider and provider quota, a Grafana dashboard and a runbook. The quota alert applies where the provider's API reports its consumption. Today, read on 2026-11-27: `packages/monitoring/circuit-breaker` and `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/checkers/circuitBreaker.ts` are still in the tree. `docs/technical/ADR-0041-dead-code-cards-and-retired-intentions.md:75` lists `@monitoring/circuit-breaker` and `CircuitBreakerHealthChecker` under card C6.
- **OBS-5**: None of the sixteen alerts is one of the six the SLOs leave TBD (availability, saga completion rate, saga average duration, outbox publish latency, post-creation p99, publish success rate). Each of the six waits on the source of its series; OBS-1 feeds availability. That none of the sixteen alerts is one of the six was verified on 2026-10-28. Where: `prometheus/alerts/api.yml`, `prometheus/alerts/outbox.yml`, `prometheus/alerts/saga.yml`, `docs/observability/SLO.md`. Done: The six rules over fed series each with a runbook under `docs/runbooks/`, and the TBD cells of `SLO.md` filled. Depends on: OBS-1, DEF-34, DEF-35.
- **OBS-6**: Every alert rule is evaluated and none is delivered: the `alerting:` block is commented out. `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md` lists the routing among its open gaps, and the nightly chaos run (`.github/workflows/chaos.yml`) only opens a GitHub issue on a failure, so nothing pages anyone. This was verified on 2026-10-28. It is design work, not a one-line wire: an Alertmanager for development against a managed one in production, the Slack, e-mail or PagerDuty webhook secrets, and a Sentry API token with its alert rules. It takes over the channels that `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts` and `apps/api/src/monitoring/rateLimitingDashboard.ts` only simulated, and the Sentry alerts planned for the chaos and provider-sandbox runs. Where: `prometheus/prometheus.yml:14-18`, `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md`, `.github/workflows/chaos.yml`. Done: Alertmanager wired with routing to Slack, e-mail or PagerDuty, and Sentry alert rules created through its API.
- **GUARANTEES-NEED-BACKING**: Every documented guarantee needs executable backing: an ADR that sets the rule, an audit pass over the normative sentences of the canon, and a detector. The Maintainer decided the rule on 2026-08-16. Each normative sentence in scope either names its test or fitness check or is rewritten as an intention. `docs/technical/` holds two `ADR-0020` (`ADR-0020-audit-actor-exclusive-arc.md` and `ADR-0020-tenant-context-at-boundaries.md`), both accepted and referenced, so the new ADR's number waits on how N-DOC-2 resolves that collision. Two of the false statements found lie outside the canon and the ports, which is why the scope reaches `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`. Where: `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md`, `docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md`, `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md`, `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md`, `packages/ports/**` JSDoc, `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`. Done: ADR accepted, the six identified false statements corrected, and a fitness or script that detects unbacked statements with a measured baseline. When a clean hard zero is not possible, the approximation and its false-positive profile are documented instead. Depends on: N-DOC-2 (ADR number). Open decision of the Maintainer: Which false statements must the canon audit still correct and name in the item, given that only a planning document due for archive lists them and two of them sit in that document itself?
- **GUARD-NESTED-INCLUDES** (SMELL-59): The Prisma tenant guard mutates only the top-level `args.where`, so an `include` or `select` of an enrolled model travels uninspected; the guard doctrine never mentions nested includes. `tenantGuardCheck` receives one `model` and mutates only that operation's `args.where`, because Prisma's extensions intercept top-level operations; an `include` or `select` travels uninspected even when its model is enrolled. `project` is enrolled and leaked anyway through the `include` of a route N-AUTH-1 deleted on 2026-10-18; the gap is in the doctrine and affects every `include` of an enrolled model. `apps/api/tests/unit/security/tenantGuard.test.ts` has no nested case. The Product Owner made it a high-priority change of its own. Its input is N-SEC-3.b's total registry of which tenant predicate applies to each target relation. Where: `infra/prisma/src/extensions/tenantGuard.ts:186-219`, `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`, `apps/api/tests/unit/security/tenantGuard.test.ts`. Done: A sweep of every `include` and `select`, a test case in `tenantGuard.test.ts`, and the correction of the canon. Depends on: N-SEC-3.b. Found by: a diff-gate suggestion, promoted to a structural-hardening candidate on 2026-07-15.
- **CI-CLIENT-DEPCRUISE** (SMELL-43): The CI dependency-cruiser scan does not cover `apps/client` (no `baseUrl`, so `@/*` imports are skipped), nor `apps/admin`. Without `baseUrl`, madge and tsconfig-paths skip about 25 % of the client's modules when resolving `@/*`, so the scan is blind. `pnpm check:architecture` scans only `apps/api/src apps/workers/src packages`; the client had no cycles on 2026-06-29, but nothing gates it. When the portals join, the `no-orphans` rule needs a `pathNot` for the Next.js App Router special files (`page`, `layout`, `loading`, `error`, `not-found`, `route`), which no module imports. Where: `apps/client/tsconfig.json`, `package.json:75`, `.dependency-cruiser.cjs:123-139`, `apps/admin/tsconfig.json`. Done: `apps/client` in the CI scope with the `@/*` alias resolved (or `baseUrl`), the same for `apps/admin`, and a `pathNot` for the App Router special files in the `no-orphans` rule. Found by: a verification pass over the repository (2026-06-29).

### Principal authorization (P0, blocking)

- **N-AUTH-3** (FITNESS-23-EVADED): Authority derived from the request body must be impossible, not a convention: a total scope registry, a brand only the authentication layer mints, a per-route authority declaration that fails at boot. The brand's seam is designed at `enterTenantContext(AccountScope)`; `enterTenantContext` lives in `apps/api/src/security/tenantContext.ts`. Fitness #23 never fired: its ERE `\.\$(queryRaw|…)\(` reads `\$` as a literal dollar, so it matches only an untyped parenthesized call. Re-measured on 2026-10-28, it saw 4 of the 28 call sites in its scope, all in excepted files, and missed 7 tagged templates (SMELL-111) and 17 typed calls (`$queryRaw<T>`); a repair that adds only the tagged form still misses the typed ones. The typed pgvector calls of `PrismaSemanticRetrievalAdapter.ts:42,63` are not excepted, so 'no leak today' must be re-checked. The repair also rewrites, dated, the count 'Seven live statements' in `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md` (SMELL-130). Where: `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/proposal.md` section 3, `docs/security/MULTI_TENANT_GUARDS.md`, fitness #23 (`.github/workflows/fitness.yml:571`), `apps/api/src/security/tenantContext.ts`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaSemanticRetrievalAdapter.ts:42,63`. Done: Violation caught at compilation, boot or CI; the mechanism declares what it cannot catch; a fitness check on actor identity taken from the request (hard-zero, baseline 6) and one on client-provided tenant scope (ratchet, baseline 12) mirrored in `.github/workflows/fitness.yml` under free numbers; fitness #23 repaired. The mechanism also names who covers what it cannot catch. Depends on: N-SEC-3.b.

- **1.13**: Fitness #32 (committed `.skip`/`.only`) is a text grep; wanted over the syntax tree in every test-shaped file, also `.fixme`, `.todo`, `skipIf`, `runIf`, option-object `skip`. The check moves into the Test Contracts package, as a module of `packages/test-contracts/src` that parses each file with the TypeScript compiler's `createSourceFile`, without a typecheck. It flags those members whether called or merely referenced, on `it`, `test`, `describe`, `suite` and `test.describe`; a `skip`, `only` or `todo` property of the options object, the second argument; and Playwright's `test.skip(` and `test.fixme(` in any position. It complements the ESLint rules of 2.4, which give IDE feedback and a per-file ratchet on the vitest files only. The block of #32 in `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md` loses its false claim that only 5 of 83 vitest configs set `forbidOnly` and its `.todo(` exemption. The `describeIf` at `apps/client/tests/integration/authContext.test.tsx:54`, a reference to `describe.skip`, goes first. Where: `packages/test-contracts/src`, `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml`, `apps/client/tests/integration/authContext.test.tsx`. Depends on: 1.10. Done: red planted: (a) `test.fixme(` in a spec, (b) `const d = describe.skip;`, (c) `it("x", { skip: true }, ...)` exit 1; (d) `{ skip: 2 }` in a Prisma call stays green.
