- **3.9**: The merge-gate verdict (static rules V1, V2, V3, V7): each required context produced by exactly one job, no job-level `if` on a required job, `needs` paired with `!cancelled()`, no `paths` filter on a required workflow. The check lives in the Test Contracts package `packages/test-contracts`, with self-tests and a block in `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`; its fitness number is still to assign, since #48 names another check. It parses every workflow with `yaml` and reads `.github/rulesets/main.json`. V1 counts jobs of workflows that run on `pull_request` to main, expanding `${{ matrix.* }}`: zero producers leave the pull request waiting forever, two mean two jobs answer for one context. V2 allows a job-level `if:` only from an allowlist of expressions always true on a pull request, initially `github.event_name != 'schedule'`, because a skipped required job counts as success. V3 also wants a step whose `run` reads `needs.<id>.result` for each needed job. V7 also forbids `paths-ignore`. Where: `packages/test-contracts/src`, `.github/rulesets/main.json`, `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`. Depends on: 1.10, 3.4, 3.5. Done: five planted reds (second `Security Audit`, deleted Integration Tests job, `if: github.event_name == 'push'` on `test-packages`, `!cancelled()` removed from Coverage Merge, `paths:` on `ci.yml`) each exit 1
- **3.10**: The verdict gate's operational rules (V4 `continue-on-error`, V5 `\|\| true` on a gate tool) and V6 drift between GitHub's live ruleset and the committed one. V4 exempts a step whose name ends in "(never gates)". V5's gate tools are `vitest`, `node --test`, `playwright`, `k6`, `osv-scanner`, `gitleaks`, `pnpm audit`, `semgrep`, `squawk`, `turbo run` and `run-tests.sh`. V6 reads `GET /repos/{repo}/rules/branches/main` with `GITHUB_TOKEN` and compares its contexts and strict setting with `.github/rulesets/main.json`; it runs on push to main, nightly and on dispatch, never on a pull request, because a pull request that edits the ruleset would stay red until an administrator applies it. A network failure gets 3 retries before "could not measure". Where: `.github/rulesets/main.json`. Depends on: 3.9, 3.11. Done: (a) V4, (b) V5 planted, (c) a false context in the ruleset plus V6 locally, each exit 1; a network failure reads "could not measure", never green

Expected results: an altered signature, `alg:none`, a malformed or an expired JWT get 401; `__proto__` or `constructor.prototype` on a write route gets 400 or is stripped, with no leak; `127.0.0.1`, `169.254.169.254`, `10/8` or `[::1]` as an external-notification sink URL is rejected; 404 and 500 carry no stack or internals; the login returns no hash, secret or MFA material and does not echo the password; an unknown origin is not reflected with credentials and its preflight is rejected; a NoSQL operator in the login body gets 400, not 500; an oversized body gets 413; a dangerous file type on the real upload route is rejected; a DOCTYPE entity on `/auth/saml/:accountId/callback` (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:320`) is rejected without expansion.

A count of 0 kills is reliable only because `disableBail` is on (`7.2`); such a test reopens its ledger row under criterion (a) or (d). Done: both-directions rule verified. - **4.X2**: Apply the decision rule H1 to H5 to X1's table (decision H7, the Maintainer). The rule is applied to the table and is not itself the decision. Vitest must meet every hard criterion: H1 verdict parity 100 % in 3 of 3; H2 every M3 red case exits non-zero under vitest; H3 the M5 guards fit in config plus a reporter of at most 60 lines; H4 M6 needs no assertion change and no workaround, so a mock, alias or stub added to let Prisma or the saga engine run under vite fails H4 by the no-patches rule; H5 CI runtime at most 1.25 times node:test's and under 15 minutes. Soft criteria: the M3 score, M7 and M8 no worse, M9 within the runner's memory. A migration, if chosen, is integration-tier work with the codemod of `4.X1`. Depends on: 1.8, 4b.3, 3.8, 4.X1. Done: a decision recorded. If H1 to H5 hold, the integration tier consolidates on vitest, since node:test then sees no class vitest misses; if a hard criterion fails, node:test stays and the failed criterion is written down as the class only node:test sees; if only H5 fails and everything else is better, the numbers go to the Maintainer.

## Security slice (leads to verify before scheduling)

- `GET /activity-feed`: any signed-in Member can read audit rows of other Organizations. Confirmed by reading `apps/api/src/audit/activityFeedRoutes.ts:55-83` and `activityFeedService.ts:114-170`; not run. The item N-AUTH-2, attached to A-ADMIN-OPS, already holds it, with its definition of done and two traps a fix must avoid.
- The create route of `/admin/accounts` accepts a role and does not check the caller's role (`apps/api/src/admin/accountLifecycleRoutes.ts:130-158`). It leaves with the deletion decided for F-05.
- Team: a route takes the acting Member's id from the request body (area A-TEAM). Not verified.
- Notifications: a Member can write to any user id, and the model is outside the tenant guard (A-TEAM). Not verified.
- Approvals, post comments and first comments carry no Organization filter (A-CONTENT). Not verified.
- Thread routes lack an ownership check and the model is not tenant-guarded (A-ANALYTICS). Not verified.
- `/health/detailed` has no guard; compliance writes ask only a read permission; five administrator routes write audit rows that name no administrator (A-ADMIN-OPS). Not verified.
- **SSO-CALLBACKS-SIGN-IN-WITH-A-LITERAL-PASSWORD**: The SAML callback and the OIDC callback each sign in through the administrators' password sign-in, passing a fixed string, a different one for each protocol, as the password. The SAML callback validates the post response (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:340`), takes the e-mail from the assertion (`:347-348`) and calls `authService.login` with that e-mail and the fixed string (`:357-360`, the string at `:358`); the OIDC callback takes the e-mail from the UserInfo claims (`apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:423-424`) and does the same (`:431-434`, the string at `:432`). `AuthService.login` hands the call to `AuthServiceCore.login` (`apps/api/src/auth/authService.ts:74-91`), which reads the administrator's stored credentials by that e-mail (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts:166`), checks the string against the stored password hash with an Argon2 verification (`:187`, `apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts:455-457`, `apps/api/src/auth/passwordHashing.ts:57-63`) and answers `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` when it does not verify (`:216`), then refuses an inactive administrator (`:219`) and, when the administrator has multi-factor enabled and no token was passed, answers that multi-factor is required (`:256-258`). What decides whether the sign-in succeeds is therefore whether an active administrator holds that e-mail and a stored password hash that verifies against the fixed string; for it to succeed, an administrator's password would have to be that string. When it does not verify, both callbacks answer 200 with the profile read from the provider and `requiresProvisioning: true` (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:365-377`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:438-450`); when it does, they set the refresh cookie and answer with the user and an access token (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:387-400`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:460-473`). No code under `apps/api`, `apps/admin`, `apps/client`, `packages` or `infra` other than these two sites holds either string (searched on 2026-11-19). The two callbacks belong to the single-sign-on feature A-TEAM-08 of the area A-TEAM (`docs/product/plan/areas.md`), which depends on F-03; the row is kept here because it is a security lead and this section is where the plan keeps those. Where: the lines above. Decided (2026-11-19, the Product Owner): the row stays a row of the plan until single sign-on opens, with what was measured below and a condition of done, and the two calls do not come out now. Measured on 2026-11-19, the two paths and the policy only, not a full security review: both route modules are registered (`apps/api/src/index.ts:670-671`); each fixed string is 12 characters made of lower-case letters and symbols, with no upper-case letter and no digit (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:358`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:432`); the administrators' password policy asks for at least 12 characters with lower case, upper case, a number and a special character (`validatePasswordStrength`, `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthSchemas.ts:192`, `:203-234`), so no path that applies the policy can leave an administrator with either string, and the callbacks therefore never sign anyone in today; the development seed does not hold `ADMIN_PASSWORD` to the policy (`evaluateAdminPassword`, `infra/prisma/src/seedGuard.ts:81-87`) and refuses under a production environment. Since F-02 T3.2 the callbacks answer every failure of `AuthServiceCore.login` alike, the refusal for a password that must be changed (`PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED`) included, with 200 and `requiresProvisioning: true` (`apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:365-377`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:438-450`). The hazard is the design: provisioning users with that string as their password would turn a string that is public in the repository into a working password at the ordinary sign-in. Done: The session of each callback is issued from the provider's validated response, never through the password sign-in, and neither fixed string remains. Found by: F-02 T2d's writer (2026-11-19).
- **SECURITY-MANAGER-DEFAULT-CSP-DIVERGES**: The `SecurityManager` constructor defines a second content security policy, with `'unsafe-inline'` and `'unsafe-eval'` in `script-src` and with `ws:` in `connect-src`, while `createApp()` passes its own policy, which replaces that default whole through the spread of the argument, so no response ever carries the default; the unit tests build `SecurityManager` without an argument and pin the default as if it were live. Dormant. Security: a policy that reads as the one in force. Where: `apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:37-56`, `:76`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:436-453`. Done: one content security policy definition, and a test that pins the policy the app sends. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **SECURITY-MANAGER-UNCALLED-METHODS**: `validateRequest`, `createSecurityMiddleware`, `updateConfig` and `getConfig` of `SecurityManager` have no caller in `apps/api/src`: `createApp()` calls only `register`, and only tests call the four. Dormant. Security: request checking that reads as active. Where: `apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:228`, `:282`, `:323`, `:335`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:533`. Done: each is wired to a route or deleted with its tests, by the Maintainer's decision. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **SECURITY-HEADERS-CORP-CROSS-ORIGIN**: Every response sends `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy: cross-origin`, set by the custom header hook after helmet's own `same-origin`, although nothing calls the API from another origin; `X-Content-Type-Options` is also written twice, by helmet and by the hook. `apps/api/tests/unit/securityHeaders.test.ts` pins `cross-origin`. Live. Security: any page may embed a response of the API as a resource. Where: `apps/api/src/security/securityHeaders.ts:151`, `:104`, `:146`. Done: `same-origin` on every response, pinned by a test that reads the header, and one write of `X-Content-Type-Options`. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **GITLEAKS-ALLOWLIST-STALE-REASON**: The comment above the `Bearer test-token` allowlist entry of the secret scanner gives `@fastify/cors` examples as its reason, and that package is no longer a dependency, so the entry's reason is false. Dormant. Security: a configuration of the secret scanner whose justification does not hold. Where: `.gitleaks.toml:40`, the entry at `:41`. Done: the comment names the files that hold the value, or the entry leaves; the Maintainer decides, since the file is security configuration. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **CHECKOUT-STEPS-KEEP-PERSISTED-CREDENTIALS**: None of the 41 checkout steps of the 13 workflows that check out the repository sets `persist-credentials: false`: every one is `actions/checkout@v7`, and `rg -n 'persist-credentials' .github` finds nothing (2026-11-20). With the action's default, each job's token stays in the checkout's git configuration, where every later step of the job can read it, a third-party action or a script included. The workflows pin each action at a major version, the recorded practice (FP-013, `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md:252`), not at a commit, so the code a step runs can change under the same tag. What the token can do is the job's `permissions`; the stall workflow of the error sites, for one, grants `issues: write` (`.github/workflows/error-sites-stall.yml:31-33`). Live. Security: a job's token readable by steps that do not need it. Where: the lines above. Done: every checkout from which no later step pushes sets `persist-credentials: false`, held by a check over the workflows; or a recorded decision of the Maintainer keeps the default. Found by: the native review of the stall workflow of the central error system's first slice (`ef7`, 2026-11-20).
- **FAILURE-LINE-URL-KEEPS-THE-QUERY-STRING**: The failure line and the Sentry report of the central error path carry the request's raw URL, query string included (`url: request.url`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:161`; the line's field at `:222`, the report's at `:235`), and a query can carry a credential: the OAuth callbacks receive `code` and `state` in it (OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS). A callback whose failure reaches the handler therefore writes its `code` and `state` to the log, and to Sentry when its status is 500 or above. Fastify's own request line already carried the same URL for every request (`url: req.url`, `node_modules/.pnpm/fastify@5.12.6/node_modules/fastify/lib/logger-pino.js:50`), through a logger that redacts nothing (FASTIFY-LOGGER-UNREDACTED); whether the handler before the slice sent the URL to Sentry was not read. Live. Security: a credential in the log and in Sentry. Where: the lines above. Done: the line and the report name the route pattern and drop or redact the query, with a case that fails an OAuth callback and reads neither `code` nor `state` in the line or the report. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B7, and the independent verifier's first report, 2026-11-20).
- **HTTP-LIMITERS-KEY-BY-IP-ALONE**: The canon prefers an authenticated principal to the IP on an authenticated endpoint (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:71`), and ADR-0021 lists per-principal keying under "Revisit if" (`docs/technical/ADR-0021-trusted-proxy-peer-model.md:179-181`). Every key of an HTTP limiter is built from `resolveClientIp`: the table-driven pre-handler (`apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:221`, `:248`) and the administrators' limiter (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthMiddleware.ts:230`); `rg -n 'keyGenerator' apps/api/src` matches nothing. Behind `socket-only` that is one shared bucket per portal. Where: the lines above. Done: authenticated routes key by user or account id and the IP key is left to unauthenticated traffic, with a test per key shape, by the Maintainer's decision on ADR-0021's "Revisit if". Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S04).
- **REQUEST-IP-READ-OUTSIDE-THE-RESOLVER**: The canon says every client IP used for a security decision comes from `resolveClientIp(request)` (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:66`). About 20 sites in `apps/api/src` read `request.ip` or `req.ip` themselves, most to stamp an audit row: `apps/api/src/auth/authRoutes.ts:67`, `:168`, `apps/api/src/auth/mfaRoutes.ts:351`, `:420`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:433`, `apps/api/src/auth/samlRoutes.ts:359`, `apps/api/src/audit/auditMiddleware.ts:88`, `apps/api/src/compliance/complianceRoutes.ts:246`, `apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthMiddleware.ts:146`, `apps/api/src/middleware/correlationMiddleware.ts:77` and ten more (`rg -n 'request\.ip\b|req\.ip\b' apps/api/src -g '*.ts' | rg -v 'resolveClientIp.ts|trustedProxy' | wc -l` prints 22, two of them comments: `apps/api/src/index.ts:232` and `apps/api/src/services/AuditableService.ts:142`). With `trustProxy` pinned (`apps/api/src/index.ts:232`) `request.ip` is the resolver's answer, so the sites agree today; fitness #29 is blind to them because it matches the header literals `"x-forwarded-for"` and `"x-real-ip"` only (`docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md:501-508`). Where: the lines above. Done: a check refuses `request.ip` outside the resolver and the audit-stamping helper, or the Maintainer records that `request.ip` is the resolver's answer by construction and the canon says so. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S04).
- **REMOVED-HOP-COUNT-VARIABLE-NAMED-AS-IN-FORCE**: Documents and a test still describe `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT` as a live setting, which ADR-0021 removed and the API refuses to boot with. The feature design `docs/features/auth-rate-limit-integrity/design.md` argues from `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT = 0` and `hops ≥ 1` (`:33`, `:489`, `:522`, `:594`); `apps/api/tests/unit/auditLogger.test.ts:180` says "the test's fail-closed hop count (TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT=0)"; `docs/architecture/ADMIN_CUSTOMER_PARITY.md:43` and `:465` say the customer routes pass `env.TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT` to the resolver. The boot refusal itself has a removal condition only in a comment, "Delete this block once no deployed environment sets the variable" (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:520-526`), with no owner, no date and no check that finds a deployment that still sets it; ADR-0021 explains why it is loud (`docs/technical/ADR-0021-trusted-proxy-peer-model.md:117-124`) and not when it goes. Where: the lines above. Done: the design and the parity document name the peer model or carry a note that the variable is removed, the test comment drops the variable, and the refusal gets a remove-when the Maintainer owns. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S08).
- **PORTAL-CLIENT-IP-RELAY-IS-ONE-FILE-TWICE**: `apps/admin/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts` and `apps/client/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts` differ in one line, the `@description` (`diff` of the two prints line 3 only). Their tests (`apps/admin/lib/http/__tests__/forwardedFor.test.ts`, `apps/client/lib/http/__tests__/forwardedFor.test.ts`) differ in the `@description` block (lines 3 to 6, the words "admin" and "client-portal", with the text re-wrapped) and in the `describe` title at line 13 (`(admin)` and `(client)`); their cases are the same. Both helpers relay `x-real-ip` when no `x-forwarded-for` is present (`apps/admin/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts:29-32`, `apps/client/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts:29-32`), and no code of `apps/api/src` reads `x-real-ip` (`rg -n -i 'x-real-ip' apps/api/src` finds two comments, `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts:526` and `apps/api/src/security/resolveClientIp.ts:9`), so the branch relays a header nothing uses. Where: the lines above. Done: one helper in a shared package that both portals import, or the Maintainer accepts the copy, and the `x-real-ip` branch is deleted or given a reader. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S09).
- **API-SECRET-NAMED-KEYS-HAVE-NO-LENGTH-FLOOR**: The api's env schema holds four secrets to `z.string().min(SECRET_MIN)` (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:49`, `:111-114`), and 22 keys whose names carry `SECRET`, `TOKEN`, `PASSWORD`, `KEY` or `CREDENTIAL` have no length floor: `apps/api/src/config/env.ts:108`, `:181`, `:182`, `:188`, `:189`, `:206-209`, `:213`, `:216`, `:218`, `:228`, `:233`, `:238`, `:243`, `:248`, `:253`, `:258`, `:263`, `:268` and `:273` (`DELETION_NAME_DIGEST_KEY_RING` at `:144` has its own check and is left out). The canon states the floor for a secret the project generates (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON_REFERENCE.md:54`); most of the 22 are issued by a third party, which may not allow a floor. Not decided: which of the 22 the project generates. Where: the lines above. Done: each of the 22 is classified as generated or issued, the generated ones take `min(SECRET_MIN)`, and the rule says what holds for the issued ones. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S12).
- **LITERAL-SECURITY-PARAMETERS-NOT-AUDITED-FOR-A-CITED-SOURCE**: The canon says a literal security value is written with the primary source that justifies it, in the JSDoc beside it or in its ADR, and lists seven kinds: an Argon2 cost, a JWT algorithm, an OAuth scope list, a CSP directive, a CORS origin, a cookie flag and a rate-limit threshold (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:127`). The walk checked one kind of the seven for a cited source and left six unchecked, so how many literals of those six carry no source is unknown; the canon names `apps/api/src/auth/passwordHashing.ts` as the model of a cited value. Where: `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:127-130`. Done: each kind is swept over the tree, the values with no source are listed, and each is given its source or the Maintainer's decision, as the canon says. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S13).
- **WORKER-TENANT-PREDICATE-NOT-VERIFIED-QUERY-BY-QUERY**: The canon says a worker runs the raw client with no guard, so every query carries an explicit `accountId` predicate and binds the GUC with `setTenantGuc` in the same transaction (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:46`). That every worker query holds it was not checked query by query. The callers of `setTenantGuc` outside the extension are `apps/workers/src/services/ChannelAuthFailureRecorder.ts:92`, `apps/workers/src/mentionIngestWorker.ts:158` and `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/ChannelRepository.ts:90`, `:136`; the raw client reaches the workers through `apps/workers/src/container/workerContainer.ts`, `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts` and `apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts` (`workerPrisma`). Where: the lines above. Done: every query the workers make through the raw client is read and listed with its predicate and its GUC binding, a miss is a row of its own, and a test or a gate holds the list. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row S03).
- **CACHE-KEY-TENANT-RULE-MISSING-FROM-THE-CANON**: The canon's cache section says keys start with a feature prefix ending in a colon (`docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md:54`) and says nothing on how a key identifies its tenant, so each key's author decides. N-SEC-4 is the known instance: the AI response cache key omits `accountId` (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:227`, `docs/product/plan/areas.md:295`). Where: the lines above. Done: a rule written with its research in the canon (the tenant is part of every shared key, or the key states why it is global), and a check or a test that reads the keys; N-SEC-4 closes under it. Found by: the context diet, T3, the walk of `LOGGING_CANON.md` (2026-11-26, row O04).

## Gates

- The gate retirement audit (item GATE-RETIREMENT-AUDIT) has its inventory: 54 fitness checks, all with something to read; four named exceptions that exempt nothing; one security check that sees 3 of 28 sites; 28 checks that do not assert their directory exists.
- The CI job "Dependency Consistency" carries six fitness checks and is not in the repository's list of required checks; to confirm against the live branch protection.
- Controls documented without effect: two lint globs that match no directory, two edit tripwires not implemented, a battery verdict no hook reads.

## Findings since the plan entered

- **PRISMA-MIGRATE-URL-ON-COMMAND-LINE**: `prisma migrate deploy` (Prisma 7.10.1) starts its schema engine with the datasource URL, password included, on the command line (`--datasource`), so `ps` on the host shows the database password while a migration runs: measured on the start sequence, 53 samples of the process list carried it during one migration of 83. It is Prisma's own spawn, the same for `pnpm db:migrate`, for CI's migrate step and for the start sequence, which calls Prisma twice on every start (the proof that the connection reaches the started database, then the migration); the start command's own spawn of pnpm carries no credential. No option of Prisma 7.10.1 keeps it off the command line: its CLI passes `--datasource` in every case, and its configuration takes only `url` and `shadowDatabaseUrl`. What can: a short-lived role for the migration, a host that hides other users' processes (`hidepid`), a socket with no password, or a change upstream. Where: `infra/prisma/package.json:21`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:415`, `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1130` (the proof) and `:1151` (the migration). Done: Decided by the Maintainer on 2026-11-12: accepted for local and CI databases. The row stays open on one condition: the migrating credential is kept off the host's process list before a shared or production host runs a migration (a short-lived role for the migration is the candidate, not measured yet). Found by: F-01's third step (the migration), by its independent verification, and measured again by the step.
- **PRISMA-CLI-BUNDLES-ITS-OWN-EFFECT**: `prisma/build/cli.js` of prisma 7.10.1 (2,748,673 bytes) inlines its own copy of `effect` 3.20.0: the version constant `"3.20.0"` sits beside `Symbol.for("effect/Effect")`, the bundle's embedded dependency list reads `effect:"3.20.0"`, and the copy carries the `Schema` namespace, `TemplateLiteral` and its lazy wildcard `[\s\S]*?` included (16 mentions of the name, 2 of the wildcard). That is a version GHSA-7589-6fvf-x4pw names (high, `effect` at or below 3.22.0, a cubic backtrack in `Schema.TemplateLiteral`). The CVE floor of 2026-11-19, `"effect@<3.22.1": 3.22.1`, moves the copy `@prisma/config` resolves under `node_modules`, which the CLI requires to decode its configuration; it cannot move a copy compiled into the bundle, and `pnpm audit`, which reads the lockfile, does not see that copy. Not measured: whether the CLI builds a template-literal schema from the inlined copy, and on whose input. Where: `node_modules/.pnpm/prisma@7.10.1*/node_modules/prisma/build/cli.js`; `pnpm-workspace.yaml` (the `effect` override); `docs/security/DEPENDENCY_AUDIT_RECORD.md` §"Floor history", note `effect`. Done: The first stable Prisma release whose CLI bundle carries `effect` at or above 3.22.1 is installed and the bundle's version constant is read again; or a measurement shows the CLI builds no template-literal schema from that copy, and the row closes with that measurement. Found by: the writer of the `effect` CVE floor (2026-11-19), re-measured on the installed bundle the same day.
- **SECRETLINT-14-PRESET-BUILT-IN**: secretlint 14.0.0 (published 2026-11-15T07:06:31Z, mature from 2026-11-22T07:06Z) builds the recommended rule preset in, and the registry now marks `@secretlint/secretlint-rule-preset-recommend` 13.0.7, the version the root manifest pins, deprecated: "Built into secretlint v14 and later. Remove this package; the rule id still works." (its registry metadata was last modified 2026-11-15T07:27:03Z). Where: `package.json:85` (the preset), `package.json:106` (`secretlint` 13.0.7), `.secretlintrc.json:5` (the rule id). Found by the lockfile change of 2026-11-15 that records the deprecation, made because `pnpm dedupe --check` had turned red on `main` with no change of ours. Done: `secretlint` moves to 14.x once it is mature, in the weekly dependency window of 2026-11-24; the preset leaves the root manifest and the lockfile, and every generated record that lists it is regenerated (`pnpm check:deps-ledger` and `pnpm check:legal` say which); whether `.secretlintrc.json` keeps the rule id with the package gone is measured on 14, not assumed from the deprecation text; a secretlint run over the tree reports what it reported on 13.0.7.
- **MUTATION-DIFF-CHILD-PROCESS-SUBJECT**: `pnpm mutation-diff` mutates its subject in a Vite transform, so a subject the suite runs as a child process is never mutated. A case over `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs`, whose suite drives the command with `execFile`, ends `UNDECIDED (not-applied)` although its block asserts the very exit code the mutation changes (measured with base and head equal); such a case can only read BEHAVIOUR-CHANGED, from its clean runs, and fitness #54 takes that record for a rewritten block. The tool's residual limits do not say so. Where: `scripts/testing/lib/mutation-diff-plugin.mjs:61`, `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs:66`, `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts`. Done: The tool learns to mutate a command the suite runs as a child process (decided by the Product Owner and the Maintainer on 2026-11-12: "It should learn to mutate commands."); meanwhile such a command is mutated in a clone, with the suite pointed at the clone through `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE`, which gives the same result for one mutation but no required record, no comparison of the base's tests with the head's and no record a machine checks. Found by: F-01's second step (the services), measured in its third (the migration).
- **MUTATION-DIFF-MIXED-KIND-BASE-RED**: Closed by #905. `pnpm mutation-diff` gave BEHAVIOUR-CHANGED only when every base clean failure fell on the blocks of the SAME case, while a case holds one kind, so a change that deletes one block of a test file and rewrites another left both of its cases UNDECIDED `base-red-clean`: the deletion case on the rewritten blocks and the rewrite case on the deleted one, although the old file fails on the new code and the head is green (measured on F-01 T3b2b, part B-ii, one deletion and two rewrites). Retitling the rewritten blocks into deletions, the way past it B-i took, changes a test only to fit the tool. A case is now BEHAVIOUR-CHANGED when its head clean run is green and its base clean run is red on blocks that it, or another case of the same case file over the same test file, lists, at least one of them its own; `--only` narrows the cases that run, never those blocks. A red on a block no case lists, or on another case's blocks with none of its own, still reads UNDECIDED `base-red-clean`; each case still holds one kind, with its refusals, and the record's shape is unchanged. Where: `decide` in `scripts/testing/lib/mutation-diff-core.mjs` (the `peers` input), `decideCase` and `mutationRun` in `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs`, and the blocks "decide: a base red on blocks another case of the file lists" and "main: a base red that falls on the blocks of two cases of one test file" in `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/mutationDiff.test.ts`. Found by: F-01 T3b2b, part B-ii.
- **TRACKER-REMOVAL-DATE-OFF-BY-ONE**: Nineteen mentions in sixteen files say the testing refoundation tracker and its fixed plan left the tree on 2026-11-10, the day the change was written; it merged on 2026-11-11 (#872, `e5045226`). Where: `docs/product/PLAN.md:14`, `docs/product/plan/cross-cutting.md:291`, `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md:6`, `docs/development/TESTING_REFOUNDATION_MEASUREMENTS.md:3`, `docs/architecture/openapi-migration.md:111`, `docs/security/DEPENDENCY_AUDIT_RECORD.md:11`, `docs/technical/ADR-0032-architecture-gate-dependency-cruiser.md:35,258`, `docs/technical/ADR-0033-layer-boundaries-and-composition-root-injection.md:340`, `docs/technical/ADR-0034-credential-model.md:258`, `docs/technical/ADR-0035-stories-part-of-creating-ui.md:46`, `docs/technical/ADR-0036-dependency-update-cadence.md:16`, `docs/technical/ADR-0038-node-26-ahead-of-lts.md:13,214`, `docs/technical/ADR-0039-decorative-demolition-coverage-exception.md:14-15`, `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json:8`, `scripts/testing/maturity-watchlist.json:88`, and the comments of fitness #30 and #37 in `CLAUDE.md:748-749,1118-1119`. The mention at `ADR-0039-decorative-demolition-coverage-exception.md:57-58` is about the metrics script, which did leave on 2026-11-10 (#859), and stays. Done: each mention gives 2026-11-11, or names 2026-11-10 as the day of the decision; a search of the tree for the phrase with the old date, wrapped lines included, returns only that one. Found by: the change that added the chain table's tracking columns.
- **PACKAGES-NO-APPS-REMAINING-GROUPS**: Ten package groups have no rule that keeps them from importing an application: `api-common`, `api-errors`, `eslint-plugin-testing`, `i18n`, `monitoring`, `observability`, `query-client`, `test-contracts`, `ui` and `vitest-shared`. The architecture gate holds `core`, `shared`, `ports`, `adapters` and `providers`, each by a rule of its own, and `infra/` is outside its scan (`depcruise apps/api/src apps/workers/src packages`). One rule from `^(packages|infra)/` to `^apps/`, with `infra` added to the scan, measured zero violations on 2026-11-12, so it would cover every group and make the four per-group rules redundant. Where: `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` (`core-no-apps`, `shared-no-apps`, `adapters-no-apps`, `providers-no-apps`), `package.json` (`check:architecture`). Done: One rule holds every workspace package away from the applications, the per-group rules it replaces leave with their rows of the policy suite, the scan reads `infra/`, and its red path is proven. Found by: DEF-155.
- **FITNESS-43-RETIRE-CANDIDATE**: Fitness #43 was written because a harness that omitted the metrics collector built a container that resolved `undefined`. The container now refuses `undefined` at registration, so that harness fails when it builds its container, and what the gate still adds is that every harness composes through `createRouteTestContainer`. Where: `.github/workflows/fitness.yml` step `#43`, the block `# 43.` of `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md` and its line in that document's `## Inventory`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/Container.ts:64-67`. Its prose also predates the tests typecheck ratchet (`apps/api/tsconfig.tests.json`, `apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs`): it still says no compiler opens `apps/api/tests`, and it cites `apps/api/src/index.ts:283` for a call now at `:326`. Done: the Maintainer decides whether #43 stays for the seam alone or retires, and its block in the reference, its inventory line, its workflow step and the count sentence move together. Found by CONTAINER-UNDEFINED-INSTANCE.
- **CONTAINER-FACTORY-RETURNS-UNDEFINED**: A singleton whose factory returns `undefined` is never cached: `resolve` and `peekInstance` read an `undefined` instance as not built yet, so the factory runs again on every `resolve` and its `undefined` is handed back with no error. `registerInstance` is closed; `register` is not. Where: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/Container.ts:96,145`. Done: a registration state separate from the instance value (or a refusal when a factory returns `undefined`), with a red test. Found by CONTAINER-UNDEFINED-INSTANCE.
- **TEST-CONTAINER-STRING-KEY-LOOP**: `createTestContainer` takes `Partial<Record<symbol, unknown>>` and still walks `Object.entries(overrides)`, which yields only string keys the parameter type cannot express; outside the two cases of `Container.test.ts` that reach the loop by a cast, its three callers pass nothing or symbol keys. Where: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setup.ts:131`. Done: the loop is deleted with its two cases, or the parameter type admits string keys. Found by CONTAINER-UNDEFINED-INSTANCE.
- **NIGHTLY-CACHE-LATE-HOOK**: Closed by #889. The nightly of 2026-11-13 failed two cases of `apps/api/tests/cache.integration.test.ts` in random order (seed 305022639): a `beforeEach` that timed out at 30 s, and a later case that read MISS for HIT. node:test stops waiting for a hook at its timeout and lets it run on; when that hook's `createApp()` returned, it assigned the suite's shared app and cache manager and flushed Redis in the middle of the next case. The suite's hooks now ask a case scope (`apps/api/tests/integration/helpers/caseScope.ts`) whether their case is still current: a late hook closes the app it built and writes nothing shared. Released on purpose while the second case awaited its cache write, the late hook failed 2 of 26 cases before the change and 1 of 26 after it. What stalled that `createApp()` is NIGHTLY-COLD-BOOT-STALL.
- **NIGHTLY-COLD-BOOT-STALL**: On 2026-11-13 the first `createApp()` of the cache suite's process took more than 30 s on the CI runner, once in twelve nights; the cause is not known. Locally it takes about 0.4 s cold and 75 ms warm, and it was not provoked by running the five suites of that night in their order with `DB_POOL_SIZE=1`, three times. The job's log could not name the slow step: `apps/api/scripts/run-tests.sh:501-502` prints the last 200 lines of a failing suite, which that night held only the last cases' boots. Where: `apps/api/src/index.ts` (`createApp`), `apps/api/scripts/run-tests.sh:489-502`. Done: the next occurrence names the boot step that stalled (a step timing printed past a threshold, or the head of a failing suite's output kept beside its tail), and the cause is then fixed or recorded. Found by NIGHTLY-CACHE-LATE-HOOK.
- **APP-CLOSE-LEAVES-RESOURCES**: `app.close()` closes the publish queue (`apps/api/src/index.ts:396`) and stops the health manager (`apps/api/src/health/healthRoutes.ts:336`); the saga runtime, the analytics broadcasters and the scheduler are stopped only by `drainServices`, which the signal handler runs (`apps/api/src/index.ts:1289-1344`). Measured on 2026-11-13 in `cache.integration.test.ts`, which builds one app per case: 24 active resources after the first case and 368 after the twenty-fourth, about 9 TCP sockets and 6 timers per `createApp()`, the same with a pool of 1 and of 8 connections; `createApp()` itself did not slow down. Done: closing the app stops what creating it started, consumers drained before the shared connections quit, safe to run beside the signal path, with a test that active resources do not grow across create and close cycles. Found by NIGHTLY-CACHE-LATE-HOOK.
- **SAGA-SUBSCRIBER-UNBOUNDED-WAIT**: By reading, never observed: the saga subscriber connection is built with `maxRetriesPerRequest: null` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/registerRedisConnections.ts:40-44`), the form for which `createRedisConnection` sets no `commandTimeout` (`apps/api/src/lib/redis.ts:56-64`), and `createApp()` awaits its `connect()` and `subscribe()` (`apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:798,805`). A Redis that accepts the TCP connection and does not answer would hold the boot with no limit. A refused connection fails at once, and a connection attempt that never completes fails after the 10 s `connectTimeout`; in both cases the boot continues without the subscriber (the `catch` at `apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:819`). It is the one wait with no limit found on the boot path, and a candidate for NIGHTLY-COLD-BOOT-STALL. Done: the Maintainer decides between a limit on that wait with the boot continuing degraded, and leaving it, on evidence from NIGHTLY-COLD-BOOT-STALL. Found by NIGHTLY-CACHE-LATE-HOOK.
- **BATTERY-STATIC-MODE**: Closed by #893. In the night of 2026-11-12 to 2026-11-13 two full local batteries went red on static gates that had not been run first (`duplicates`, then `deps-ledger`), each at the cost of a whole battery. By the Maintainer's decision of 2026-11-13, `scripts/testing/battery.sh --static` runs the 25 steps that need no test environment and run no product suite, with the full run's commands and order, and judges their logs by the full verdict's own rules through `scripts/testing/battery-static.mjs`: green only when every static step exited 0 and no log holds a warning line. It writes no verdict and certifies nothing; a full battery still decides a push. Each step is declared once with its kind, `static` or `suite`, and `--list` prints the steps a mode would run.
- **BOOLEAN-QUERY-OTHER-PARSES**: Eight boolean query flags are parsed by hand beside the shared `BooleanQuerySchema`. Three repeat its contract inline (`parentOnly`, `unreadOnly`, `resolved`: `z.enum(["true", "false"]).transform(...)`). Three go through a local `z.preprocess` helper with the same contract for strings (`isActive`, `emailVerified` and `mfaEnabled` of the admin account filters). Two are read as free strings and compared with `"true"` (`competitorAnalysis`, `includeCompetitors`), so `1`, `yes` or `TRUE` is read as false in silence instead of refused. Where: `apps/api/src/comments/commentRoutes.ts:43`, `apps/api/src/notifications/notificationRoutes.ts:33`, `apps/api/src/compliance/complianceSchemas.ts:63`, `apps/api/src/admin/accountLifecycleRoutes.ts:85`, `apps/api/src/trends/trendRoutes.ts:59,86,172,242`. Done: Every boolean query flag of the API parses through `BooleanQuerySchema`, and the two trend flags refuse another spelling once their callers are measured, each with a test. Found by: DEF-169.
- **DUPLICATES-GATE-EDITED-CLONE-READS-AS-NEW**: The duplicates baseline identifies a clone by jscpd's fingerprint of the cloned text, so an edit inside or at the edge of a recorded clone makes the gate report it as new although nobody duplicated code. Measured twice in DEF-169: the parameter tags fitness #47 asks for changed three recorded fingerprints in `analyticsRoutes.ts` (#883), and one call made shorter moved the start of a recorded clone in `postRoutes.ts` by one token (#884). Each time the remedy was `pnpm check:duplicates:update-baseline`, after the reported clone was compared by hand with the base's listing. Where: `scripts/testing/jscpd-baseline-gate.mjs`, `.jscpd-baseline.json`. Done: The gate tells a recorded clone whose fingerprint changed (the same two files, overlapping lines) from a new one and names the refresh as the remedy for the first, and its red path still fails on a clone that is new. Found by: DEF-169.
- **FLOOR-OTEL-INSTRUMENTATION-HTTP**: Closed by #890. `pnpm audit` failed on `main` for GHSA-3v2p-hm54-8qcq (moderate, CVE-2026-128080): `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-http` 0.219.0 wrote the raw inbound query string into its server spans. A range-scoped override holds it at 0.222.0, the minimal patched version, on which OSV, GitHub's `first_patched_version` and the upstream fix commit agree; its one consumer, `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` 0.77.0, declares `^0.219.0`. The override brings `@opentelemetry/core` 2.11.0 and no other package, `pnpm audit --audit-level low` exits 0, and `@observability/opentelemetry` passes 33 of 33 tests, its count before the change. Where: `pnpm-workspace.yaml:270`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:317,443,604`.
- **OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS**: Closed by #896. The OAuth callbacks receive `code` and `state` in the query string, and two span channels kept them raw when tracing was on. `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-http` 0.222.0 redacts only the seven keys of its default list (`sig`, `Signature`, `AWSAccessKeyId`, `X-Goog-Signature` and three `X-Amz-*` keys) on its server spans unless `redactedQueryParamsServer` is set, and the telemetry set-up set no redaction option. The `request` span of `@fastify/otel` 0.21.2 writes Fastify's `request.url`, query included, to `url.path` (its `index.js:419`), which no `instrumentation-http` option reaches. Both were measured on 2026-11-13 with fake values on a loopback server (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:443`); the API's own process was not. The telemetry set-up now registers `QueryParameterRedactionSpanProcessor` ahead of every exporter's processor (`createTelemetrySpanProcessors()`): in `onEnding`, before the span is read-only, it replaces the value of every name of `SENSITIVE_QUERY_PARAMETERS` with `REDACTED` in every attribute, the span name, the status message and each event's name and attributes, whatever instrumentation made the span, and every exporter sits behind `RedactionGuardedSpanExporter`, which forwards only the spans that processor marked. The HTTP instrumentation receives the same list as `redactedQueryParams` and `redactedQueryParamsServer`, and Sentry's `beforeSendSpan` applies the same function, since Sentry sent `code` and `state` raw in `url.full`, `http.url`, `http.target` and `http.query`. `querySecretSpans.test.ts` and `querySecretExport.test.ts` pin both inbound channels and the OTLP body, each red before the change. Where: `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/index.ts:197-216`, `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuth.ts:43`, `apps/api/src/auth/providerOAuthFlow.ts:48-52`, `apps/api/src/auth/oidcRoutes.ts:356,374,406`; closed in `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/queryRedaction.ts`, `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/spanQueryRedaction.ts`, `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/index.ts` and `apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts`. Found by: FLOOR-OTEL-INSTRUMENTATION-HTTP.
- **OTEL-OUTBOUND-QUERY-TOKENS**: Closed by #896. Provider clients put the channel's access token in the query string of outbound `fetch` requests, and `access_token` is not one of the seven keys the HTTP instrumentation redacts by default. `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-undici` 0.29.0, which traces `fetch`, builds the span's URL from the request's origin and path, query included (`build/src/undici.js:154`), and that file names no redaction option. Measured on 2026-11-13 with a fake token over loopback, before and after the floor: the client span of a `fetch` whose query carries `access_token` held the token raw in `url.full` and `url.query`. So with tracing on, the span of such a provider call would have carried the token to the tracing backend; no provider call was run. The span processor of OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS closes it on every instrumentation: `access_token` is in `SENSITIVE_QUERY_PARAMETERS`, with the other credentials this product sends in a query (`client_secret`, `fb_exchange_token`, `input_token`, `api_secret`, `key`), and `querySecretSpans.test.ts` pins the HTTP and `fetch` client spans, their error spans and the `?key=` of the Gemini connection test (`packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:451`), each red before the change. Where: `packages/providers/instagram/src/apiClient.ts:358`, `packages/providers/facebook/src/apiClient.ts:115`, `:433`, `packages/providers/threads/src/ThreadsAdapter.ts:227`, `:243`, `:295`. Found by: FLOOR-OTEL-INSTRUMENTATION-HTTP.
- **DEP-OUT-ZOD-TO-JSON-SCHEMA**: Closed by #891. `zod-to-json-schema` left `apps/api`. Nothing in the tree imported it: its one mention in source is a comment (`apps/api/src/utils/schemaUtils.ts:10`). It had been kept on 2026-10-25 as a peer of `@langchain/langgraph`; that peer is optional (`peerDependenciesMeta` of langgraph 1.4.4), and the installed `dist` of langgraph holds no import of it, so the repurpose graph never loaded it. The package left the lockfile with its declaration. Found by the dependency audit of 2026-11-13; removal decided by the Maintainer that day.
- **DEP-OUT-GEIST**: Closed by #891. `geist` left `apps/admin`. The root layout put the package's two classes on `<html>`, which define the variables `--font-geist-sans` and `--font-geist-mono`, and no stylesheet or component of `apps/admin` or `packages/ui` reads either, so the Admin has always rendered Tailwind's default font stacks and only preloaded two font files nothing used. Removing it changes no rendered text. Whether the Admin should use a font of its own is a product question nobody has asked; this row closes the unused package only. Found by the dependency audit of 2026-11-13; removal decided by the Maintainer that day.
- **FITNESS-41-BLIND-TO-MUST-CHANGE-PASSWORD**: Fitness #41 holds a list of consumption markers, the literal `data` forms that consume a single-use credential (`docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md:1127-1136`, the same list as `MARKERS` in `.github/workflows/fitness.yml:1325-1331`), and `mustChangePassword: false` is not on it, so the guarded write that clears the mark in the required password change (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:213-229`, the mark named in its `where` at `:216`) is not measured by the gate. Adding the marker would also report two sites that exist today. The authenticated change's unguarded `update` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:151-160`, keyed on `{ id }` alone, the mark cleared at `:158`) would be reported as it stands. The reset's claim (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:434-459`) clears the mark at `:450` and its `where` names the reset token and not the mark, so the gate would have to accept it as a claim of another credential, by a rule that says so. The site floor (`SITE_FLOOR`, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml:1332`) would be measured again with the marker. Where: the lines above. Done: The marker is added to the block, the workflow and the floor, the red path is proven on a planted write that clears the mark without naming it, and each site it reports is fixed or carries its reason. Found by F-02 T3.1's writer (2026-11-19).
- **TESTS-TYPECHECK-RATCHET-LETS-ITS-BASELINE-GO-STALE**: `apps/api/scripts/typecheck-tests-ratchet.mjs` fails on a new pair of file and error code and on a count that rises (`:250-253`, the failure at `:271-289`); when a count falls it prints that the ledger shrank and exits 0 (`:254-269`, `:290`), so a baseline above the tree's count passes. `apps/api/tests-typecheck-baseline.json` was last changed on 2026-11-10 (`b869cbf7`); `7e168244` and `c4d08146` (2026-11-16) rewrote the double of `apps/api/tests/unit/auditService.stats.test.ts` and did not touch it, and the rewrite of the baseline that followed lowered `totalErrors` from 2006 to 2002, so it stood 4 above the tree in between. While a baseline is above the tree, as many new errors of the same file and code as the difference can enter with no failure. The script runs in the `typecheck` script of `apps/api` (`apps/api/package.json:13`). Where: the lines above. Done: The gate fails when the baseline is not the tree's count, in both directions, with a case for each and its red path proven. Found by F-02 T3.1's writer (2026-11-19).
- **ADMIN-AUTH-ROUTES-EXEMPT-FROM-THE-IP-ALLOWLIST-WITHOUT-A-REASON**: `EXEMPT_PATHS` of the IP allowlist middleware names `/admin/auth/login`, `/admin/auth/password/reset` and `/admin/auth/password/reset/confirm` (`apps/api/src/security/ipAllowlistMiddleware.ts:27-34`, the lookup at `:95`), and the only comment above the list says what it is (`:26`), not why each path is on it. Through the last one, the confirmation of a reset (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:703`), an address outside an enabled allowlist can complete a lasting change of a credential, with the reset token alone. The required password change (`POST /admin/auth/password/change-required`) is not on the list, by the decision taken when it was added (2026-11-19). With an allowlist enabled and not empty (`apps/api/src/security/ipAllowlistMiddleware.ts:99`), an administrator outside it meets the two routes differently. `POST /admin/auth/login` is exempt (`:95`), so it reaches its handler, and a marked administrator is answered 403 `PASSWORD_CHANGE_REQUIRED` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthRoutes.ts:56`). `POST /admin/auth/password/change-required` is not exempt, so the hook answers 403 `IP_NOT_ALLOWED` before the handler (`apps/api/src/security/ipAllowlistMiddleware.ts:103-108`), which the portal's page shows as its `ipNotAllowed` message (`apps/admin/lib/auth/types.ts:109`). That administrator is told to change the password and cannot change it from that address. Where: the lines above. Done: Each exemption has its reason written beside it, or leaves, and the decision on the required change is written beside the list too. Found by F-02 T3.1's writer (2026-11-19).
- **AUDITABLE-SERVICE-STORES-FAILED-OPERATIONS-AS-SUCCESSFUL**: `AuditableService.writeAuditLog` stored `success: true` on every row until F-02 T3.2. An entry can now name its failure: `failure` on `AuditLogEntry` and on `UserActionOptions` (`apps/api/src/services/AuditableService.ts:91-92`, `:102-103`), stored as `success: false` with the code as `error` (`:363-364`); `logUserAction` passes it (`:155`) and one caller uses it, the second sign-in's refusal for a password that must be changed (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts:337`). Still wrong, each measured on 2026-11-20. The base's own branch for a failed operation, the catch of `executeWithAudit` (`:445-472`), puts `success: false` and the message in the details only (`:465`) and writes the row with no `failure` (`:469`), so that row is stored as successful. The second sign-in's other refusals call `logUserAction` with a reason in the details and no failure (`apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts:232`, `:249`), record a security event the same way (`:267`, `:294`) or write the row directly (`:196`). The loggers beside `logUserAction` do not pass the option through: `logAccountAction` (`:173`), `logSystemAction` (`:207`), `logResourceAction` (`:236`) and `logSecurityEvent` (`:266`, which calls `logAccountAction`). The writer also swallows a failed write and logs the whole entry, the e-mail in its details included (`:372-381`). Where: the lines above. Done: Every refusal and failed operation is stored as failed with its code, every logger passes the option, a failed audit write is not silent, and no personal value is logged, with a case for each. Found by F-02 T3.2's native review (2026-11-20).
- **PORTAL-FORWARDEDFOR-COMMENTS-NAME-THE-RETIRED-HOP-COUNT-MODEL**: The header comment of `apps/admin/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts` (`:6-9`) says the helper relays the client-IP header "so the backend resolver can hop-count to the real client" and that "the backend's hop-count math stays the sole authority"; the client portal's copy says the same (`apps/client/lib/http/forwardedFor.ts:6-9`). The backend retired hop counting: it resolves the client address by the immediate peer's trust, through `resolveClientIp`, and refuses to boot while `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOP_COUNT` is set (ADR-0021, `docs/technical/ADR-0021-trusted-proxy-peer-model.md:77`, `:117-121`). The relay itself, forwarding the inbound chain verbatim, is right for that model. Where: the lines above. Done: Both comments describe the peer model and point to ADR-0021. Found by F-02 T4's explorer (2026-11-20). More instances, read on 2026-11-26 by the context diet's walk of `SECURITY_CANON.md` (T3): the two proxy route handlers say the same in a comment, "so the backend resolver hop-counts the" client address (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:85`, `apps/client/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:80`), and the phrases the row quotes sit at `:7` and `:10` of both `forwardedFor.ts` files.
- **API-ERROR-ENVELOPES-DIFFER-AND-ROUTE-HANDLERS-OMIT-REQUIRED-FIELDS**: When it was found, the API answered an error in three shapes. The route handlers' base writes `{ ok: false, error: <text or code>, details? }` (`apps/api/src/lib/route-handler/BaseRouteHandler.ts:273-286`, and `"Internal server error"` at `:291-299`); the global error handler wrote `{ ok: false, error: { code, message, requestId, timestamp, details? } }`; the route limiter and the IP allowlist write `{ ok: false, error: { code, message } }` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthMiddleware.ts:245-252`, `apps/api/src/security/ipAllowlistMiddleware.ts:105-108`). The shared mapper `ApiError.fromResponse` (`packages/api-errors/src/ApiError.ts`) read the second and third shapes and read the first as an answer with no code, since `error` there is a string. The coding standard asked every error response to carry `statusCode`, `message`, `correlationId` and `timestamp`: the first shape carries none of them besides the message, and the second named `requestId` and not `correlationId`. The administration portal's sign-in reads the three shapes with its own reader (`apps/admin/lib/auth/backend-client.ts:132-145`) instead of the shared mapper. Decided by the Maintainer on 2026-11-20, in [ADR-0047](../../technical/ADR-0047-central-error-system.md): one registry of error codes, `packages/shared/src/error-registry/`; one body, RFC 9457's `application/problem+json`, written by one function, `sendProblem` (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts`), which the global error handler and the not-found handler call since the central error system's first slice, and the coding standard says an error response is that body (`docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md:103`); and the lint rule `errors/no-inline-error`, which refuses an error written in place. This item is the sweep of what the rule found when it was turned on: 3,492 sites in 522 files and 129 contexts on 2026-11-20, listed in `eslint-suppressions.json` (the history's birth row, `scripts/testing/error-sites-history/2026-11-20-no-inline-error-born.json`), one change per context, the administrators' sign-in first (ADR-0047, decision 5), while fitness #57 holds that the list only falls. Two questions the sweep answers when it reaches them. The four health and readiness answers whose status is 200 or 503 by the system's state (`apps/api/src/health/healthRoutes.ts:117`, `:125`, `:146`, `:314`) are counted as inline replies, since their status is no literal: whether a status report is an error answer is decided there. The portals' readers of the old bodies that the first slice did not change move with the routes they read, in slice 2: `apps/client/app/actions/auth.ts:130`, `:208-212`, `:297-299`; `apps/client/lib/hooks/useProjectChannels/api.ts:20`; `apps/client/lib/api/clients/usageClient.ts:66`; `apps/client/hooks/api/useBulkScheduling.ts:51`, `useRepurpose.ts:40`, `useTrendRadar.ts:41`; `apps/client/components/ai/analytics/hooks/usePredictiveData.ts:51`, `:63`; `apps/client/hooks/api/useBrandVoice.ts:52`; `apps/admin/lib/auth/backend-client.ts:132-145`; `apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:135`. A problem's `issues` are not carried into `ApiError.details`, which no reader of either portal or of `packages/ui` reads, and the client reads the body's type from the registry (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/problem.ts:31`, re-exported at `apps/client/lib/api/types.ts:207`), while the generated contract carries the body as its own `Problem` type since #965 (`packages/shared/src/api-generated/types.gen.ts:12`). Where: the lines above. Done: Every count of fitness #57's list is zero, and the change that empties the last context removes the constructors that take a message, the legacy reply helpers, the `ErrorCode` enum with `AppError` and the history (ADR-0047, decision 5.4), and declares `Problem` as every operation's default response (decision 2.8). Found by F-02 T4's explorer (2026-11-20); the health answers and the readers by the central error system's first slice.
- **PASSWORD-HASHER-PORT-DOES-NOT-REPORT-ITS-ALGORITHM**: The algorithm's name is written in three places and no port reports it. The schema default of `AdminUser.passwordHashAlgo` is `argon2id` (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:187`), `PasswordService.hashPassword` returns the literal `"argon2id"` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/PasswordService.ts:41-43`, stored at `:155`, `:224` and `:445`), and the hash's parameters name the type (`ARGON2_PARAMS.type`, `apps/api/src/auth/passwordHashing.ts:31`). The `PasswordHasher` port has `hash`, `verify` and `needsRehash` and says only in a comment that `hash` returns "the encoded Argon2id hash" (`packages/core/domain/src/repositories/PasswordHasher.ts:12-19`, the comment at `:13`). The creation leaves the column to the schema default, and the re-issue leaves it as it is, because the hasher's port does not report its algorithm and an eligible row was hashed by the same hasher (the sentence of the port method's JSDoc, `packages/core/domain/src/repositories/AdminUserRepository.ts:182-185`, and the adapter's, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaAdminUserRepository.ts:300-301`). A change of algorithm would be named in one of the three places and not in the others. Where: the lines above. Done: One source for the name, reported by the hasher and written by every write that stores a hash. Found by F-02 T3b's explorer (2026-11-20).
- **SCHEMA-UTILS-NO-CALLER**: `apps/api/src/utils/schemaUtils.ts` (`toJsonSchema`, `createFastifySchema`) is imported by its own suite alone, `apps/api/tests/schemaUtils.integration.test.ts`: no source or test file under `apps/` or `packages/` imports the module or calls either export (`rg -n 'schemaUtils|toJsonSchema|createFastifySchema' apps packages -g '*.ts' -g '*.tsx'`, 2026-11-13). `apps/api/ROUTE_MIGRATION_AUDIT.json` names the helper in three description strings (`:91`, `:111`, `:132`). Its comment says it stays for backwards compatibility after `fastify-type-provider-zod` replaced `zod-to-json-schema`. Where: `apps/api/src/utils/schemaUtils.ts`, `apps/api/tests/schemaUtils.integration.test.ts`. Done: not stated. Found by: deps-remove-3.
- **DEP-DECISIONS-2026-11-13**: Closed by #895. `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json` records the decisions the Maintainer took on 2026-11-13 on the dependency audit of that day, 18 entries in all. Kept on the ground `planned`, each with a note that names the plan rows describing its feature: eight of the ten packages the plan describes with no row that connects a caller (`@fastify/cookie`, `@node-saml/node-saml`, `openid-client`, `@langchain/langgraph`, `@langchain/core`, `isomorphic-dompurify`, `cronstrue`, `@opentelemetry/exporter-prometheus`), with no date, each leaving with its feature and not by itself; and seven of the eight that run only behind an operator switch that is off by default (`@fastify/otel`, `@opentelemetry/core`, `@opentelemetry/exporter-trace-otlp-http`, `@opentelemetry/sdk-node`, `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node`, `@opentelemetry/resources`, `stripe`). Of the six removals that leave with a row that already exists, `ws` and `@types/ws` move from `planned` to `remove` with BROADCAST-GATED-ON-WEBSOCKETS and DEF-21, `autocannon` takes `remove` with 6.P2, and `validator`, `@types/validator` (DC-3) and `cloudinary` (STOR-1) already carried that decision. Not written: `@googleapis/youtubeanalytics`, already `planned`; `pgvector` and `@opentelemetry/api`, at or above the real-site floor, where the check admits only a `remove` entry. The ledger's summary moves from 110 kept and 3 removals to 108 and 6, and `pendingBaseline` stays 1. Where: `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json`, `dependency-ledger.json`.
- **SENTRY-EVENT-QUERY-SECRETS**: Sentry sends a request's query outside its spans too, where `beforeSendSpan` never reaches. Measured on 2026-11-13 with `@sentry/node` 10.76.2 over loopback with fake values, after the span hook redacted every span: the transaction event of an inbound `GET /oauth/callback?code=…&state=…` carried `code` and `state` raw in `request.url` and `request.query_string`, and the HTTP breadcrumbs of later events carried them in `data.http.query`; Sentry filters `access_token` there itself (`[Filtered]`) and leaves `code` and `state`. The independent verification of 2026-11-13 measured two more facts: request headers carry the query too (a `Referer` and an `x-original-url` header reached the transaction event raw; cookies were filtered), and with the OpenTelemetry SDK registered first Sentry sends no span envelopes at all, so `beforeSendSpan` has spans to redact only when OpenTelemetry tracing is off. A lead, not measured: the native option `dataCollection.urlQueryParams` of `@sentry/core` 10.76.2 (`build/esm/integrations/requestdata.js:22,32,65`) may close the query channel. Sentry builds the request fields and the breadcrumbs in `@sentry/core` 10.76.2 `build/esm/integrations/requestdata.js:64-65` and `build/esm/integrations/http/add-outgoing-request-breadcrumb.js:19`, and the API's `Sentry.init` sets neither `beforeSendTransaction` nor `beforeBreadcrumb`, and its `beforeSend` reads no request field. Where: `apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts:118-132`. Done: not stated. Found by: OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS.
- **OTEL-REDACTION-AS-CANON-RULE**: The query redaction of OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS lives in two modules of `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/`: the list and the pure functions in `queryRedaction.ts`, which imports no OpenTelemetry package at run time, and the span processor, the exporter guard and the reporter in `spanQueryRedaction.ts`; it holds only while every span exporter goes through `createTelemetrySpanProcessors()`; no canon document states that rule and no check refuses an exporter registered outside it. `SocialMediaTelemetry` always hands the NodeSDK its own span processors, so the SDK never builds exporters from `OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER`, and built without `otlpEndpoint` it exports no span (`tests/telemetryWithoutEndpoint.test.ts`). Two things stay outside the redaction: span links, which are not rewritten (the one instrumentation of the set-up that sets a link attribute, `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-aws-lambda` 0.71.0, sets `messaging.message.id` on its links, `build/src/instrumentation.js:369-372`; the auto-instrumentations map enables it by default, `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` `build/src/utils.js:68`; it activates only inside a Lambda runtime, `LAMBDA_TASK_ROOT` and `_HANDLER`, `instrumentation.js:62-64`; and the value is a message id, not a URL), and a credential that reaches a span outside a query parameter: inside a cache key (OTEL-REDIS-KEY-CARRIES-OAUTH-STATE) or in a URL path (OTEL-URL-PATH-CARRIES-TELEGRAM-TOKEN). The processor relies on `onEnding`, a span processor hook upstream marks experimental; `RedactionGuardedSpanExporter`, in front of every exporter, fails closed if an SDK stops calling it, dropping every span the processor did not mark and reporting that once for the whole set-up, across exporters and flushes, with the cause stated as either that the SDK did not call `onEnding` or that the redaction processor is not registered on the tracer provider: one error-level line through the telemetry's `pino` logger, visible with default settings, and the same text through the OpenTelemetry diagnostics logger, which prints nothing unless a diag logger is registered; the text carries no span data (`tests/redactionGuard.test.ts`). Making the chokepoint a rule of the canon, with a check that refuses an exporter registered outside it, is the Maintainer's decision. Where: `packages/observability/opentelemetry/src/index.ts`, `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md`. Done: not stated. Found by: OTEL-INBOUND-QUERY-SECRETS.
- **OTEL-REDIS-KEY-CARRIES-OAUTH-STATE**: The OAuth flow store keeps each flow under a cache key that holds the OAuth `state` (`oauth:flow:<state>`, under the cache's `api:` prefix), and the Redis instrumentation writes each command with its key into the span's `db.statement`, so `state` reaches an exported span by a route that is not a query string, which the query redaction does not read. Measured by the independent verification of 2026-11-13 with fake values on a loopback Redis: raw ioredis `get` and `del` on the key `api:oauth:flow:<state>` produced `db.statement = "get api:oauth:flow:<state>"` and the matching `del`. That `OAuthFlowStore.consume` issues those two commands is a code reading, not a measurement: `consume` calls the cache's `get` and `delete` (`apps/api/src/auth/oauth/OAuthFlowStore.ts:44-45,49`), which call `redis.get(key)` and `redis.del(key)` (`packages/adapters/cache-redis/src/cache-manager.ts:167,331`), and the `put` at flow initiation writes the same key by the same mechanism, also not measured. The statement comes from `@opentelemetry/redis-common` 0.38.3 (`build/src/index.js:16-38`: `GET` and `DEL` serialize every argument, `SET` its key; serializer at `:47-58`), called by `@opentelemetry/instrumentation-ioredis` 0.67.0 (`build/src/instrumentation.js:81-96`). Where: `apps/api/src/auth/oauth/OAuthFlowStore.ts:13,30,44-45,49`. Done: not stated. Found by: otel-query-redaction.
- **OTEL-URL-PATH-CARRIES-TELEGRAM-TOKEN**: The Telegram Bot API takes the bot token in the URL path (`https://api.telegram.org/bot<token>/<method>`), and both callers send that URL through `fetch`, whose span records the whole path in `url.full` and `url.path`: the undici instrumentation builds both from the request's origin and path. The query redaction reads `name=value` parameters only and leaves a path as written. The same class holds the Slack and Teams incoming-webhook URLs of the external notifications: `ExternalNotificationDispatcher.send` hands the configured `webhookUrl` to `SlackNotifierAdapter.send` or `TeamsNotifierAdapter.send` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/ExternalNotificationDispatcher.ts:38-47`), each posts to it through the `HttpClientPort` (`SlackNotifierAdapter.ts:29,32`, `TeamsNotifierAdapter.ts:29,32`), which the container binds to `FetchHttpClient` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupServices.ts:350`, `setupExternalNotificationUseCases.ts:46-53`), and that calls `fetch(url, …)` (`FetchHttpClient.ts:40`, `post` at `:80-85`), so the undici span records the whole URL. That the secret of such a URL is in its path is the vendors' webhook format, not something this repository shows beyond the Slack-shaped fixture `https://hooks.slack.com/services/test` (`packages/core/external-notifications/tests/unit/ConfigureExternalNotificationUseCase.test.ts:39,69`). Read, not measured. Where: `packages/providers/telegram/src/apiClient.ts:151,153`, `packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:556,595,603`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/FetchHttpClient.ts:40,80-85`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/SlackNotifierAdapter.ts:29,32`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/adapters/TeamsNotifierAdapter.ts:29,32`. Done: not stated. Found by: otel-query-redaction.
- **NODE-26-13-1-AND-ZLIB-STAGE-OUT**: Closed by #898. Both stages of the four Dockerfiles (`apps/api`, `apps/workers`, `apps/admin`, `apps/client`) moved to Node 26.13.1, the security release of 2026-11-11 (`nodejs.org/dist/index.json` dates it 2026-11-10), whose sixteen CVEs include seven High, one a remotely reachable TLS server crash (CVE-2026-126051): the build stage to `node:26.13.1-bookworm-slim` at index `sha256:5df79989…`, and the runtime to the distroless `latest` index `sha256:80631bd2…`, both read on 2026-11-14 and run with `podman`, each running v26.13.1. That digest's own `var/lib/dpkg/status.d/zlib1g` reads `1:1.3.dfsg+really1.3.1-1+deb13u1`, the fix of DSA-6613-1 (the first digest that held it, `e2a8b92e`, had been uploaded on 2026-11-08T21:54:58Z; 2026-11-14 is the day this repository moved to a digest that holds it), so the `zlib-patched` stage and its `COPY` left the four Dockerfiles, and the watchlist entry `zlib-stage-remove` left with them. `engines.node` stays `>=26.12.1 <27`: CI's `setup-node` already resolved 26.13.1 from `.nvmrc` in every job that installs Node on `main` `af93f0fa`. Records: `docs/deployment/base-images.md` §"Moved to Node 26.13.1 on 2026-11-14" and §"zlib1g from Debian's archive on 2026-11-07", the paragraph of `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md` §"Ignored image CVEs", and the watchlist entry `contract-0.16-node-26`. Measured on 2026-11-14 with `podman` and Trivy 0.74.0 (database of 2026-11-14T01:05:40Z), the gate's flags: the four images built from the change each run `/nodejs/bin/node` v26.13.1 as `nonroot` and report `zlib1g` `1:1.3.dfsg+really1.3.1-1+deb13u1`; argon2 round-trips in the API image; both portals answer 200 on `/api/health` and `/` and pass `podman healthcheck run`; and Trivy exits 0 for all four, while the base image `456bd22a` exits 1 with two HIGH `zlib1g` findings (CVE-2026-76844, CVE-2026-85091) and `80631bd2` exits 0. 26.13.1 is inside the 7-day buffer until 2026-11-18T16:44Z; the Maintainer decided on 2026-11-15 to publish the change inside it, with its maturity reviewed on 2026-11-18 (the watchlist entry `contract-0.16-node-26`, due that day). Found by the maturity reviews of 2026-11-14.
- **CI-ARGON2-SMOKE-WORKDIR**: The argon2 step of the `Container Security` smoke runs the API image with `--workdir /app/apps/api`, a directory that does not exist in that image: the production stage copies the tree `pnpm deploy` wrote (`/prod/api`) to `/app` (`apps/api/Dockerfile:115,120`), so the step runs from an empty directory that only exists because Docker creates a missing `--workdir` silently. Podman refuses it (`workdir "/app/apps/api" does not exist on container`), and `require("argon2")` succeeds from `/app` in the same image. Read, and reproduced with podman by two agents on 2026-11-14. Where: `.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:386`. Done: not stated. Found by: node-26-13-1.
- **CI-NODE-RESOLVES-BELOW-SECURITY-RELEASE**: Closed by #925. ADR-0038 names the `engines.node` floor as the control "when CI must not resolve below it", and the floor had stayed `>=26.12.1 <27` while Node 26.13.1, the security release of 2026-11-11, was the version CI installed (the `CI` run of `main` that began on 2026-11-14T01:27Z). The floor was raised on 2026-11-18 by the Maintainer's decision: `engines.node` is `>=26.13.1 <27` in all 101 manifests, taken inside 26.13.1's 7-day buffer (published 2026-11-11T16:44:25Z, mature from 2026-11-18T16:44Z), with `.nvmrc` at `26` and the four Dockerfiles unchanged (ADR-0038 §"Amendment of 2026-11-18: the `engines.node` floor at 26.13.1"). The floor fails nothing by itself, so a run that resolves a 26.x below 26.13.1 still fails nothing; that residual is the row ENGINES-NODE-FLOOR-FAILS-NOTHING. Where: `package.json:10` and the 100 workspace manifests. Found by: node-26-13-1.
- **HIGH-MEMORY-ALERT-IS-A-HEAP-RATIO**: The error-level line "Performance alert triggered" with the rule "High Memory Usage", seen 12 s after the API boots, is not an out-of-memory condition. The rule fires when a request ends with `heapUsed / heapTotal` above 85%, and V8's `heapTotal` is the heap it has reserved so far, which starts small and grows on demand, so the ratio is high right after boot. A request is what evaluates the rule, health polls included, so a start command that polls `/health` can raise the line (once per 15-minute cooldown) and an idle API cannot; the 30 s timer only logs a warning. The alert says nothing about the memory the process may still use, and an operator reading it as a leak or a limit is misled. Where: `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts:112-121`, `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts:158`, `apps/api/src/monitoring/performanceMonitor.ts:277`, `apps/api/src/index.ts:571-579`. Done: a decision recorded on what the rule should measure (the heap against its configured limit, `v8.getHeapStatistics().heap_size_limit`, or the process's resident size, instead of the heap's current size), and the rule changed to it with a test that a young process with a small heap raises no alert. Found by: F-01 T3b2a, which measured the line 12 s after boot while its own polling was the only traffic.
- **START-API-PID-REUSE-ADOPTION**: The start command recognises the API it started earlier by the process id in `.tmp/api.pid` and a 200 from `/health` on the port. What `api.pid` holds is the pnpm process the run started to run the API (the leader of the session that holds the API), not the API, and a run reads it before it looks at the port. When the system has given that id to another live process and something else answers `/health` with 200 on the same port, the command reports that process as the API it started earlier, says that no second API was started, and prints a `kill -s TERM -- -<id>` line that names the foreign process. Measured by the independent verification of the start step. When a run's start fails (its wait runs out, or its pnpm ends first), the run sweeps the session of the pnpm it started itself and removes the record only when no process of that session is left, naming any that is; a process that leaves the session (one that calls `setsid`) is not swept, and once the record is removed it reads as unrecorded. The command never signals the recorded process (its only calls of `kill` on it are the existence check with signal 0, `exists` at `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1437`, which a run makes when it reads the record, on each poll of its wait and once more when that wait runs out; the `spawnSync` limits of the Docker query and of Prisma's proof send SIGTERM to those steps, as at the baseline), so the harm is a wrong claim and a stop instruction that points at the wrong process; the header of the command states the limit. A later stop mode of the command that reads the id from the file and ends its session would end a foreign session if the system had reused the id. Where: `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1419` (`recordedProcess`, which asks only whether the id exists), `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1811-1815` and `:1853` (the adoption), `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:146-195` (the header that states the limit). Done: the record holds something that identifies the process beyond its id (its start time, read from `/proc`), and the command refuses a record that does not match, with a case that plants a foreign process under the recorded id. The other half this row asked for, a record that is not removed while a process of its session survives, holds on both paths of a failed start: whether its wait runs out or its pnpm ends first, the record is removed only when the sweep finds that session empty (`scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1727`, `sweepSession`). Found by: F-01 T3b2a, by the independent verification of the stage it was cut from.
- **START-COMPOSE-FIXED-CONTAINER-NAMES**: Every service of `docker-compose.yml` has a fixed `container_name` (`omnipost-postgres`, `omnipost-redis`, `omnipost-grafana` and the rest), and container names are global to one Docker daemon, so the start sequence cannot run in a second checkout on one host. Measured: the second checkout's run prints Compose's own line naming the conflict (`Conflict. The container name "/omnipost-redis" is already in use by container ...`) and then the command's `docker compose up -d postgres redis exited 1`; the command's own line adds no cause. Two real runs of the start command in two checkouts, or a run beside a battery that has its own services up, collide on it. Where: `docker-compose.yml:7`, `docker-compose.yml:29`, `docker-compose.yml:42`, `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs` (the services step). Done: not decided; the options on record are that the names come from the Compose project, or that the command refuses by name when another checkout holds them. The compose file is outside the surface of the start steps and the choice is the Maintainer's. Found by: F-01 T3b2a, from the verification of the stage it was cut from.
- **API-BOOT-WRITES-A-SYSTEM-HEALTH-EVENT**: Closed by #914. Each API boot inserted a `system.health` row into `stored_events` (stream `EventService`): the verification of F-01 T3b2a measured 8 rows after about 8 boots, because `EventService.initialize()` published a `SYSTEM_HEALTH` event as its last step and the API's composition root calls it at every boot. The Product Owner decided on 2026-11-16 that the boot stops writing it (F-01 L37): `initialize()` now registers the default handlers and marks the service ready and appends and publishes nothing, and the case `apps/api/tests/unit/EventService.initialize.test.ts` holds that with the event store and the publisher replaced, beside a case that a caller can still publish once the service is initialized. So the database holds no row of the application after a boot (S6 as F-01 reads it), and the CI job of F-01 asserts that strictly.
