Where: `apps/api/src/events/EventService.ts:70-87` (`initialize`), `apps/api/src/index.ts:730` (`sagaEventService.initialize()`), `infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3623` (`StoredEvent`). Found by: the verification of F-01 T3b2a.
- **EVENT-SYSTEM-HEALTH-HAS-NO-EMITTER**: After API-BOOT-WRITES-A-SYSTEM-HEALTH-EVENT nothing emits `system.health`: the event type, its schema and the default handler that reads its status and does nothing with it remain, so a reader takes them for a live signal. By reading, not measured: no other file of `apps/` or `packages/` names `SYSTEM_HEALTH` or `system.health`. Where: `packages/shared/src/events.ts:257` (`EVENT_TYPES.SYSTEM_HEALTH`), `packages/shared/src/events.ts:322` (`SystemHealthEventSchema` in the schema map), `apps/api/src/events/EventService.ts:475-485` (the default handler). Done: not stated; the event type is a shared contract, so whether it leaves with its schema and handler is the decision. Found by: F-01 T7p.
- **BATTERY-STORIES-STEP-STALL-HAS-NO-USEFUL-LIMIT**: The battery's `stories` step (the client's `test:stories`, vitest in browser mode) stalled for 15 minutes with the browser idle and no test collected while other heavy work ran on the host on 2026-11-14 (02:16Z to 02:31Z), and the step's own limit is 3600 seconds, so a stalled run does not fail by itself in useful time. Cause not established. Where: `scripts/testing/battery.sh:251` (the `stories` step), `scripts/testing/battery.sh:331` (`STEP_TIMEOUT=${BATTERY_STEP_TIMEOUT:-3600}`), `scripts/testing/battery.sh:140` (the `timeout --kill-after=30 "$STEP_TIMEOUT"` that bounds each gate), `scripts/testing/battery.sh:22` (the documentation of that limit). Done: not stated; the stall is reproduced or ruled out first, and then either the step gets a limit of its own that fits the longest healthy run, or its collection is shown to stop stalling. Found by: the battery of the telemetry query redaction (#896).
- **MUTATION-RUNS-SHARE-THE-HOST-PID-NAMESPACE**: `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs` runs each mutated suite in the host's PID namespace, so a mutation of code that sends signals can make a suite signal -1 and end every process of the host. Measured on 2026-11-15: a mutation that wrote `1` into the start command's record `.tmp/api.pid` made the teardown of `platformStart.test.ts` run `process.kill(-1, "SIGKILL")`, which as root ended every process of the container but init; the journal holds seven such mass kills on 2026-11-14 and 2026-11-15, each during mutation runs of that suite. The suite's teardown now sends a signal only to a target that names a real, separate process; until the tool runs its suites in a namespace of their own, such mutations are run through a PID-namespace helper by hand. Where: `scripts/testing/hard-probe.mjs:248` (the `spawn` of `runGroup`, which `runTool` uses), `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs:924-928` (`spawnRun`, which hands each run to `runTool`), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts:590-748` (the suite's teardown: `afterEach`, `RECORDS`, `endRecorded`, `endRecordedApi`, `endRecordedWorkers`, `recordedApi`, `recordedWorkers`, `recordedIn`, `separate`, `signal`, `endGroup`). Done: not stated. Found by: F-01 T3b2a.
- **START-RECORD-READS-A-ZOMBIE-AS-LIVE**: The start command reads its record of the API's pnpm as live when `process.kill(pid, 0)` succeeds (`scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1439`), and that call succeeds on a zombie, a process that has ended and that its parent has not reaped yet. Measured on 2026-11-15 inside a PID namespace whose pid 1 reaped no orphan: a second run took the ended pnpm of the first for a live record, started nothing and waited the whole `--wait-seconds`. On a host whose init reaps at once the window is short, so the run can misread an API that has just ended; the suite's own `alive` reads the state in `/proc/<pid>/stat` and counts a zombie as ended (`apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts:707`). Where: `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1439`. Done: not stated. Found by: F-01 T3b2a.
- **START-SCRIPTS-NOT-TYPECHECKED**: No tsconfig covers `scripts/start/`, so no gate type-checks the start command, although its JSDoc declares types. A strict `tsc --checkJs` over it reports one error: `scripts/start/lib/start-environment.mjs:142` returns `process.env`'s `string | undefined` values as `Record<string, string>`. It reported three when T4a attached this row; the other two, `api` returned typed `number | null` where `number` is declared, were narrowed by T4b, whose problems check now tests the two ports it returns. Where: `scripts/start/`. Done: not stated: a tsconfig over `scripts/` that the typecheck runs with `checkJs`, or this one narrowed. Found by: F-01 T4a.
- **WORKERS-HEALTH-LISTEN-NO-ERROR-HANDLER**: The workers' health server calls `listen` with no `error` listener, after the publish and mention-ingest workers have started their consumers, so a port another process holds ends the workers through an uncaught `EADDRINUSE` instead of a refusal that names the port. The start command refuses a held port before it starts them, but a port taken between that check and the bind, and every other start path (`pnpm dev`, `dev:workers`, the image), meets the crash. By reading, not measured. Where: `apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts:250`. Done: Not stated. Found by: F-01 T4 design map (2026-11-15).
- **API-METRICS-PORT-UNREAD**: The API's environment schema declares `METRICS_PORT`, and nothing under `apps/api/src` reads it: the variable is the workers' port (`apps/workers/src/bootstrap.ts:139`), so the API's schema accepts and validates a setting the API never uses, and an operator reading that schema can take it for the API's own metrics port. Where: `apps/api/src/config/env.ts:172`. Done: Not stated. Found by: F-01 T4 design map (2026-11-15).
- **OTEL-PROMETHEUS-PORT-SHARED**: With tracing on in both, the API and the workers each start an OpenTelemetry Prometheus exporter on `PROMETHEUS_PORT`, else 9464 (`enablePrometheusExporter: true` in both presets), so on one host the second to start cannot bind it. - **PORTAL-API-URL-FALLBACKS-DEAD**: Three modules of the administration portal read `env.API_URL ?? env.NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL ?? "http://localhost:3000"` (one with `||`), but the portal's environment schema gives both variables a default of `http://localhost:3000` (`apps/admin/lib/env.ts:36`, `:52`), so `API_URL` is never absent where it is read: `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` is never consulted and the literal fallback is never reached. The sign-in and the required password change both send their request to that resolved address (`apps/admin/lib/auth/backend-client.ts:186`, `:254`), measured again on 2026-11-20 with the same chain at the three sites (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:15`, `apps/admin/app/api/auth/refresh/route.ts:24`) and the defaults at `apps/admin/lib/env.ts:36` and `:52`. The client portal repeats the literal at two more sites, against its own schema default (`apps/client/lib/env.ts:24`, `API_URL: urlString.default("http://localhost:3000")`): `apps/client/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:44` (`env.API_URL ?? "http://localhost:3000"`) and `apps/client/app/actions/auth.ts:23` (`env.API_URL || "http://localhost:3000"`), so five readers in the two portals repeat a fallback that cannot be reached. Dormant. Evidence added on 2026-11-20 by the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950). Done, added: each reader reads `env.API_URL` alone, and the literal appears only in the schemas, or nowhere once the class of DEF-100 lands.
- **CLIENT-INSTAGRAM-IMPORT-BYPASSES-EXPORTS**: Eight modules of the client portal import `@providers/instagram/src/mediaProcessor`. - **PORTAL-FAILURE-LINE-HIDES-NEXT-500**: When the start command's wait for the administration portal runs out, its line can say of `/en/login` that "nothing answered it" while the real page did answer, with 500: on a real Next.js 16.4.1 run with DEF-85 the first compile of `/en/login` answered 500 after 2.94 s, longer than the 2000 ms one attempt waits, and from then on `/api/health` answered 500 too, so the line names neither the 500 nor the page that caused it. Measured by the independent verification of T5. - **PREDEV-STALE-TSBUILDINFO**: The portal's `predev` builds its workspace dependencies with `tsc -b`, and a checkout whose `dist` was removed while the build info stayed is not rebuilt: the composite builds (`tsconfig.build.base.json:4`) write `tsconfig.build.tsbuildinfo` at the package root, beside `src`, not under `dist` (`packages/i18n/tsconfig.build.json:4-5`, `packages/shared/tsconfig.build.json:4-5`, `outDir` `./dist` and `rootDir` `./src`), so `tsc -b` finds the build info current, reports done and emits nothing, and DEF-85 persists after the predev fix. Where: `apps/admin/package.json:9`, `packages/i18n/package.json:22`, `packages/shared/package.json:22`. Done: not stated. Found by: F-01 T5's verification (2026-11-16).

- **GENAI-3-DEFAULT-API-VERSION**: The Product Owner approved taking `@google/genai` 3.0.0 in the weekly dependency window of 2026-11-17 (`apps/api` declares 1.20.0). The production pass's writer took 3.1.0, the newest release mature at 21:17Z (published 2026-11-10T20:47:12Z), and the family left the pass: from 3.0.0 the SDK's default Gemini Developer API version is `v1`, where it was `v1beta` ("BREAKING CHANGES" of the v3.0.0 release, 2026-11-06). The same notes say that the `gemini-flash-latest`, `gemini-flash-lite-latest` and `gemini-pro-latest` aliases and some `-preview` models exist only on `v1beta`, that the Live API and context caching (`caches`) are not available on `v1`, and that `apiVersion` (or `httpOptions.apiVersion`) passed to `GoogleGenAI`, or the environment variable `GOOGLE_GENAI_API_VERSION`, sets the version. The API's client sets none: `new GoogleGenAI({ apiKey: config.apiKey })` (`apps/api/src/ai/providers/gemini.ts:37`). Through it the API sends `generateContent` with `gemini-1.5-flash` as the default model (`:43`, `:73`, `:108`; the factory's default is `gemini-1.5-pro`, `apps/api/src/ai/AIProviderFactory.ts:21`, and the orchestrator passes `env.GEMINI_MODEL || "gemini-1.5-flash"`, `apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:158`), structured output with `responseMimeType` and `responseSchema` (`:113-114`), and `embedContent` with `gemini-embedding-001` and `outputDimensionality` (`:330-335`); whether each model and field is served the same way on `v1` was not measured. The settings' connection test lists models on `v1` through its own `fetch`, not the SDK (`packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:451`). The suites cannot see the change: their HTTP double matches any path of the host (`apps/api/tests/unit/ai/msw/aiWireServer.ts:24`), so a request to `v1` passes where one to `v1beta` did. Where: `apps/api/package.json:90`, `apps/api/src/ai/providers/gemini.ts:37`, `apps/api/tests/unit/ai/msw/aiWireServer.ts:24`. The Product Owner's rule, stated on 2026-11-17 when this was reported, decides the direction: no beta, alpha or release-candidate surface is allowed in the project, so the version the client names is `v1`, and setting `v1beta` back is not a way out; with 1.20.0 the client already calls `v1beta` today, by a default no search finds. The provider uses none of the features the versions page (https://aistudio.google.com/api-reference/api-versions) lists as `v1beta` only (the Live API, audio, explicit context caching, the Agents and Webhooks APIs), and that page lists structured output in both versions and does not name embeddings. The fallback model ids `gemini-1.5-flash` and `gemini-1.5-pro` are not on the models page (https://aistudio.google.com/docs/deprecations, whose oldest line is 2.5, deprecated with shutdown on 2026-12-01), and `GEMINI_MODEL` is optional (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:219`). Done: the client names `v1` explicitly, a test asserts the version in the request path, the fallback model ids are replaced with a generally available model the Product Owner chooses, structured output and the embedding call with `outputDimensionality` are proven on `v1` with one real request, and the bump lands as its own change; Dependabot's #756 (`@google/genai` 1.20.0 to 2.31.0) stays open until then. Found by: DEPS-WINDOW-2026-11-17.

- **PADDLE-3-11-FUTURE-WEBHOOK-TIMESTAMPS**: The production pass of the dependency window of 2026-11-17 took `@paddle/paddle-node-sdk` 3.11.1, the newest release mature then (published 2026-11-09T08:56:25Z; 3.12.0, published 2026-11-12T11:19:01Z, matures 2026-11-19T11:19Z), where `apps/api` declares 3.6.1, and the family left the pass. The 3.11.1 release names one change: "fix: reject future webhook timestamps, compare signatures in constant time" (PaddleHQ/paddle-node-sdk#263). In the installed 3.6.1, `WebhooksValidator.isValidSignature` returns false only when the current time is past the signature's `ts` plus `MAX_VALID_TIME_DIFFERENCE`, five seconds (`dist/cjs/notifications/helpers/webhooks-validator.js:38`), so it refuses a timestamp more than five seconds in the past and accepts any timestamp in the future, and it compares the HMAC with `===`; 3.11.1 compares `Math.abs(Date.now() - ts * 1000)` with the same five seconds, refuses a difference that is not finite, and compares the HMAC in a constant-time loop (the same file, `:38-43`). The API reaches it through `this.paddle.webhooks.unmarshal` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/billing/PaddlePaymentAdapter.ts:230`): with 3.11.1 a host whose clock runs more than five seconds behind Paddle's refuses every valid notification, which 3.6.1 accepted. The suite cannot see the change: its `sign` helper dates each signature at `Date.now()` (`apps/api/tests/unit/infrastructure/billing/PaddlePaymentAdapter.test.ts:60-64`) and the block over the SDK's real check pins the clock (`:115-147`), so no case signs a timestamp outside the window on either side, and the comment at `:120` states only the old half of the rule ("The SDK refuses a signature timestamp older than five seconds"). Where: `apps/api/package.json:101`, `apps/api/src/infrastructure/billing/PaddlePaymentAdapter.ts:230`, `apps/api/tests/unit/infrastructure/billing/PaddlePaymentAdapter.test.ts:120`, the Paddle row of `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:104`. Done: the bump lands as its own change, in the weekly dependency window of 2026-11-24, with a test that refuses a signature dated more than five seconds ahead, the test's comment states both halves of the rule, and the deployment checklist says the host's clock must be synchronised; Dependabot's #687 (3.6.1 to 3.12.0) stays open until then. Found by: DEPS-WINDOW-2026-11-17.
- **INSTRUCTION-SETTINGS-UNGUARDED**: The managed settings file, `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json`, which Claude Code reads at every start (its debug log names the path), is guarded by neither the pre-edit nor the pre-bash gate: `SENSITIVE_PATTERNS` names `/.claude/settings.json` and `/.claude/settings.local.json` (the project's, the local and the user's settings) and nothing under `/etc/claude-code/`. A `disableAllHooks: true` or an `env` block written there turns off every hook of the project, the guards of sensitive paths included; `disableAllHooks` would do it with no warning, and an `env` block carrying `MAINTAINER_DISABLE_INSTRUCTION_CHECK=yes` is announced only by the check's own warning. A `--settings` file or argument and the terminal's environment reach the hooks too, chosen by whoever launches the session. Where: `.claude/hooks-py/pre_edit.py:30-50`, `.claude/hooks-py/pre_bash.py:40` and `:216-269`. Done: not stated; the Maintainer decides whether the managed settings path joins the guarded list. Found by TOKEN-1 T2b's verification.

- **ENGINES-NODE-FLOOR-FAILS-NOTHING**: Nothing fails when a run resolves a Node 26.x below the `engines.node` floor, `>=26.13.1 <27` since #925. This repository sets no `engineStrict` (neither `.npmrc` nor `pnpm-workspace.yaml` names it), and pnpm documents that without it an `engines.node` that does not accept the running Node prints a warning and the install continues; on pnpm 12.6.0, `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline` under Node 26.12.1 exited 0 with no engines warning, both over the installed tree and after `packages/ports/node_modules` was removed, so that the install relinked it and ran the lifecycle scripts (measured 2026-11-18). `scripts/testing/engines-node-gate.mjs` holds every manifest to the root's value and to the runtime major, never the running patch release to the floor, and the shared setup-node step resolves `.nvmrc` (`26`) with no `check-latest`. So the floor raised by CI-NODE-RESOLVES-BELOW-SECURITY-RELEASE declares the security release and enforces nothing. Where: `package.json:10`, `.github/actions/setup-node-pnpm-cache/action.yml:20-23`, `scripts/testing/engines-node-gate.mjs:4-5`. Decided by the Maintainer on 2026-11-19: the runtime is pinned to one exact version. `.nvmrc` names the floor's exact version, as the images already do (`apps/api/Dockerfile:15`), and the engines gate holds three values to one number: `.nvmrc`, the floor of `engines.node` and the Node that runs the gate. A new Node release enters with a pull request. Not chosen: `check-latest`, which follows new releases by itself and leaves a local machine out, and the check alone with `.nvmrc` left at the major. Done: `.nvmrc` holds the exact version; `scripts/testing/engines-node-gate.mjs` fails when `.nvmrc`, the floor and `process.version` are not one version, with its red path proven. Found by: node-floor-and-inert-ignore-2026-11-18.

Please use a supported Node.js version.", because `prisma/preinstall/index.js` checks the majors 20, 22 and 24 only (measured 2026-11-18 under Node 26.12.1 and 26.13.1, with `prisma generate` succeeding after it). ADR-0038 §"Revisit if" names the case "Prisma, or a production dependency, states Node 26 unsupported, or fails on it", and no earlier review of the watchlist entry `contract-0.16-node-26` recorded the banner. npm's `latest` dist-tag is the prerelease 8.0.0-rc.34, whose `engines.node` is `^22.18.0 || ^24.11.0 || >=26.0.0`. Where: `infra/prisma/package.json:27,33`, `docs/technical/ADR-0038-node-26-ahead-of-lts.md` §"Revisit if". Done: not stated; the Maintainer decides what the statement means for the Node 26 runtime. Found by: node-floor-and-inert-ignore-2026-11-18 (the review of `contract-0.16-node-26`).
- **CACHE-DIVERGENCE-PROBE-EMPTY-RESTORE**: The scheduled workflow "Cache Divergence Probe" (`.github/workflows/cache-divergence.yml`, cron `17 4 * * *`; GitHub started the eight scheduled runs from 2026-11-10 to 2026-11-17 between 11:34Z and 13:34Z, seven to nine hours after that time, a delay this row does not explain) failed on `main` on 2026-11-17 (run 44093999523, started 13:34:01Z, commit `083c44cf`) and on 2026-11-10 (run 42818294704, started 12:44:16Z, commit `a72e0525`), and succeeded on each of the six days between. Both failures stop at the same step, "Gate on restored cache identity" of the job "Cached vs cold build comparison", with exit 1 and "Probe malfunction: no cache restored — the probe observed nothing": the read-only restore asked for `Linux-turbo-<hashFiles('pnpm-lock.yaml', 'turbo.json')>-<sha>` and its prefix and got "Cache not found for input keys". The step assumes that "a scheduled run on the default branch always has a predecessor cache entry from the last main push", and that entry had been saved: the `Build Check` job of the `CI` run of `main` for the same commit saved the exact key at 2026-11-17T00:07:08Z (run 43984818516) and at 2026-11-10T02:24:39Z (run 42742367628), 13.5 and 10.3 hours before the probe ran, and the key expression is the same in both files. So the entry was gone when the probe ran; why is not measured. The repository's Actions caches held 12,241,539,572 bytes in 187 entries on 2026-11-18T01:51Z (`gh api repos/edwardricardo/omni-post/actions/cache/usage`), and both failures fell on a Tuesday, the day of the weekly dependency window. Read again on 2026-11-20 with #962: the premise is corrected, since the gate no longer says a scheduled run always has a predecessor's entry and names the five ways its restore can be empty; the cause of the two failures is still not measured. Ruled out: a `Build Check` still running (the exact entry had been saved 13.5 and 10.3 hours earlier) and a changed `pnpm-lock.yaml` or `turbo.json` (neither failing commit changed them against its first parent). Left: every entry of main under that hash evicted (GitHub evicts in order of last access once a repository's caches pass their limit, and this repository held 12.2 GB), a cache version that differs, or a failure of the cache service. Where: `.github/workflows/cache-divergence.yml:110-149`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:643-666` (the step that restores and saves on `main`, and its read-only twin elsewhere). Done: the cause of the two empty restores is found, or the Maintainer records that it stays unexplained. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.
- **PNPM-PACKAGE-MANAGER-BEHIND-NO-GATE**: `package.json:12` pins `packageManager` `pnpm@12.6.0`, and no gate holds the field: `scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs` does not read `packageManager`, pnpm is not in `scripts/testing/toolchain-population.json`, and the watchlist entry `pnpm@12.6.0` has both its reviews recorded, `flagged`, the final one of 2026-10-05 counting four security fixes in 12.7.0. Read on 2026-11-18 (`pnpm view pnpm dist-tags time --json`): npm's `latest` is 12.21.0, published 2026-11-13T21:01:11Z and mature from 2026-11-20T21:01Z; 12.22.0, published 2026-11-17T20:01:11Z, carries the dist-tag `next-12` and is not `latest` (`latest` and `latest-12` both name 12.21.0); the newest mature release is 12.19.0 (published 2026-11-10T01:24:37Z, mature since 2026-11-17T01:24Z). The release notes from 12.7.0 to 12.21.0 (`https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/releases/tag/v<version>`) list these fixes under "Security": in 12.7.0, a `userAgent` set in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` no longer expands environment variables (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/issues/15415), a dependency's bin named like a system utility can no longer redirect the shims on Nix (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/issues/14883), manifests inside the store, cache, state or modules directories are no longer treated as workspace projects (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/issues/15033), and packages that run a lifecycle script are no longer hard-linked into the virtual store (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/issues/15483); 12.9.0 "carries a security fix for `pnpm login`"; 12.10.0 seven, among them "`pnpm install` now prevents dependency versions with path traversal from writing files outside the global virtual store"; 12.15.0 fails with `ERR_PNPM_REGISTRY_NAME_MISMATCH` when a registry returns metadata for another package; 12.16.0 fixes "memory exposure and allocation handling vulnerabilities when processing zstd-compressed data" (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/pull/17388) and signing arithmetic "that may leak the private key through timing"; 12.19.0 no longer prints credentials in the offline-install error for a missing tarball; and 12.20.0's bundled `node-gyp` no longer ships undici 6, "which security scanners flag for CVE-2026-84947" (https://github.com/pnpm/pnpm/issues/17941). None of the published security advisories of `pnpm/pnpm` covers 12.6.0 (`gh api repos/pnpm/pnpm/security-advisories`). Where: `package.json:12`, `scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs`, `scripts/testing/toolchain-population.json`, `scripts/testing/maturity-watchlist.json` (entry `pnpm@12.6.0`). Done: the Maintainer decides the bump as its own change, in the weekly dependency window of 2026-11-24, and a gate or the watchlist holds the field. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.

- **DEP-OUT-ANTHROPIC**: Withdrawn by the Product Owner on 2026-11-19. The removal had been approved on 2026-11-13 in one answer to nine removals of that day's dependency audit; asked about this one alone, the Product Owner said that Anthropic is one of the four initial AI providers: "they were the 4 initial ones, with the option of adding others later, but to start it was those 4 for everything". The SDK and the option to load an Anthropic key stay, and what is wrong is the routing, which is AI-ROUTING-LEAVES-ANTHROPIC-OUT in [areas.md](areas.md). The record of the removal as it stood on 2026-11-18 follows. The removal of `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, with the option to load an Anthropic key in the Admin and the client, which the Product Owner approved on 2026-11-13 with that day's dependency audit, is not in `main`. `apps/api/package.json:35` declares 0.104.1; `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json:242-248` keeps it on the ground `single-adapter`, decided on 2026-10-25; the provider is `apps/api/src/ai/providers/anthropic.ts`; `apps/api/src/settings/settingsSchemas.ts:30` accepts `anthropic` as an AI provider, and the Admin pool form lists `anthropicApiKey` and `anthropicModel` (`apps/admin/components/settings/constants.ts:51-52`). No AI request reaches the provider: the orchestrator routes every task type to OpenAI, Gemini and Perplexity alone (`apps/api/src/ai/orchestrator.ts:191-207`), and the own-key path reads the key of `preferredProvider ?? "openai"` (`packages/core/ai/src/AiRequestService.ts:65`), which no caller outside the tests sets; the one call that reaches Anthropic is the key test, `GET https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models` (`packages/core/settings/src/SettingsService.ts:447`). The removal was written on 2026-11-13 and not published: fitness #54 refused four rewritten test blocks whose rewrite only drops the provider's name from literal data, two read NO-GAIN and two UNDECIDED because their suites import types only, and that needs the Maintainer's decision; its mutation records were lost on 2026-11-15, so resuming runs `mutation-diff` again. The removal also decides what becomes of the Anthropic keys a database already holds (a Member's own key, the pool's two fields), which the product can no longer delete once the provider is gone. Where: `apps/api/package.json:35`, `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json:242-248`, `apps/api/src/ai/providers/anthropic.ts`, `apps/api/src/settings/settingsSchemas.ts:30`, `apps/admin/components/settings/constants.ts:51-52`. Done: nothing lands. The branch `workstream/deps-remove-3`, which holds the removal, is not published; the question it raised about fitness #54 is RECORD-COVERAGE-HAS-NO-CLASS-FOR-A-REWRITE-THE-TYPES-FORCE; no stored key is touched. Found by: the dependency audit of 2026-11-13, measured again on `main` by the review of open items of 2026-11-18.
- **DEP-AUDIT-RECLASSIFICATIONS-UNWRITTEN**: The dependency audit of 2026-11-13 recommends reclassifying 46 of its names: they stay, but their entry in `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json` does not say what they are used for. DEP-DECISIONS-2026-11-13 wrote 13 of them; 28 keep the entry decided on 2026-10-25, and 5 carry none, being at or above the real-site floor, where the check admits only a `remove` entry (`scripts/testing/lib/dependency-ledger-core.mjs:430-432`), the limit that row records for `pgvector` and `@opentelemetry/api` too. Decided on 2026-11-19 (the Maintainer, with the Product Owner's rule for what stays): the 28 entries are rewritten in one pull request, and each name is first measured for what it serves, not for whether it is imported: "rewrite them and really find out whether they are of any use or not, and make the same classification: if it is of use in something that serves the application it stays, otherwise they go". A name that serves nothing the application uses or plans is removed; a removal that would take an option away from a user, or that rests on what the product intends, is put to the Product Owner by itself first, as DEP-OUT-ANTHROPIC showed. The decisions of the same day enter with it: Anthropic is one of the four AI providers, the emoji picker and the template search are functions of the product, ten cheap replacements are approved and each is done by the first unit that touches its code, and `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` gets a row of its own. Planned for the weekly dependency window of 2026-11-24. Where: `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json`, `scripts/testing/lib/dependency-ledger-core.mjs:430-432`. Done: each of the 28 names carries a measured verdict and a true sentence of what it serves, or is removed; how a name at or above the floor is recorded follows the reformed measure, DEPS-LEDGER-COUNTS-IMPORT-SITES-NOT-USE. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.
- **HOOK-ITEMS-AWAIT-DECISION**: Three items of the hook layer's third pull request of 2026-10-02 were never built, and no recorded decision drops them. A review runner with a disposition guard that writes `dispositions-<lineage>.json`: no such file exists in the tree. One push detector shared by `pre_bash` and Stop, and a Stop hook that blocks a turn whose text pushes without a green battery for the tip: the detector is `gate_git_push_requires_token` (`.claude/hooks-py/pre_bash.py:438`), Stop holds no push logic, publication is gated by the push grants of `CLAUDE.md` ("Push grants"), and the section Gates of this file records "a battery verdict no hook reads". A rule inventory drawn from the feedback files, injected on four trigger kinds (prompt keywords, a Bash pattern, an edited path or content, the end of a turn): `.claude/hooks-py/user_prompt_submit.py:2` injects repository context and mentioned files only, and TOKEN-1's path-scoped rules under `.claude/rules/` load a rule when a governed file is touched. Where: `.claude/hooks-py/pre_bash.py:438`, `.claude/hooks-py/stop.py`, `.claude/hooks-py/user_prompt_submit.py:2`, `.claude/rules/`. Done: the Maintainer decides, item by item, whether the push grants, the native review or TOKEN-1's path-scoped rules supersede it; an item they do not supersede gets its own row. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.
- **TRACING-ENABLED-OPERATOR-VALUE-UNDECIDED**: `TRACING_ENABLED` defaults to `false` in the API and the workers (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:171`, `apps/workers/src/config/env.ts:79-81`), and the deployment checklist lists it with `false` (`docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:77`), but no row decides what the operator's environment runs. The value decides which observability rows apply: DEF-150 holds the measurement owed with it `true`, the OpenTelemetry SDK started before Sentry, and SENTRY-EVENT-QUERY-SECRETS measured that with the SDK registered first Sentry sends no span envelopes. Decided by the Product Owner on 2026-11-19: an operator's environment runs `false` until a tracing backend is chosen for production, and choosing one reopens the value; the requirement above, that each deployment can add an OTLP backend beside its error monitor and independently of it, makes that backend optional for a deployment. The error monitor is no part of the reason: the API starts Sentry only with a DSN read from the `MONITORING` platform credentials (`apps/api/src/index.ts:485-501`, `apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts:113-114`), so it is off until an administrator saves one, and no file under `apps/workers/src` mentions Sentry. The local platform is a separate decision of the same day, LOCAL-PLATFORM-RUNS-WITHOUT-TRACING-AND-TRUNCATES-ITS-LOGS. Where: `apps/api/src/config/env.ts:171`, `apps/workers/src/config/env.ts:79-81`, `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:77`. Done: the value the operator's environment runs is decided and recorded with its reason. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.
- **ROWS-WAIT-UNDER-CLOSED-F-01**: F-01 closed with #916 while 24 rows attached to its record were not closed, so they waited under a feature that no longer opened. By the Product Owner's decision of 2026-11-20, F-01 is in progress again, in the same pull request as the check, and closes again when its 24 rows are resolved. Its confirmed cut of 2026-11-11 left 18 of them under "Wait": DEF-44, DEF-14, DEF-146, DEF-145, DEF-15, DEF-29, DEF-100, DEF-22, DEF-19, DEF-98, DEF-102, CRED-1, N-CI-8 and TURBO-CACHE-RESTORED-DIST-UNVERIFIED "because the start passes without them", and SECRETS-ROTATION-GOLIVE, K8S-1, IMAGE-1 and DEF-160 "until the product goes to production". The other 6 are findings made while the feature was open, which the cut does not name: DB-UP-CHECKS-AN-EXTERNAL-HOST, START-ENV-URLS-UNCHECKED, WORKERS-DEV-PICKS-ITS-ENV-FILE-BY-NODE-ENV, WORKERS-LOG-LEVEL-READ-BEFORE-ENV-FILE, API-HEALTH-SLOW-RECOVERY-AFTER-REDIS-PAUSE and WORKERS-READY-FAILS-ON-A-SLOW-DATABASE. The plan's rule, "An old item is attached to its feature first; its viability is decided when the feature opens" ([PLAN.md](../PLAN.md), §Rules), does not say where a row goes when the feature it was attached to closes without it. A proposal, not the rule: each row attaches to the first open feature whose rows depend on it, which for DEF-29 is F-05, because DC-3 there goes after DEF-29's second pull request, and a row no open feature depends on becomes cross-cutting. Answered on 2026-11-20 by the Product Owner: no feature closes with open items. F-02 does not close while its record holds open items (22 on that day), and the rows of F-01 are decided and repaired after F-02's, so that nothing stays pending under either feature; a check refuses a feature marked closed while an item under its record is open. The check is fitness #56, `scripts/testing/plan-closure-gate.mjs` (`pnpm check:plan-closure`, [ADR-0046](../../technical/ADR-0046-feature-closes-with-no-open-item.md)), and it is green on this tree because F-01 is `in progress`: it would name these 24 rows under F-01 and exit 1 if F-01 were `closed`. A row closes when its text starts with one of the two forms, `Closed by #<n>` or `Closed by decision of the <Product Owner|Maintainer> on <YYYY-MM-DD>: <the decision>`, which carry no punctuation of their own; the rest of the row's text follows. Where: [chain.md](chain.md), F-01's "Attached (20)" and "Findings since the feature opened". Done: Each of the 24 rows listed here is closed, resolved in code or by a recorded decision, and F-01 is `closed` again in the chain table with the check green; the check that refuses a closed feature with an open item under its record exists and is green since 2026-11-20, so the row closes when the 24 rows are closed and F-01 is closed again. Found by: the review of open items of 2026-11-18.

- **MAIN-HOLDS-CODEQL-RESULTS-NOBODY-DISPOSED**: Measured by the parent on 2026-11-20 through the code-scanning API: the two CodeQL analyses of `main` at `dd69dedc` report 109 results each. Five of them, alerts #91, #93, #94, #170 and #626 (`js/insufficient-password-hash`, open since 2026-05-02, 2026-05-04 and 2026-09-13), were dismissed as false positives on 2026-11-20 by the Maintainer's decision, after the check of pull request #942 showed them again because a line it changed joined their path. A pull request's `CodeQL` check fails for an existing alert whenever the change adds a path to it, and that check is not among `main`'s required checks, while the two analysis jobs are (`CodeQL (javascript)` and `CodeQL (typescript)`). So the other results stay open with no disposition, and any change that touches one of their paths can be held by the check. Where: the code-scanning alerts of the repository (read through the API on 2026-11-20). Done: Every open result of `main` has a disposition, fixed or dismissed with its reason, and the count is held by a gate or read on a schedule. Found by: the publication of F-02 T3.2 (2026-11-20).
- **INSTRUCTION-BUDGET-LOCAL-FILES-HELD-BY-NO-CHECK**: The instruction-context gate holds the tracked auto-loaded set to its ceiling (135,623 bytes) and to the target of 250,000, which the Product Owner decided on 2026-11-18 covers the project's files a session loads. The local files and the feedback files of the main checkout, 88,177 bytes on 2026-11-18, are ignored by git, so the gate cannot read them, and the feedback files grow with every new rule: the sum, 223,800 bytes today, can pass 250,000 with every check green. - **FITNESS-INVENTORY-COUNTS-ANY-SEQUENCE-ENTRY**: Fitness #44 reads an entry of any sequence of the workflow as a step, not only an entry of a `steps:` list, so a numbered `name:` that is the own key of another list's entry (an entry of a matrix `include:`, say) is credited as a check no step runs. - **FITNESS-INVENTORY-CRLF-READS-ZERO-STEPS**: Fitness #44 reads a workflow with CRLF line endings as holding zero numbered steps, so it fails closed with a message that names no line ending. `.gitattributes:3` keeps the workflow at LF, so the case needs a checkout that ignores it. Where: `scripts/testing/fitness-inventory-gate.mjs` (`NAME_KEY`). Done: not stated. Found by: TOKEN-1 T5's verification (2026-11-18). A reference with CRLF line endings also fails closed, with 57 messages that say a line "opens like a line of the check list" and one that zero list lines were read, none naming a line ending (measured on 2026-11-25 by the context diet's verifier and again on a CRLF copy of the reference).
- **RECORD-GUARD-FAILS-ON-A-LONG-ARGUMENT**: The record guard judges a Bash command by probing each argument of an in-place writer as a path: `judge` asks `repository_of` for the argument's repository, which asks whether `<candidate>/.git` exists. An argument longer than the filesystem's name limit raises `OSError: [Errno 36] File name too long` there, and the guard, which fails closed, refuses the whole command and tells the Maintainer to start the session with the instruction check disabled. Measured on 2026-11-18 with `sd -F` and a replacement text of about 450 characters, on a file no record governs; the same edit passed through the Edit tool, which the guard judges by the file's path. It happened again on 2026-11-20: a Bash command with one argument of about 400 characters, a sentence, was refused with "The record guard failed (OSError: [Errno 36] File name too long: …/.git)"; the message does not show which segment of the command fed the word to the probe. It refused two more commands run for the central error system's first slice on 2026-11-20, one of them a `cp` and `sd` command that derived one probe file from another (the independent verifier's third pass), and was reported to the Maintainer that day. The path of the mechanism, read on 2026-11-20: `bash_write_targets` resolves every word after an in-place editor, or of a segment a coarse write pattern matches, as a path (`.claude/hooks-py/pre_tool_record_guard.py:550-551`), and so does a `-C` or `--dir` value of a pnpm segment (`:508`, `:527`); `judge_call` hands each target to `Judge.judge` (`:740-741`), which calls `repository_of`, whose `(candidate / ".git").exists()` is not guarded (`.claude/hooks-py/_common.py:610-613`); the only handler is the blanket `except Exception` of `main`, which refuses the call (`.claude/hooks-py/pre_tool_record_guard.py:808-813`), and the OSError handler of the manifest reads (`:605`) is not on that path. The suite of the hook names no case for a long argument (`.claude/hooks-py/tests/test_record_guard.py`, searched for "too long" and "ENAMETOOLONG"). Where: `.claude/hooks-py/pre_tool_record_guard.py:637-642`, `.claude/hooks-py/_common.py:611`. Done: not stated; an argument that cannot name a file is not probed as one, with its failing case. Found by: TOKEN-1 T5's publication (2026-11-18); again by the parent, while correcting a pull request's description (2026-11-20), and twice more during the central error system's first slice (2026-11-20).
- **LEDGER-REPOSITORY-HOOK-AT-ITS-TIME-LIMIT**: The block 'ledger.mjs over the repository' of the ledger's suite prepares in a `beforeAll` that runs the reach engine and the ledger over the whole repository, under `REPOSITORY_TIMEOUT = 60_000`. On a CI runner that hook runs close to its limit. On the run of `main` for the merge of #929 it timed out ('Hook timed out in 60000ms') and failed `Test Suite (shard 1)` while the other 5,603 tests of the shard passed; on the pull request's own run, the same tree, the file passed in 98.8 s for its 203 tests, and the rerun of the failed job passed. The hook's work grows with the number of test files. Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/ledger.test.ts:2700`, `:2707`. Done: not stated; the Maintainer decides between a limit that fits a CI runner and a cheaper hook. Found by: the runs of `main` after #929 (2026-11-18).
- **PLATFORM-START-SECOND-RUN-FAILED-ONCE-UNEXPLAINED**: One case of the platform-start suite failed once in a full battery and passed in the rerun on the same commit: 'fails at once, with the last lines of the log, when the API exits while the run waits, and a second run goes on from there', whose second run ended with code 1 where code 0 and an empty stderr were expected. Measured on 2026-11-19 in the battery of `2a770af4` on lane 1 (01:08:49Z to 01:44:00Z; 10,820 of 10,821 tests passed), while another battery ran on lane 2 for its first 25 minutes; the rerun, alone on the machine, was GREEN. That commit touched nothing the suite reads. The cause is not established: the test report keeps no stderr of the failing run. Two facts could explain it and neither is shown: the suite asks the system for a free port, releases it and binds it later, so another process can take it in between; and the second run waits a bounded time for `/health`, on a machine that was running two batteries. Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts:2219` (the case; the assertion that failed is at `:2244`), `:756-763` (`freePort`), `:834-842` (the ports of a staged checkout). Done: not stated; a first step is to make the failing run's stderr part of what the case reports. Found by: F-02 T1b's battery (2026-11-19). Seen again on 2026-11-26, in another case of the suite: the full battery of `170edb243` on lane 1 (07:01:34Z to 07:43:01Z; 11,984 of 11,985 tests of the step passed) failed 'hands the client portal the calling shell's API_URL when the shell sets one, and holds the run to the API it names' (`apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts:3423`; the assertion that failed is at `:3432`), with code 1 where code 0 and an empty stderr were expected. No other battery ran on the machine, and the rerun on the same commit was GREEN (07:44:01Z to 08:25:27Z; 11,985 of 11,985). That commit changed documents, one workflow step and the instruction manifest, nothing the suite reads. So the failure needs no second battery on the machine and is not bound to one case. Found by: the context diet, T3, its battery.

- **UOW-REGISTERED-SINGLETON**: The composition root registers the Unit of Work with its singleton flag set to `true`, so one `PrismaUnitOfWork` serves every resolution (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/setupRepositories.ts:215-220`, the flag at `:219`), while the architecture canon states the lifecycle as "repositories → singleton, use cases → singleton, UoW → **transient**" (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md:239`). - **DOMAIN-ERROR-PASSES-NO-CAUSE**: The constructor of `DomainError` passes only the message to `Error` (`packages/core/domain/src/errors/DomainError.ts:15-16`), and `toJSON` leaves any cause out (`:25-31`). The two errors of F-02 T2b that keep what the client raised each assign `cause` themselves after `super` (`packages/core/domain/src/errors/RepositoryFailureError.ts:38`, `packages/core/domain/src/repositories/SystemRoleRepository.ts:45`), through one function that decides what is kept of a raised value (`causeOfStoreFailure`, `packages/core/domain/src/errors/RepositoryFailureError.ts:49-55`). The use cases of F-02 hold their own function, shared by the two, that turns a raised value into an `Error` for a cause, with another message, beside `causeOfStoreFailure` (`asCause`, `packages/core/auth/src/singleUsePassword.ts:45-59`). Where: the lines above. Done: `DomainError` takes and exposes a cause, and its subclasses stop assigning it themselves. Found by: F-02 T2b's writer (2026-11-19).
- **REPOSITORY-STRING-CODES-DROP-THE-CAUSE**: Adapters beside `PrismaSystemRoleRepository` answer a failure of the client with a string code and keep neither what the client raised nor a log entry: a `} catch {` with no binding whose only statement is `return err("<CODE>")`. Measured on 2026-11-19 by a multi-line search of `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/`, there are 68 such blocks in 19 files, out of 69 `} catch {` with no binding in that directory. Three examples: `PrismaRoleManagementRepository.ts:85-86`, `PrismaDsarRequestRepository.ts:43-44` and `PrismaPlatformCredentialRepository.ts:54-55`, all under `apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/`. These blocks are not empty, so the zero tolerance of an empty catch does not name them (`docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md:104`), but the standard also says infrastructure errors are wrapped into domain or application errors before crossing layers (`:101`), and the string code carries none of the error. The new adapters return a `RepositoryFailureError` whose cause is the raised error (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/repositories/PrismaSystemRoleRepository.ts:89`). Where: the sites above. Done: Not stated beyond the decision whether these adapters keep the cause, which depends on DOMAIN-ERROR-PASSES-NO-CAUSE. Found by: F-02 T2b's writer (2026-11-19).
- **EMAIL-ADDRESS-HAS-THREE-RULES**: What an e-mail address is has no single owner. `normalizeEmail` only trims and lower-cases, and its own documentation says it is not a validator (`packages/core/domain/src/value-objects/EmailAddress.ts:39-43`). Two entities each hold a private copy of one loose pattern, `/^[^\s@]+@[^\s@]+\.[^\s@]+$/` (`packages/core/domain/src/entities/Account.ts:226-228`, `packages/core/domain/src/entities/CustomerUser.ts:92`, used at `:167`). The sign-in schemas validate with the schema library's `email()` (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthSchemas.ts:26`; `apps/api/src/security/inputValidation.ts:216-219`, which adds `max(320)` that the first lacks), whose pattern in the installed version, zod 4.6.7, is the library's own `email` pattern, in its `regexes` module. Read against that pattern, the loose one accepts what the sign-in refuses: a one-letter top-level domain (`a@b.c`), a non-ASCII local part, an empty segment in the local part (`a..b@x.com`, `.a@x.com`), a character outside the sign-in's local-part set (`a!@x.com`) and a domain label that starts with a hyphen (`a@-x.com`). F-02 T2c's writer ran both patterns on those five on 2026-11-19: each passed the loose pattern and failed the sign-in's, and `a@x.com` passed both. The use case of F-02 T2c asks the sign-in's rule through a port (`AdministratorSignInAddressRule`, `packages/ports/src/AdministratorSignInAddressRule.ts:11`), so it restates none. Where: the sites above. Done: One rule of what an address is, owned in one place and called by the others. Found by: F-02 T2c's writer (2026-11-19).
- **CORE-USE-CASES-IMPORT-NODE-CRYPTO**: Use cases of `packages/core` import Node's `crypto` to build ids, keys and tokens, for example `packages/core/auth/src/ConfigureOidcUseCase.ts:18` (`randomUUID`) and `packages/core/integrations/src/GenerateIntegrationApiKeyUseCase.ts:10` (`randomBytes`). A search on 2026-11-19 for `from "crypto"` and `from "node:crypto"` in the `.ts` files under `packages/core`, leaving out `node_modules`, `dist` and `tests`, finds 25 source files: 21 outside `packages/core/domain`, 19 of them named `*UseCase*`, and 4 in `packages/core/domain/src`. Fitness #2 does not read it: its command searches `packages/core` for `prisma`, `fastify`, `redis` and `bullmq` only (`docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md:28-29`). The architecture canon says that `domain/` imports nothing external, naming Prisma, Fastify, Redis, BullMQ and SDKs, and that `application/` imports domain, ports and the shared kernel only, with no concrete adapters and no infrastructure classes (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md:19-20`); it names no Node built-in. The use case of F-02 T2c imports none and asks its password from a port (`SingleUsePasswordGenerator`, `packages/ports/src/SingleUsePasswordGenerator.ts:11`). Where: the sites above. Done: Not stated beyond the decision whether the core may import Node built-ins, and the check that holds it. Found by: F-02 T2c's writer (2026-11-19).

- **VITEST-ALIAS-CANNOT-RESOLVE-INFRA-PRISMA-SUBPATHS**: The shared vitest configuration maps `@infra/prisma` to one file, the test-only entry `infra/prisma/src/vitest-entry.ts` (`packages/vitest-shared/src/index.ts:119-122`, in `buildWorkspaceAliases`, `:81`), and special-cases one subpath, `@infra/prisma/extensions` (`:121`); the API's configuration takes that map as it is (`apps/api/vitest.config.ts:36`). A string alias also matches the subpaths under it, so an import of any other `@infra/prisma/<subpath>.js` resolves to the entry file followed by a path segment, and it fails: F-02 T2d's writer measured it on 2026-11-19 with a probe (`ENOTDIR`), and the path join was measured again on 2026-11-19 (`ENOTDIR` on `infra/prisma/src/vitest-entry.ts/adminSystemRoles.js`). The package exports every subpath (`infra/prisma/package.json:48-51`), so Node resolves the same import. The first-administrator command's composition root imports `@infra/prisma/adminSystemRoles.js` (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/firstAdministratorComposition.ts:22`, and as a type `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/firstAdministratorRole.ts:11`), so no unit test can load it, and its proof is the integration file `apps/api/tests/integration/firstAdministratorCommand.integration.test.ts`. The test of the role's narrowing imports the definition by a relative path (`apps/api/tests/unit/adminSystemRoles.test.ts:14`) for the same reason. Where: the lines above. Done: The alias resolves any subpath the package exports. Found by: F-02 T2d's writer (2026-11-19).
- **IMAGE-IMPORT-CHECK-STOPS-AT-THE-ENV-REFUSAL**: The build step of `apps/api/Dockerfile` that imports the API's entry and, since F-02 T2d, the two modules that hold the first-administrator command's graph (`apps/api/Dockerfile:103-117`, its comment `:93-101`) proves that each module graph resolves from the deploy tree. A module that cannot resolve, or that raises `TypeError`, `ReferenceError` or `SyntaxError` while evaluating, fails the step; any other rejection counts as the normal case, named in the comment as a Zod env rejection or a refused connection (`apps/api/Dockerfile:86-88`, `:99-101`). Evaluation of a graph stops at the first module that throws, and the API's env module throws when the build stage's environment does not satisfy its schema (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:427-429`); it is the first import of the API's entry (`apps/api/src/index.ts:9`) and of the command's composition module (`apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/firstAdministratorProcess.ts:10`, before its imports of the database client and the composition at `:11-16`). A defect raised while evaluating a module past that point is therefore not seen, for the command's composition module and for the API's entry alike. The command's function module (`apps/api/src/cli/firstAdministratorCommand.ts:19-36`) imports no env module, so its graph is evaluated in full. Where: the lines above. Done: Not stated beyond the decision whether the step is given an environment that lets evaluation finish. Found by: F-02 T2d's writer (2026-11-19).
- **LOCAL-PLATFORM-RUNS-WITHOUT-TRACING-AND-TRUNCATES-ITS-LOGS**: The platform `pnpm start:platform` starts keeps no trace, and loses each process log at the next start. It starts Postgres and Redis only (`scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1008`), so the Jaeger service of the local stack (`docker-compose.yml:82-93`) is not running; it does not set `TRACING_ENABLED`, whose default is `false` (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:171`, `apps/workers/src/config/env.ts:79-81`), so the API and the workers create no span, although with it `true` they send to Jaeger's receiver by default (`docs/development/getting-started.md:90`); and it opens each process log with `"w+"` (`scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:1867`), so every start truncates `.tmp/api.log` and `.tmp/workers.log` (`:518`, `:545`). Until an error monitor is configured those logs are the only record of an error: the API starts Sentry only with a DSN an administrator has saved (`apps/api/src/index.ts:485-501`), and no file under `apps/workers/src` mentions Sentry. Decided by the Product Owner on 2026-11-19, under the requirement above (an OTLP backend beside the error monitor and independent of it): Jaeger is a service the local platform starts by default, as it starts Postgres and Redis, with tracing on, and the platform keeps its logs. Jaeger is turned off only by its own switch: configuring Sentry or GlitchTip does not turn it off, and making the two work at once is DEF-150. Where: the lines above. Done: `pnpm start:platform` starts Jaeger with Postgres and Redis and runs the API and the workers with tracing on, unless the switch says not to; Jaeger keeps its traces across a restart of its container, which is DEF-149; a start no longer discards the previous logs of the API and the workers; `docs/development/getting-started.md` says what the platform keeps, where, and how tracing is switched off. Found by: the decision round of 2026-11-19.
- **RECORD-COVERAGE-HAS-NO-CLASS-FOR-A-REWRITE-THE-TYPES-FORCE**: Fitness #54 refuses a rewritten test block whose base form no longer compiles against the new code. It lists, and never refuses, a rewrite whose base form fails at run time on the new code, the tool's BEHAVIOUR-CHANGED verdict, and it knows no class for one whose base form fails to compile (`docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, block 54, "WHAT FAILS": `failing-verdict`). Measured on the withdrawn removal of DEP-OUT-ANTHROPIC, on the unpublished branch `workstream/deps-remove-3`: four blocks of `apps/api/tests/unit/ai/providers/AICircuitBreaker.test.ts`, `apps/api/tests/unit/ai/providers/AIProviderError.test.ts` and `apps/api/tests/unit/ai/types.test.ts` only replace or drop the literal `"anthropic"`, which stops compiling once the name leaves `AIProviderName` (`apps/api/src/ai/providers/AICircuitBreaker.ts:62`, `:83`, `:99` take that type); their base forms still pass at run time, where the name is a key, so the removal's records of 2026-11-13, since lost, read NO-GAIN for two and UNDECIDED for two whose suite imports types only, and the gate refuses both verdicts. Any change that takes a name out of a typed list meets it. Where: `scripts/testing/lib/record-coverage-core.mjs`, `scripts/testing/mutation-diff.mjs`. Done: the Maintainer decides, when a change next meets it, whether the tool learns that class; no change waits on it today. Found by: the review of DEP-OUT-ANTHROPIC in the decision round of 2026-11-19.
- **DEPS-LEDGER-COUNTS-IMPORT-SITES-NOT-USE**: Fitness #50 justifies a direct dependency by counting the sites that import it. At or above the floor of 3 real sites (`floor` in `scripts/testing/dependency-decisions.json`) a name is justified by itself, and the check refuses any entry for it but `remove` (`scripts/testing/lib/dependency-ledger-core.mjs:430-432`); below the floor it asks for the Maintainer's decision. An import is not a use: the dependency audit of 2026-11-13 judged 8 names dead whatever their sites, and DEP-OUT-ANTHROPIC showed the opposite error, a provider the product plans judged removable because no request reached it. Decided on 2026-11-19 (the Maintainer, by the Product Owner's rule of the same day): the way a dependency is measured is reformed to that rule, "if it contributes to something that contributes to the application it stays". Where: `scripts/testing/dependency-ledger.mjs`, `scripts/testing/lib/dependency-ledger-core.mjs`, `scripts/testing/lib/dependency-sites.mjs`, `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md` (block 50). Done: the check holds every direct dependency to a measured answer to what it serves (a path the application runs, a tool the repository uses, or a plan row that names it) in place of the count of sites, and a name that serves none of them fails it; the design is written before the first change, and the changed check ships with its red path proven. Found by: the decision round of 2026-11-19.
- **SYSTEM-ACTOR-NAMES-NO-TASK**: Decided on 2026-11-19 (the Product Owner, on the Maintainer's assessment): the product has no system user. No row of `AdminUser` or of `CustomerUser` stands for the system; an action no person takes is attributed by its actor type and names the task that took it. What exists, measured on `main` `98556a7b`: the audit model holds the actor as a type, `SYSTEM`, `ADMIN` or `CUSTOMER`, with no user for the first (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:255-259`, `:265`), and an audit write that carries no user is a system row (`apps/api/src/services/audit.ts:48-49`); work that crosses tenants runs inside `withSystemContext`, which already takes its reason (`apps/api/src/security/tenantContext.ts:137`); and no file under `apps/workers/src` names an address of the API, so no process signs in to another. A user row for the system would cut against four things: the Product Owner's rule of 2026-11-12 that a migrated database holds no row of the application; the first-administrator command, which refuses when any administrator exists (`packages/core/auth/src/CreateFirstAdministratorUseCase.ts:180-181`); tenant isolation, since a customer user belongs to one Organization and the system crosses them; and the rules of access, since both user models require a password hash (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:176`, `:346`), so sign-in, sessions and permissions would each need an exception for it. What is missing: a system row of the audit log carries its action and its details, and no convention says where the task that wrote it is named; `DeletionRecord.deletedBy` is required (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma:3752`) and is written from the administrator who asks (`packages/core/accounts/src/HardDeleteAccountUseCase.ts:241`), so a deletion no person takes has no defined value; and one site passes the text `system` as a user id (`apps/api/src/webhooks/webhookDashboardService.ts:777`). The first two system rows that name their task exist since F-02 T3b: `recordSystemAction` (`packages/core/auth/src/singleUsePassword.ts:138-156`) writes a `SYSTEM` actor with no user, `AdminUser` as the resource, the administrator's id as the resource id and `{ task }` as the only detail, and its two callers are the creation (`FIRST_ADMINISTRATOR_CREATED`, task `create-first-admin`, `packages/core/auth/src/CreateFirstAdministratorUseCase.ts:39-42`, `:194`) and the re-issue (`ADMINISTRATOR_PASSWORD_REISSUED`, task `create-first-admin --reissue`, `packages/core/auth/src/ReissueAdministratorPasswordUseCase.ts:37-40`, `:203`), each inside its transaction. Where: the lines above. Done: an ADR writes the criterion into the canon; every system row of the audit log names its task, starting with the first-administrator command of F-02; a deletion no person takes has a defined `deletedBy`; the `system` user id of the webhook dashboard is gone. Found by: the decision round of 2026-11-19.
- **AUDIT-LOG-DETAILS-CLASSIFIED-FROM-ONE-WRITER**: What the product's audit writers put into `AuditLog.details`, a free JSON column (`infra/prisma/schema.prisma`, model `AuditLog`), is not inventoried, so the register classifies the column from one writer. The entry of `docs/legal/classification/personal-data.json` (`AuditLog.details`, added by hand, personal, `contact`, `admin-user`) rests on the audit rows of `apps/api/src/auth/authServiceCore.ts`, which write the administrator's e-mail into it, or the address a sign-in typed when no administrator has it (`registerAdmin` and the sign-in of `POST /auth/login`, each `details.email`); no other writer of the column was read. Where: `docs/legal/classification/personal-data.json`, `AuditLog.details`. Done: every writer of the column is read and the entry says what the column can hold. Found by: F-02 T5's writer, 2026-11-20.
- **COMPOSE-ENV-FILE-HANDS-EVERY-SECRET-TO-EVERY-CONTAINER**: Compose's `env_file: .env` passes every variable of the file into every container that declares it, so the development seed's administrator password, which `pnpm start:platform` now generates into that file, reaches containers that never read it. Where: `docker-compose.yml:9-11`, `:31-33`, `:44-46`, `:65-67`, `:86-88`, `:99-101`, `:122-124`, the seven services (`postgres`, `redis`, `grafana`, `prometheus`, `jaeger`, `minio`, `minio-init`). Done: Each service receives only the variables it reads, by `environment:` entries or by a file of its own. Found by F-02 T6 (2026-11-20).
- **START-ENVIRONMENT-MODULE-TYPE-ERROR**: `scripts/start/lib/start-environment.mjs` starts with `// @ts-check` (`:1`) and `readEnvironment` returns `{ ...parseEnv(text) }` typed `Record<string, string>` (`:169`), which the compiler refuses with TS2322 because `parseEnv` yields `string | undefined` values: reproduced on 2026-11-20 with `pnpm exec tsc --ignoreConfig --noEmit --allowJs --checkJs --skipLibCheck --target es2023 --module nodenext --moduleResolution nodenext --types node scripts/start/lib/start-environment.mjs`. No check reads the file: no tsconfig of the repository includes it (`rg -n 'scripts/start' tsconfig*.json apps/api/tsconfig*.json` finds nothing), so the error is found by nothing. Where: `scripts/start/lib/start-environment.mjs:1`, `:169`. Done: The type error is fixed and a check reads the file's types, or the `@ts-check` line leaves. Found by a writer of F-02 T6 (2026-11-20), reproduced by the documentation writer.
- **INTEGRATION-EVENT-CONSUMER-DISCARDS-WORKER-ERRORS**: The `error` listener of the integration-event consumer's worker discards the error and holds only a comment, so a connection error of that worker reaches no log. Dormant: no production code constructs `IntegrationEventConsumer`. Where: `apps/api/src/infrastructure/integration-events/IntegrationEventConsumer.ts:206-209`. Done: the listener logs through `createThrottledWorkerErrorListener` (`packages/observability/logger/src/throttledErrorListener.ts`) with a case that fails without it, or the class is deleted by a decision. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949).
- **UNTHROTTLED-CONNECTION-ERROR-LISTENERS**: Thirteen error listeners write every error they receive, so during a Redis outage each reconnection attempt writes one line, which the throttled helper of DEF-19 exists to prevent. Live, except `auditLogger`, which no production code imports. Where: `apps/workers/src/publishWorker.ts:181`, `apps/workers/src/mentionIngestWorker.ts:468`, `packages/adapters/fallback-strategies/src/index.ts:83`, `packages/adapters/cache-redis/src/cache-manager.ts:117`, `packages/adapters/queue-bullmq/src/consumer-adapter.ts:89`, `packages/adapters/db-prisma/src/cached.ts:153`, `packages/adapters/dead-letter-queue/src/index.ts:171`, `:175`, `:179`, `:199`, `apps/api/src/saga/SagaIntegration.ts:800`, `apps/api/src/events/EventPublisher.ts:451`, `apps/api/src/security/auditLogger.ts:76`. Done: each goes through the helper, or stays by a recorded decision, with a case per site that fails without it. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949).
- **DLQ-GET-FAILED-OPERATIONS-DATE-ON-JSON**: `getFailedOperations` sorts with `createdAt.getTime()` on job data that went through JSON, where `createdAt` is a string, so the sort probably throws (read, not run). Dormant: the method has no caller, though the API builds the manager (`apps/api/src/index.ts:426`, assigned to `_deadLetterQueue`). Where: `packages/adapters/dead-letter-queue/src/index.ts:519`. Done: a case with JSON-serialized job data passes, or the method is deleted. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949), reported and confirmed by reading, not run.
- **ADR-0013-WORKERS-INLINE-PINO-STALE**: ADR-0013 says the workers build `pino()` inline in their entry files, while every worker logs through `createLogger` and the workers row of the logging canon says so. Live (documentation). Where: `docs/technical/ADR-0013-three-logger-factory-model.md:28`, `:56`, `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md:21`. Done: both lines of the ADR match the workers row of the canon. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949).
- **LOG-REDACTION-COMMAND-ARGS-SHAPES**: The redaction of `command.args` covers an error at three levels (`*.command.args`, `*.*.command.args`, `*.*.*.command.args`), so these shapes still write the arguments of a failed Redis command, which on an AUTH failure hold the password: an error four levels down (`{a:{b:{c:{err}}}}`), an array of errors two levels down (`{a:{ctx:{errors:[err]}}}`), and an error passed as a `%o` or `%j` message argument. Dormant: no production call uses these shapes. Security: a leak of a credential into the log, shaped by the caller. Measured on 2026-11-20 with pino's `redact` and the list's paths, without the logger's serializers; the verifier also listed an error nested three levels and an `AggregateError` under `err`, which the third path covers on this tree (`packages/observability/logger/tests/redaction.test.ts:176`). It narrows the class of REDACT-NESTED-AND-VARIANT-NAMES. Where: `packages/observability/logger/src/index.ts:85-87`. Done: a case covers each shape, redacted or refused by a lint rule. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949).
- **LISTENER-TEST-PINS-THE-FIRST-CONNECTION-NOT-THE-SERVICE**: The suite takes the first connection a factory creates and checks that its listener throttles; it does not check that the service the factory returns holds that connection. The role connections and the gateway switch connection are pinned; the `setupServices` sites are not. Live (a test gap). Where: `apps/api/tests/unit/infrastructure/container/redisConnectionErrorListeners.test.ts:108-114`. Done: each `setupServices` case asserts that the service received that same instance. Found by: the independent verification of F-01's listener repairs (#949); it is also finding `R3-untested-setupServices-sites` of review `review-efdf3d857a9e7aff`.
- **INDEX-TS-LINE-CITATIONS-STALE**: Documents cite lines of `apps/api/src/index.ts` that no longer hold their target. Live (documentation). Where: `docs/reports/review-suggestions-book.md:1742` cites `index.ts:595` for the registration of the administrators' routes, which is `typedApp.register(adminAuthRoutes)` at `apps/api/src/index.ts:614`; `docs/technical/ADR-0033-layer-boundaries-and-composition-root-injection.md:288` cites `index.ts:567-577` for the `healthRoutes` registration, which is at `:586-596`; `docs/features/principal-derived-authority/explore.md:33` cites `index.ts:508` for the bare registration of `activityFeedRoutes`, which is at `:604`; `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md:244` cites `index.ts:596` for the webhook dashboard, whose routes are imported at `:692`, and line 596 now closes the `healthRoutes` registration. Done: each citation names a symbol, or the line that holds its target. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **SOURCE-SCAN-REGEX-COMMENT-OPENER**: `blankComments` treats a `/*` or a `//` inside a regular-expression literal as the start of a comment: it blanks the code up to the next `*/`, or to the end of the line, because it tracks quotes and not regular expressions. The scanner is shared by the legal and support inventories (`scripts/legal/oauth-scopes.mjs`, `scripts/support/non-features.mjs`) and by `apps/api/tests/unit/security/noCorsHeaders.test.ts`, whose header lists the limit. Dormant: no such regular expression is known in `apps/api/src` today. Where: `scripts/legal/lib/source-scan.mjs:56`, the branch at `:65`. Done: a test with `/^\/*$/` followed by real code shows that the code is still scanned, and the scanner skips a regular-expression literal. Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **RECORD-COVERAGE-CLASS-BY-IMPORT**: Fitness #54 lists and never refuses a deleted test block whose test imports a changed source, whatever the block tested: the class `behaviour change` is decided by the import alone. In the CORS removal 19 deleted blocks passed on that ground alone, as the independent verifier counted (reported, not recounted by reading). Live. Where: `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, block 54, "WHAT FAILS" ("behaviour-change blocks are listed, never refused"), `scripts/testing/record-coverage-gate.mjs`. Done: such a deletion needs a committed record, or the gate shows that the block's own subject changed. Related: RECORD-COVERAGE-HAS-NO-CLASS-FOR-A-REWRITE-THE-TYPES-FORCE (the rewrite side of the same class). Found by: the independent verification of the CORS removal (#950).
- **BATTERY-VERDICT-NUMERIC-WARN-RECORD-READ-AS-A-WARNING**: Closed by #952. The local battery's verdict read an application record at level warn as a warning line when pino wrote the level as its number, the spelling of every logger that sets no level formatter, and the record's message held a warning word: the verdict's header named that record as the one line that is no warning, and its code knew only the label spelling (`"level":"warn"`). Two runs of #952's battery were RED on twelve such lines with every step at exit 0, the health manager's alert line (`packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/index.ts:551`), which the unit's cases drive. A line that starts with `{"level":40,` and parses as one JSON object became that record, counted per message; eight cases hold what is still read whole. Found by: the local battery of #952.
- **POOL-CHECKER-ALWAYS-HEALTHY**: `DatabaseConnectionPoolHealthChecker` can only report healthy, because `RepoPort` exposes no pool counts: its three counts are constants of 0 and its utilization is 0 (`packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/checkers/database.ts:75`, the doc of `check` at `:82-83`). Nothing registers it (the only other mention is the export, `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/index.ts:608`), yet `docs/reports/UNUSED_CODE_INVENTORY.md:549` calls it "a one-line registration with real signal" and `:867` lists registering it among two one-line wire-ups. Dormant. Done: the class is deleted, or the port exposes the counts and a test sees it report degraded; either way the two inventory rows are corrected. Found by: the independent verification of the health repairs (#952).
- **HEALTH-ALERT-THRESHOLDS-HAVE-NO-READER**: Two of the manager's alert thresholds have no reader: `alertThresholds.degradedLatency` and `criticalFailureCount` (`packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/types.ts:61`, `:63`; their values at `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/index.ts:64`, `:66`). The one that is read, `unhealthyLatency`, equals the 5 s timeout (`index.ts:65`), and the high-latency alert raises only for a dependency whose status is `healthy` and whose latency exceeds it (`index.ts:510-513`): a check that takes longer than the timeout is ended by it first and reads unhealthy, so the alert cannot fire. Dormant. Done: each threshold has a reader with a test, or leaves the type; the alert fires in a test, or leaves. Found by: the unit's writer, while removing an unreachable branch of the database checker (#952).
- **PLATFORM-START-SUITE-FAILS-UNDER-CONCURRENT-RUNS**: One case of `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/platformStart.test.ts` ("the workers stage hands the workers the calling shell's environment, without the variables the file owns") failed once in a full local battery, on 2026-11-20 at 08:35Z on the tip of #950, a change that does not touch the suite: the start the case spawns exited 1 where 0 was expected. The case passes alone on the same tree in about 2 s and passed in the battery's second run. When it failed, three copies of the suite were running on the machine: two battery lanes and one more in a writer's worktree. The cause is not established, because the battery's report keeps the assertion and not the spawned start's output. Found by: the full battery of the CORS removal (#950). Done: the cause is found (how the suite's stand-ins get their ports, or a wait whose budget a loaded machine exceeds), the suite passes with three copies running at once, and a failing case's report holds the spawned process's standard error.
- **BATTERY-VERDICT-READS-A-TEST-TITLE-AS-A-WARNING**: Closed by #955. The static battery went red with every step at exit 0 because the record-coverage gate (fitness #54) listed a deleted block titled "should log operational errors as warnings", and the battery's verdict read any line holding `warn`, `warns`, `warning` or `warnings` as a warning line, so a change that deleted or rewrote any of the 66 blocks of 15,562 whose title held such a word (`ledger.json` at `2aacdc2e`) met a red its author could not fix. The verdict now reads a line of `record-coverage.log` on which the gate lists a block without the block's titles, by the gate's own line grammar, and reads the rest of that line, every other line of that log and every other log as before. Where: `scripts/testing/battery-verdict.mjs` (`warningTextOf`), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/batteryVerdict.test.ts`. Found by: the static battery of the central error system's branch, 2026-11-20.
- **BATTERY-VERDICT-APP-WARN-RECORD-EXEMPTS-THE-WHOLE-LINE**: Closed by #986. A line of any log that held the text `"level":"warn"` of an application record was counted as that record and never read as a warning line, so a tool's warning on the same line passed: `warning: x {"level":"warn"}` in a step's log was GREEN (measured on 2026-11-20). The verdict now takes a line for an application record only when, without its escape sequences (a record a test writes under a vitest console header opens on the header's closing ones), it starts with the level, in either of pino's spellings (`{"level":"warn",`, `{"level":40,`), and parses as one JSON object, the rule #952 gave the number; a line that only holds a record, with another tool's text before or after it, or that is cut, is read whole. Every record in the 657 local battery runs kept on 2026-11-25 is a whole line, so none of them is read anew. Where: `scripts/testing/battery-verdict.mjs` (`isAppWarnRecord`), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/batteryVerdict.test.ts`. Found by: the independent verification of the battery verdict's title exception (#955).
- **BATTERY-VERDICT-COLOURED-WARNING-NOT-READ**: Closed by #986. A warning word written right after an ANSI colour sequence was not read, because the sequence's final `m` is a word character beside it and the path-neighbour guard took the word for part of a name: `\u001b[33mwarning\u001b[39m: x` in a step's log was GREEN (measured on 2026-11-20). Live for any tool that colours its output into a log: ESLint's stylish formatter colours a row's severity that way, and vitest opens the first line of a test's console output on the closing sequences of the header above it. The verdict now reads each line as a terminal shows it, with its escape sequences removed by Node's `stripVTControlCharacters`, and judges that text by the same word rule, so a coloured path is still read as a path. No line of the 657 local battery runs kept on 2026-11-25 is read anew. Where: `scripts/testing/battery-verdict.mjs` (`scanLogs`), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/batteryVerdict.test.ts`. Found by: the independent verification of the battery verdict's title exception (#955).
- **BATTERY-VERDICT-READS-A-SLOW-TEST-TITLE-AS-A-WARNING**: Closed by #986. Vitest writes the title of a test slower than its threshold, and of a failed one, into the step's log, and the battery's verdict read a title holding `warn`, `warns`, `warning` or `warnings` as a word as a warning line, so the battery went RED with every step at exit 0: measured on 2026-11-21 in the work of #965, whose author renamed 13 titles in `01ebc0a90` to get past it. At `0d6f8d608` 62 test blocks in 31 files held such a word in their title, 60 of them in the 29 files vitest runs (the others are a Playwright spec and a node:test suite), counted from the test ledger's block ids, one per block with its file before `::`: `git show 0d6f8d608:ledger.json | jq -r '.files[].blocks[]?.id' | rg '::.*\b(warn|warns|warning|warnings)\b'`. The verdict now reads a test line, a console header and a failure header of a log after vitest's banner without the test's name, by vitest's line grammar, and reads the rest of each line, every other line of that log and every other log as before; the api runs' count of `Worker exited` and `Unhandled Error` lines reads the same text, so a test named after either is not counted. Over the 657 local battery runs kept on 2026-11-25 one verdict changes, from RED to GREEN: the run of #965's work, on the two lines of that title. No other runner writes a title on a line of a step that can be green: node:test's reach the integration tier's log only in the output of a suite file that failed, and no step runs Playwright's runner. Where: `scripts/testing/battery-verdict.mjs` (`warningTextOf`), `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/batteryVerdict.test.ts`. Found by: the full battery of #965's work, 2026-11-21.
- **RECORD-COVERAGE-GATE-DROPS-GIT-WARNINGS**: The record-coverage gate runs git with its standard error piped and returns only the standard output of a successful call, so a warning git writes on a call that succeeds reaches no log. Dormant: no such warning has been observed. Where: `scripts/testing/record-coverage-gate.mjs:126-132`. Done: the gate writes git's standard error of a successful call to its own standard error, held by a case, or the Maintainer records why it drops it. Found by: the independent verification of the battery verdict's title exception (#955).
- **LEDGER-SUITE-REPOSITORY-RUN-OUTGROWS-ITS-BUDGET**: Closed by #956. The `beforeAll` of the repository-level block of `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/ledger.test.ts` ("ledger.mjs over the repository") ran the reach engine and a full ledger over the tree under a fixed budget of 60 s, and both costs grow with the tree. On `main` the hook timed out twice on 2026-11-20 in the `CI` job `Test Suite (shard 1)` (commits `55fecbe5` and `c8c10535`); in the two green runs before the second, the whole file took 70 s and 102 s on the runner, against 31 to 36 s locally. The budget became 180 s, sized for the runner. The sibling budget of `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/dependencyLedger.test.ts` was measured and left as it is: its whole file takes 38 s on the runner. Residual limit: the budget is still a fixed figure on a cost that grows with the tree; when it is met again, the failure names the hook. Found by: the runs on `main` after the merge of #950.
- **LEDGER-OVERLAY-CONFIRMS-A-ROW-ON-A-PARTIAL-BLOCK-LIST**: The script that reads the test ledger's adjudications checks that every block an entry lists belongs to its row (`scripts/testing/lib/ledger-overlay.mjs:192-194`) and not that the entry lists every block of the row, so an entry that names only some of them still confirms the whole row: measured on 2026-11-20, an entry with one block dropped gave no problem. A row can then read as adjudicated while one of its blocks was never read. Dormant: the entries of `docs/development/testing-refoundation/adjudications.json` name every block of their rows. Done: the overlay refuses an entry whose blocks are not exactly its row's, held by a case. Found by: the independent verification of the first administrator's adjudications (F-02).
- **LEDGER-LABELS-PLAYWRIGHT-SPECS-AS-VITEST**: The test ledger records `"framework": "vitest"` for every Playwright spec.
