The classifier's `Framework` is `"vitest" | "node"` and a file is `vitest` unless it imports a `node:test` module, and `@playwright/test` is not one, so a spec that Playwright runs is labelled as one that Vitest runs, while its row's `collectors` field names the Playwright configuration. Measured on 2026-11-20 on the journey's row and on the four older specs of `apps/admin/tests/e2e`. Live, in the label. Where: `ledger.json:475` (the journey), `:660`, `:684`, `:841`, `:925` (the `a11y`, `auth`, `content-flow` and `smoke` specs), `packages/eslint-plugin-testing/src/blocks.js:33`, `:128`, `:138`. Done: the classifier names Playwright as a framework of its own, the ledger regenerates with the label on those five rows, and a case holds that a file importing `@playwright/test` is labelled so. Found by: the native review of F-02 T6b-2 (2026-11-20; row SB-803 of the suggestions book).
- **NEXT-DEV-LOGS-EACH-SERVER-ACTION-WITH-ITS-ARGUMENTS**: In development Next 16 writes a line to the portal's log for each call of a server action, with the action's arguments: on the first administrator's journey the sign-in appears as `loginAction(null, {})`, a `FormData` printing as `{}` (measured on 2026-11-20, no password in the log). An action that took a plain object holding a password would print it, and the CI job `Platform Start` prints the portal's log, in a step that runs `if: always()`. Dormant: the two actions the journey calls take `FormData`. Where: `apps/admin/app/actions/auth.ts:63-66` and `:149-152` (the two actions), `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1120-1134` (the step that prints the logs). Done: an action of either portal that takes a password never takes it as a plain argument, held by a rule or a case over the actions' signatures, or the logs the job prints carry no argument of an action's call; which of the two is decided when the feature that owns the portal's sign-in opens. Found by: the independent verification of F-02 T6b-2 (2026-11-20).
- **FIRST-ADMINISTRATOR-SUITE-QUOTES-TWO-COMMAND-LINES-BY-HAND**: The integration suite of the first administrator's command builds the command line of two cases by wrapping each word in single quotes with `.map((word) => `'${word}'`).join(" ")`, which breaks for a word that holds a single quote, while the shared runner now exports `commandLine`, which quotes every word whatever it holds (`packages/vitest-shared/src/pseudoTerminal.ts:55`). Dormant: the words of both lines are the Node path, fixed flags and the address `first.admin@example.com`. Where: `apps/api/tests/integration/firstAdministratorCommand.integration.test.ts:727` and `:1099`. Done: both lines call `commandLine`, with the mutation-diff record fitness #54 asks for a rewritten block, which is why the lines stayed inside their existing blocks when the runner moved. Found by: F-02 T6b-2, the change that made the shared runner export `commandLine` (2026-11-20).
- **TEST-AND-BUILD-MEETS-ITS-TIME-LIMIT**: Closed by #960. The job `Test and Build` of `Production CI/CD` ran the API's whole suite in one piece whenever a change touched `apps/api`, with nothing replayed from the cache, under a limit of 30 minutes. On `main` on 2026-11-20 it took 27 min 04 s (`c8c10535`) and 29 min 34 s (`5957a8d2`), and was cancelled at 30 min 15 s on `fc62355a` with every step green until the limit (type checking 4 min 46 s, tests 20 min 59 s, build 1 min 19 s); with the cache it took about six minutes. By decision of the Maintainer on 2026-11-20 the limit became 45 minutes (`.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:149`). The job is the duplicate that item 3.5 of this part removes, and until it does nothing bounds the suite's growth against the new limit. Found by: the runs of `main` that followed #955.
- **HEALTH-OVERALL-HIDES-A-DEGRADED-DEPENDENCY**: A report's overall status is `degraded` only when more than 30% of its dependencies are degraded or their average score is under 0.7, and `unhealthy` only when a critical one is, so one degraded or unhealthy dependency among the API's six reads `healthy` overall: computed on 2026-11-20 with the API's registrations, a degraded database gives 0.917, an unhealthy storage 0.833, both 0.750, all `healthy`, while the workers, with three, read `degraded` for one degraded dependency. Every reader of the overall status sees nothing of it: `/health` of both applications, the Docker `HEALTHCHECK` that asks it, and the overall word of `/health/detailed`. `pnpm start:platform` names each dependency that is not healthy whatever the overall status says (F-01's START-PASSES-SILENTLY-WITH-A-DEGRADED-APP in [chain.md](chain.md)), by decision of the Product Owner on 2026-11-20, which left the scoring as it is. Live. Where: `packages/monitoring/health-checks/src/index.ts:448-485` (`calculateOverallHealth`; the 30% and 0.7 at `:480`), `apps/api/src/health/healthRoutes.ts:59-87` (the six registrations, three of them not critical). Done: the Product Owner decides whether the overall status changes so that one degraded dependency reads degraded (a change of `/health` for everything that reads it, a unit of its own) or stays as it is; if it changes, the health manager's tests hold the new rule for both applications' registrations. Found by: the independent verification of F-01 T8 (2026-11-20).
- **HEALTH-ROUTES-WAIT-ON-THE-RATE-LIMITER**: The API's HTTP rate limit is a global pre-handler with a rule for `/health`, so every health route, `/health/live` included, first asks Redis for a token: one `eval` on a connection whose commands are cut at 5 s, and the request goes on when that call fails. A slow Redis therefore delays the liveness and readiness probes and the detailed report by up to 5 s before any check runs; for the start's read of `/health/detailed`, which waits 10 s of silence, a Redis close to 5 s a call can outlast that bound, and the run then says that nothing answered the report instead of naming redis. Read, not run. Live while `ENABLE_RATE_LIMITING` is on, its default. Where: `apps/api/src/index.ts:515-529` (the hook, before the health routes are registered at `:585-586`), `apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:90` (the `/health` rule), `:172` (the call), `:190-194` (the call that fails lets the request through), `apps/api/src/lib/redis.ts:64` (the 5 s command timeout), `scripts/start/platform-start.mjs:345-357` (the start's bound and its stated limit). Done: the health routes do not wait on the limiter, or a recorded decision keeps them behind it with the delay bounded and stated, held by a case either way. Found by: the independent verification of F-01 T8 (2026-11-20).
- **TURBO-MAIN-COLD-BUILD-LATENCY-UNMEASURED**: Closed by #965. Since #962 a first attempt on `main` restores no turbo cache in the jobs `Lint and Format Check`, `Build Check` and `Package Tests`, so each builds its dependency closure from source. What that costs was measured on pushes to `main`, each job from its own start to its end, read with `gh run view <id> --json jobs`. Before #962, with a restore by prefix: #959 (`ec0fd65d`) Lint 5 min 05 s, Build Check 3 min 24 s, Package Tests 3 min 35 s; #960 (`e6043eca`) 5 min 26 s, 1 min 24 s and 2 min 25 s; #961 (`53367c34`) 4 min 26 s, 1 min 36 s and 3 min 38 s. After, with an exact match only, on the first push to `main`, #962 (`62f6dee2`): 4 min 58 s, 3 min 14 s and 3 min 44 s. `Build Check` took about a minute and a half more than its two fastest runs before and as long as its slowest; Lint and Package Tests stayed inside the spread they already had; each is far inside its limit: 15 minutes for Lint (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:21-23`, where a fully cold run is on record at 6 min 56 s), 10 for Build Check (`ci.yml:634`) and 15 for Package Tests (`ci.yml:853`). One run after the change is one run: it shows no trend. Where: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:74`, `:661`, `:871`. Found by: the native review of the turbo cache unit (2026-11-20; row SB-812 of the suggestions book).
- **PULL-REQUEST-CACHES-EVICT-THE-CACHE-OF-MAIN**: Closed by #983. Every pull request saved its own caches. An entry a pull request saves can be read only by that pull request, so it counts against the repository's cache limit and buys `main` nothing. On 2026-11-24 the repository's Actions caches held 10.61 GB against GitHub's 10 GB limit: Docker layers saved by pull requests 6.63 GB (110 entries), pnpm stores saved by pull requests 2.86 GB (6 entries, one per lockfile hash), and everything `main` saved 1.07 GB. Ten Dependabot pull requests (#970 to #979) were open at once. GitHub evicted `main`'s turbo entry, and the scheduled `Cache Divergence Probe` (run 45419741816) restored nothing and failed closed: the same empty restore CACHE-DIVERGENCE-PROBE-EMPTY-RESTORE records. The four images of `Container Security` also shared the `gha` backend's default scope, `buildkit`, so each build overwrote the cache of the one before it, and only the last image's layers survived a run. Every cache a step of this repository writes is now saved by `main` only. Each one is a pair of steps with the same inputs: on `main`, `actions/cache` restores and its post step saves at the end of a job that passed, as before; on every other ref, `actions/cache/restore` restores and never saves. Each image also takes its own scope and exports its layers only on `main`. Third-party actions still save three caches from pull requests: the scanner's database and binary (TRIVY-ACTION-SAVES-ITS-CACHE-FROM-EVERY-REF in `chain.md`), and `pnpm/setup`'s `pnpm-lockfile-verified-*` entries of about 600 bytes. Where: `.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:327-328`, `:613-614`, `.github/actions/setup-node-pnpm-cache/action.yml:66-96`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:643-666`, `:949-969`, `:1088-1104`. Found by: the probe's red run on `main` (2026-11-24).
- **DEPLOYMENT-CHECKLIST-WORKFLOW-CITATIONS-NOT-HELD**: The independent verification of the Node runtime advisory unit reported that four citations of `.github/workflows/production-ci.yml` in `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md` do not show what their rows say, on `main` before that unit and after it: `:19-20`, `:207-216` and `:481` at `DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:90`, and `:462` at `:156`. The report was not re-read row by row. No check opens the citations of that document; `pnpm check:deployment`, the plan item N-CI-8 (`docs/product/plan/chain.md`), is the gate decided for it. Dormant: a reader lands on another line. Where: `docs/deployment/DEPLOYMENT_CHECKLIST.md:90`, `:156`. Done: each of the four citations is on the line that shows what its row says, or N-CI-8 holds every citation of the document. Found by: the independent verification of the Node runtime advisory unit (#963, 2026-11-20).
- **NODE-ADVISORY-OUTPUT-OF-A-FAILED-LEG-UNPROVEN**: The job `Node Runtime Advisory` reads each image's Node version from an output of its leg of the matrix job `Container Security`. GitHub's documentation says the outputs of a matrix job are combined and that an empty value does not override another leg's; it does not say whether a leg that failed or timed out after its `Node version` step still delivers its output, nor how the legs combine into `needs.<job>.result`. The job is right either way: an image with no version is named as not checked and the job is red. Dormant until a leg fails after reporting. Where: `.github/workflows/production-ci.yml:331-350`, `:628-687`. Done: a run in which one leg failed after its `Node version` step is read, and this row says whether the job judged that image or named it as not checked. Found by: the independent verification of the Node runtime advisory unit (#963, 2026-11-20).
- **ERRORS-STRUCTURAL-CODE-COLLISION**: The error registry recognises a coded error by its shape, any object with a string `code` (`isCodedError`, `packages/shared/src/error-registry/registered-error.ts:111-115`), and finds its entry by that code compared by value (`resolveError`, `:128-134`), the first step of the API's error body (`toProblem`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:286`, `:430-431`). So an error of another library whose `code` equals a registered string is answered with that entry's status and text, not its own; the registry holds generic codes such as `NOT_FOUND`, `CONFLICT`, `FORBIDDEN` and `UNAUTHORIZED` (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/common.ts:11-46`). The documentation of `resolveError` states it (`:121-123`). Live wherever an upstream error with such a code reaches the handler; none was measured. Where: the lines above. Done: The libraries whose errors can reach the handler with a string code are listed, each one that can carry a registered code is mapped before it does, with a test per library; or a recorded decision keeps the recognition by shape. Found by: the independent verification of the central error system's registry (2026-11-20).
- **FITNESS-49-INLINE-CONFIG**: Fitness #49 refuses an `eslint-disable` directive that names one of its twelve test-block rules and reads no other comment (`docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md:1783`), and the lint configuration sets no `noInlineConfig` (`eslint.config.ts`), so an inline configuration comment such as `/* eslint testing/no-empty-test: "off" */` turns a test-block rule off in its file and nothing counts it. The sites gate of fitness #57 refuses the same comment for the `errors/` rules (its rule S5). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: Fitness #49 also refuses a configuration comment that names one of its rules, with its red path shown, or the lint configuration refuses inline configuration in test files. Found by: the independent verification of the central error system's lint rule (2026-11-20).
- **FASTIFY-LOGGER-UNREDACTED**: The API builds Fastify with `logger: true` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:221`), so Fastify makes its own pino logger, with no `redact` option, and `REDACT_PATHS` of `@observability/logger`, which the API's `createLogger` applies (`apps/api/src/lib/logger.ts`), does not reach it. What is written through `app.log` or `request.log` is not redacted: Fastify's own request lines, the line of the error handler and of the not-found handler, which are given `typedApp.log` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:506-508`), and 11 more calls in 5 files of `apps/api/src` (counted on 2026-11-20 with `rg -c '\b(request|req|app|fastify|typedApp|server)\.log\.(error|warn|info|debug|fatal|trace)' apps/api/src`). The failure line of `sendProblem` holds named fields and never the raised value (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:214-227`); the other 11 calls were not read. Live at the base. Where: the lines above. Done: Fastify logs through the API's redacted logger (its `loggerInstance` option, or the same `redact` paths and censor), with a test that writes a field of `REDACT_PATHS` through `request.log` and reads its mask. Depends on: DEF-42 (one list every logger applies). Found by: the independent verification of the central error system's handler (2026-11-20).
- **SEND-PROBLEM-AFTER-A-SEND-IN-FLIGHT-REWRITES-THE-STATUS**: `sendProblem` leaves a reply as it is only when `reply.sent` is true (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:372`), and Fastify 5.12.6 reads `sent` as a hijacked reply or `raw.writableEnded`, so a route that calls `reply.send` and then fails while the `onSend` hooks of that send are pending gets the problem's status and content type over its own body. Fastify's own second `send` has the same window, and the function's documentation states it (`:337-343`). Dormant: no route is known to send and then fail, and the base had the same window. Where: the lines above. Done: A test shows which answer the client receives in that window, and either the handler leaves a send in flight alone or a recorded decision keeps the window. Found by: the independent verification of the central error system's handler (2026-11-20).
- **FRAMEWORK-REFUSALS-ANSWER-OUTSIDE-THE-PROBLEM-BODY**: Closed by #965. The API builds Fastify with `frameworkErrors` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:243`), whose handler sends each refusal the framework makes before routing through `sendProblem` (`answerFrameworkRefusal`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:146-158`): a URL whose percent-encoding does not decode answers `PATH_NOT_DECODABLE` with 400, and a path parameter longer than the router's limit answers `ROUTE_PARAMETER_TOO_LONG` with 414, in the `too_large` category the Maintainer opened to 414 on 2026-11-20 (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/http.ts:33-46`; the mapping at `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:138-139`), each in the one body, with a correlation id and its one line. Proven through `inject` by `apps/api/tests/unit/lib/errors/frameworkRefusals.test.ts:103` and `:146`. Before: the API set no `frameworkErrors`, so both refusals were answered by Fastify before any handler, in its own body `{ error, code, message, statusCode }`, the first with a message that repeated the path, and neither wrote the line of the central path. Found by: the central error system's first slice (the writers of the body and of the handler, 2026-11-20).
- **NOT-FOUND-ANSWERS-LOG-AT-WARN**: Closed by decision of the Maintainer on 2026-11-20: the severity of a failure line follows its status with no exception by code, so `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`'s line stays at warn, as the line of every registered code below 500 does (`severityOf`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:224-226`), held by `apps/api/tests/unit/lib/errors/sendProblemRealLogger.test.ts:153`; the volume of those lines is ERROR-LINES-AND-REPORTS-HAVE-NO-VOLUME-LIMIT. Fastify's own not-found handler logged a path no route matches at info; the not-found handler answers through `sendProblem` (`createNotFoundHandler`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:99-103`), so a scan of unknown paths writes one warn line per request. Found by: the central error system's first slice (the handler's writer, 2026-11-20).
- **CLIENT-ERROR-READERS-SHOW-OBJECT-OBJECT**: Two answers of the API that are not the problem body reach the client portal's screens as the wrong text. A body `{ ok: false, error: { code, message } }`, which the IP allowlist writes on every route (`apps/api/src/security/ipAllowlistMiddleware.ts:105-108`, registered at `apps/api/src/index.ts:537`), as do `apps/api/src/auth/customerOrAdminAuth.ts`, `apps/api/src/auth/customerRbacMiddleware.ts` and `apps/api/src/security/csrfMiddleware.ts`, is read by `apps/client/lib/api/clients/request.ts:40` (`body.message || body.error`) and `apps/client/lib/auth/authApi.ts:38` (`body.error ?? body.message ?? fallback`) as a message that is an object, shown as "[object Object]" with no code. The HTTP rate limiter's body carries `error: "RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED"` (`apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:183-185`), which `authApi` shows as the message "RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED" with no code, and a case of the client's suite pins that as the behaviour today (`apps/client/tests/integration/authApiErrorBody.test.ts:82`). And a code the client's catalogs do not word is shown in the registry's English `detail`: every code of the `http` domain is one, since each names no front (`fronts: []`, `packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/http.ts`), `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`, `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` and the codes of each cause of a body the server cannot read (`JSON_BODY_EMPTY`, `JSON_BODY_NOT_PARSABLE`, `BODY_LENGTH_MISMATCH`, `CONTENT_TYPE_MISSING`) among them. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: Those writers answer through `sendProblem` (API-ERROR-ENVELOPES-DIFFER-AND-ROUTE-HANDLERS-OMIT-REQUIRED-FIELDS) and the client shows its catalog's text for a code (DEF-130), with a test per body. Found by: the central error system's first slice (the readers' writer, 2026-11-20).
- **CORE-AUTH-SUITES-COPY-READINGOF**: The two suites of the first-administrator use cases each define the same helper `readingOf` (`packages/core/auth/tests/unit/CreateFirstAdministratorUseCase.test.ts:142`, `packages/core/auth/tests/unit/ReissueAdministratorPasswordUseCase.test.ts:132`), beside the shared doubles of that directory (`packages/core/auth/tests/unit/useCaseDoubles.ts`). Where: the lines above. Done: The helper lives once in `useCaseDoubles.ts` and both suites import it. Found by: the central error system's first slice (the use cases' writer, 2026-11-20).
- **TESTS-BESIDE-SOURCE-GLOBS-MATCH-A-PACKAGE-SRC-TESTS-DIRECTORY**: The exclusion `packages/*/*/tests/**` of the scope of `errors/no-inline-error` (`scripts/testing/lib/error-scope.mjs:91`) and `TESTS_BESIDE_SOURCE` of the import rule `production-imports-no-test-code` (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs:98`) mean a grouped package's `tests` directory (`packages/core/auth/tests/`), and at that depth they also match `packages/<package>/src/tests/`, a `tests` directory inside a source tree, which ADR-0047's scope leaves in the rule's reach. No such directory exists today, so it is dormant; once one does, the sites gate's S4 refuses the entry for taking files under a `src` directory and the import rule refuses an import of those files, both failing closed. Where: the lines above. Done: The two patterns name the grouped package roots (`packages/core`, `packages/providers`, `packages/adapters`, `packages/monitoring`, `packages/observability` and any other the workspace groups), so a `tests` directory inside a `src` tree is in scope, held by a case. Found by: the independent verification of units W and D of ADR-0047 (2026-11-20).
- **ERROR-LINES-AND-REPORTS-HAVE-NO-VOLUME-LIMIT**: Nothing limits how many failure lines the API writes or how many reports it sends to Sentry. Every answer of the central error path writes one line, at error with one report to Sentry when its status is 500 or above and at warn below (`severityOf`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:224-226`; the line and the report, `answerFailure`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:228-242`), and each of these paths writes one per event. A request the framework refuses before routing: one warn line (`answerFrameworkRefusal`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:146-158`, installed as `frameworkErrors` at `apps/api/src/index.ts:243`). A connection whose parser fails or whose request times out: one warn line, written by the client error handler before any hook, so the HTTP rate limit, a `preHandler` hook (`apps/api/src/index.ts:523-529`), never reaches it (`createClientErrorHandler`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:384`, installed at `apps/api/src/index.ts:246`). An answer of 500 or above: one error line and one report, a provider's outage included, since `ProviderError.externalService` raises a 502 (`packages/providers/shared/src/ProviderError.ts:73-86`) at 47 sites outside tests (`rg -c 'ProviderError\.externalService\('` over `apps` and `packages`, 2026-11-20), which the handler answers as a carrier of its status, `INTERNAL_ERROR` at error (`statusCarrierAnswer`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:407-418`), so an outage sends one report per failed request. A CONNECT request: one error line and one report, at 501, with the target the client chose as the line's `url` (`refuseTunnels`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:433-451`; `tunnelRequestOf`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:186-188`). A path no route matches: one warn line (`createNotFoundHandler`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:99-103`). Two brakes exist today: Sentry starts only once an administrator has saved a DSN (`apps/api/src/index.ts:486-499`, `apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts:113-116`), and its `beforeSend` drops an event whose exception's value contains `ECONNREFUSED` or `ENOTFOUND` (`apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts:122-131`). The dedupe of consecutive identical events, which earlier reports of the slice counted as a third brake, is not active: `Sentry.init` passes no `integrations` (`apps/api/src/observability/sentryInit.ts:118-133`), and the default integrations of `@sentry/node` 10.76.2 do not include `dedupeIntegration` (`@sentry/node-core` 10.76.2, `build/esm/sdk/index.js:23-48`, read under `node_modules/.pnpm` on 2026-11-20). Decided by the Maintainer on 2026-11-20: the severity rule has no exception by code, and the volume stays uniform now; its protection is decided when the observability work opens, with real figures of traffic and of the contracted Sentry plan. Live once the central error system's first slice is on `main`. Where: the lines above. Done: the observability work decides and builds the limit, or the Maintainer decides that none is needed. Found by: the central error system's first slice (the independent verifier's first report, two writers' reports and the native reviews of the server's error path, 2026-11-20).
- **ERROR-BODY-DERIVED-HEADERS-NOT-BUILT**: Decision 2.6 of ADR-0047 says that a response header a failure needs, `Retry-After` or `WWW-Authenticate`, is derived from the entry's category and a declared parameter, never set where the failure is raised (`docs/technical/ADR-0047-central-error-system.md:106`); no code derives one. Every answer of the central path carries the frozen empty set `NO_HEADERS` (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:105`, set by `answerOf` at `:249`), which `sendProblem` applies to the reply (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:379`) and the socket writer to its response (`:259`). So a 401 the system answers, `UNAUTHORIZED` (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/common.ts:25-31`) or a carrier of 401, has no `WWW-Authenticate`, although RFC 9110, section 15.5.2, says: "The server generating a 401 response MUST send a WWW-Authenticate header field (Section 11.6.1) containing at least one challenge applicable to the target resource."; and a carrier of 429, which the system answers `REQUEST_REFUSED` (`statusCarrierAnswer`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:407-418`; the registry holds no 429 code), has no `Retry-After`. The two answers that send `Retry-After` are written by hand, each with a body of its own, outside the central path: the HTTP rate limiter's (`apps/api/src/security/httpRateLimitPreHandler.ts:180-189`) and the administrators' sign-in limiter's (`apps/api/src/admin/auth/adminAuthMiddleware.ts:242-252`). The API sent no `WWW-Authenticate` before the slice either: no file under `apps/api/src` names the header on `main` at `e20efb6a7` (`git grep -i www-authenticate`). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the headers decision 2.6 names are derived from the entry where the answer is built, so a 401 carries a challenge and a 429 its `Retry-After`, with a case per header through `sendProblem`, and the two limiters answer through `sendProblem`; or the Maintainer amends decision 2.6. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B9, and a writer's report, 2026-11-20).
- **RAW-PRISMA-ERRORS-ANSWER-INTERNAL-ERROR**: A Prisma error that reaches the global handler unconverted answers `INTERNAL_ERROR` with 500, at error and reported to Sentry: its code is no registered code, and it is neither an `AppError` nor a carrier of a failure status, so `toProblem` reads it only at its last step (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:429-444`). A unique violation answers so, pinned by `apps/api/tests/unit/lib/errors/sendProblemRealLogger.test.ts:222-233` (`P2002`, 500, error), and the client is told that the server failed when its request conflicted with a stored row. Converters for those causes exist and nothing calls them outside their own suite: `convertPrismaError` (`P2002` to 409, `P2025` to 404, `P2003` and `P2011` to 400; `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:541`), `convertZodError` (`:591`) and `handleAnyError`, which dispatches to the two (`:613`), are called only by `apps/api/tests/unit/errorHandler.test.ts` and by each other. It answered 500 before the slice too. How often a raw Prisma error reaches the handler was not measured. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: each Prisma cause that can reach the handler answers a registered code of its own, mapped where the repository raises it or in one step of `toProblem`, with a case per cause; the three functions are wired or deleted with their suite. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap A8, 2026-11-20).
- **PROVIDER-REFUSALS-BLAME-THE-CLIENT-REQUEST**: A platform's refusal of the server's own call reaches the client as a refusal of the client's request. `ProviderError` extends `Error`, not `AppError` (`packages/providers/shared/src/ProviderError.ts:37`), and carries the status its factory gives it: 400 (`badRequest`, 1 site outside tests), 401 (`unauthorized`, 10), 404 (`notFound`, 25), 409 (`conflict`, none), 500 (`internal`, none) and 502 (`externalService`, 47) (`:73-137`; counted on 2026-11-20 with `rg -c 'ProviderError\.<factory>\('` over `apps` and `packages`, tests left out). Its code is in no domain of the registry, so the handler answers it as a carrier of its status: below 500, `REQUEST_REFUSED` at that status, whose message says the request was refused (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/http.ts:142-149`), logged at error and reported to Sentry (`statusCarrierAnswer`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:407-418`; a provider's 400 is pinned by `apps/api/tests/unit/lib/errors/problem.test.ts:620-640`). `AIProviderError` carries an upstream status the same way (`apps/api/src/ai/providers/AIProviderError.ts:23-29`). Which routes let either reach the handler was not traced. Live wherever one does. Where: the lines above. Done: each provider failure that can reach the handler answers a registered code that names the platform's failure, never the client's request, with a case per factory; or a recorded decision keeps the carrier's answer. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap A9, a writer's report and the independent verifier's second pass, 2026-11-20).
- **API-REFERENCE-ROUTES-MAY-KEEP-THE-DEFAULT-ERROR-HANDLER**: By reading, not run: the two routes of the API reference are registered before the API sets its error handler, and Fastify gives a route the handler in force when the route's registration completes, so they may keep Fastify's own. `registerApiReference` adds `GET /docs/json` and `GET /docs/openapi.yaml` to the root instance (`apps/api/src/lib/apiReference.ts:47-52`) and is called at `apps/api/src/index.ts:311`; `setErrorHandler` runs at `:507`, after the awaited registrations at `:352`, `:366`, `:473` and `:479`. Fastify 5.12.6 copies the instance's handler into the route's context in an `after` callback (`node_modules/.pnpm/fastify@5.12.6/node_modules/fastify/lib/route.js:376-379`), and `setErrorHandler` replaces the instance's handler with a new object (`node_modules/.pnpm/fastify@5.12.6/node_modules/fastify/fastify.js:781`). A failure of either route, such as `app.swagger()` raising, would then answer Fastify's own body, with no code, no correlation id and no line of the central path. Dormant: the routes exist only with `API_DOCS_ENABLED`, false by default (`apps/api/src/config/env.ts:297`). Where: the lines above. Done: a case through `inject` makes either route fail and reads its answer; if it is Fastify's own, the routes are registered after the error handler is set. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B1, 2026-11-20).
- **ROUTE-NOT-FOUND-ALSO-ANSWERS-A-METHOD-NO-ROUTE-TAKES**: One code answers two causes. `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`, 404, "No route matches this method and path" (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/http.ts:26-32`), is what the not-found handler answers for every request no route takes (`createNotFoundHandler`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/errorHandler.ts:99-103`): a path that does not exist, and a path that exists asked with a method none of its routes takes. Neither the client nor the line tells them apart, and the second gets no 405 with its `Allow` header (RFC 9110, section 15.5.6). That Fastify hands the second case to the not-found handler was not run here. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the two causes answer codes of their own, or a recorded decision keeps one code, with a case per cause through `inject`. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B2, 2026-11-20).
- **FIRST-ADMINISTRATOR-CODES-SHARE-CAUSES-THAT-CAN-BE-TOLD-APART**: The first consumers of the error registry, the first-administrator command and its use cases, answer several causes with one code each, while the Maintainer asked on 2026-11-20 that a cause that can be told apart gets a code of its own. `ENV_VARIABLES_REFUSED` says "missing or not valid" for every refused variable and does not tell which (`packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/platform.ts:10-16`; `refusedVariables`, `apps/api/src/config/env.ts:387-394`). Every rejection of the connection check is `DATABASE_UNREACHABLE` (`apps/api/src/cli/firstAdministratorCommand.ts:345-353`): a refused connection, a name that does not resolve, refused credentials, a missing database and a failed TLS handshake alike; the cause line names the driver's class and code, which for a closed port is `PrismaClientKnownRequestError`, `P2010` (`apps/api/tests/integration/firstAdministratorCommand.integration.test.ts:748`), and what the driver raises for the other causes was not measured. Every argument error but an invalid option value, an unknown option and an unexpected positional among them, is `FIRST_ADMIN_COMMAND_OPTION_UNKNOWN`, and `FIRST_ADMIN_COMMAND_OPTION_VALUE_INVALID` covers both an invalid option value and an empty `--email` (`parseCommandArguments`, `apps/api/src/cli/firstAdministratorCommand.ts:168-176`, `:199-204`); neither names the option. `SINGLE_USE_PASSWORD_STEP_RAISED` answers a step that raised inside the transaction of the creation and of the re-issue alike, and names no step (`runInTransaction`, `packages/core/auth/src/singleUsePassword.ts:116-120`). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: each cause the code or the driver can tell apart has a code of its own or a declared parameter that names it, with a case per cause; or a recorded decision keeps a shared code. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gaps B3 to B6, 2026-11-20).
- **FAILURE-LINE-NAMES-NO-ROUTE-PRINCIPAL-OR-ISSUES**: The failure line of the central error path names a request by its method and its raw URL and by nothing else of what was asked or by whom: its fields are the logged and the wire code, the status, the source, the request's id, the correlation id, `method`, `url`, `ip`, `ipSource` and the error's projection (`apps/api/src/lib/errors/sendProblem.ts:211-227`). It carries no route pattern, so the failures of one route under different identifiers share no field; no user or account id, so a failure is not tied to who made it; and no validation issues: a request a route's schema refuses gets its issues in the body (`validationAnswer`, `apps/api/src/lib/errors/problem.ts:329-348`), and the line has them only inside the error's message. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the line names the matched route pattern, the authenticated principal when there is one, and a refused request's issues as fields of their own, with a case per field; or a recorded decision leaves one out. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gaps B7 and B8, 2026-11-20).
- **AUTH-REGISTRY-WORDING-IS-NOT-UNIFORM**: The 33 codes of the registry's `auth` domain are written in more than one form: 10 messages end with a period and 23 do not, three begin with a lower-case letter (`FIRST_ADMIN_COMMAND_SETUP_FAILED`, `FIRST_ADMINISTRATOR_NO_ANSWER` and `PASSWORD_REISSUE_NO_ANSWER`, `packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/auth.ts:224-254`), and the codes of one feature carry two prefixes, `FIRST_ADMIN_COMMAND_` (`:180-238`) and `FIRST_ADMINISTRATOR_` (`:68`, `:75`, `:239`). None of the 33 declares a parameter (`params: {}` 33 times), so a message that could name what failed, such as an option, says it in fixed words. The other three domains, 35 codes, end no message with a period and begin none in lower case (counted on 2026-11-20). The command prints one form after the other: `Failed: The environment was refused…` and `Failed: the command could not be set up…` (`apps/api/tests/unit/cli/firstAdministratorCommand.test.ts:767`, `:783`). The decision record gives a message's content (`docs/technical/ADR-0047-central-error-system.md:56`), and no check holds its form. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: one form of a message and one prefix per feature, held by the registry's gate, with the `auth` domain brought to them; or a recorded decision. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B11, 2026-11-20).
- **PORTAL-PROXIES-ANSWER-THEIR-OWN-503-BODY**: When the API cannot be reached, both portals' proxies answer 503 with a body of their own, `{ ok: false, error: "Backend unavailable" }`, with no code of the registry and no correlation id, and log nothing, each in a `catch` that binds no error: the client portal's (`apps/client/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:116-117`) and the administration portal's, on its first send and on its retry after a token refresh (`apps/admin/app/api/backend/[...path]/route.ts:125-126`, `:151-152`). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the two proxies answer the one error body with a registered code for an API they cannot reach, and log the failure, with a case per proxy. Found by: the central error system's first slice (its reading for uniformity and granularity, gap B12, 2026-11-20).
- **SERVER-TIMEOUT-COMMENT-PROMISES-A-BOUND-NO-TIMEOUT-GIVES**: The comment above the API's server timeouts says that `keepAliveTimeout` and `requestTimeout` "Together they prevent socket hoarding and indefinite request hangs" (`apps/api/src/index.ts:235-240`), and no timeout the server sets bounds a route that never answers: `requestTimeout` bounds the time to receive a request, and the server's options set no other (`apps/api/src/index.ts:220-250`). Measured with probes by a writer and the independent verifier of the central error system's first slice on 2026-11-20, and not run here: Node checks its request timeouts every 30 seconds, so the 30-second `requestTimeout` was seen 30 to 60 seconds late, and a request whose body stopped arriving was ended after about 60 seconds, by Node's `headersTimeout`; a pipelined request whose body stopped arriving behind an answered request was not timed out within 6 seconds under a `requestTimeout` of 1 second, and its connection ended only when the client left (not investigated further). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the comment says what each timeout bounds, and the Maintainer decides whether a route that never answers, and a connection held as above, gets a bound, with a case per bound. Found by: the central error system's first slice (two writers' reports and the independent verifier's first, fourth and fifth passes, 2026-11-20).
- **UNTIL-TIMEOUT-INERT-UNDER-A-FAKED-DATE**: `until` of `@packages/vitest-shared/determinism` reads its deadline from `Date.now()` and pauses with the global `setTimeout` (`packages/vitest-shared/src/determinism/until.ts:62-71`), and its header says only that under fake timers a waiter moves as far as the test advances the clock (`:9-11`). A suite that fakes `Date` alone keeps `setTimeout` real, so the waiter polls under a frozen clock and its `timeoutMs` never fires: a condition that never comes true ends at vitest's own test timeout, "Test timed out", with no description of what was awaited. `apps/api/tests/unit/lib/errors/clientErrors.test.ts` fakes `Date` alone (`:436`) and waits with `until` (`:732`, `:760`, `:831`, `:869`). No case there relies on `until` rejecting, so nothing passes falsely; only the failure's message is lost. Live in that suite. Where: the lines above. Done: `until` measures its deadline with a clock fake timers do not replace, or refuses to run under a faked `Date`, with a case; or the suite stops faking `Date` around its waits. Found by: the central error system's first slice (a writer's report and the independent verifier's fourth pass, 2026-11-20).
- **ENCRYPTION-AUDIT-EMIT-READS-ITS-CONTEXT-FOR-NOTHING**: `EncryptionService.emitAudit` calls `getRequestAuditContext()` and discards the result (`void getRequestAuditContext();`, `apps/api/src/security/EncryptionService.ts:256`), under a comment that the call keeps the request's context alive when the event is emitted (`:254-255`). The getter only reads the store (`apps/api/src/security/decryptAuditContext.ts:59-61`), so the statement does nothing. The audit port reads the context itself (`apps/api/src/audit/auditService.ts:403-404`), which a hook fills for every request (`apps/api/src/index.ts:544-557`). Dormant: dead code under a comment that claims an effect. Where: the lines above. Done: the statement and its comment leave. Found by: the central error system's first slice (a writer's report, 2026-11-20).
- **INSTRUCTION-CONTEXT-BUDGET-NINE-BYTES-FROM-ITS-CEILING**: The auto-loaded instruction files hold 135,614 bytes against a ceiling of 135,623 (`budget.bytes`, `.claude/instruction-manifest.json:34`), as `node scripts/testing/instruction-context-gate.mjs` printed on 2026-11-20. A change that adds more than 9 bytes to one of the five auto-loaded files fails fitness #55 unless the same change removes as much from them or raises `budget.bytes` by hand, the visible edit the budget's rule asks for any growth (`docs/technical/ADR-0045-instruction-context-budget.md:162-169`). The central error system's first slice met the ceiling: to add its sentence on `resolveClientAddress` to `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md`, it took out the canon's backlog note on a display of the proxy topology, which is now TRUSTED-PROXY-TOPOLOGY-HAS-NO-OPERATOR-DISPLAY in [areas.md](areas.md). Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the Maintainer decides how the next change to an auto-loaded file gets its room: material leaves those files for documents read on demand, as TOKEN-1 did, or `budget.bytes` is raised by a recorded decision. Found by: the central error system's first slice (a writer's report, 2026-11-20).
- **STALL-MODE-EXIT-CODES-SHARE-TWO-MEANINGS**: Closed by #987. The Product Owner, who also holds the Maintainer role, chose A on 2026-11-24: the stall mode exits with `STALL_EXIT` of `scripts/testing/error-sites-gate.mjs`, one code per meaning: 0 not stalled, 20 stalled, 21 a list above zero its history does not date, 22 a newest row dated after today, 23 a suppressions file that cannot be read, 24 a history that cannot be read, its directory or an entry, 25 a call GitHub refused (a 4xx other than 408 and 429 that names no rate limit, `gh`'s exit 4 for a missing authentication, or "Resource not accessible by" the token, with or without a status), 26 a `gh` the runner cannot start (`ENOENT`, `EACCES`), 27 a GitHub call that failed for a moment (a 5xx, a 408, a 429, a rate limit on a 403 or a 429, a network error, a timeout, an answer that cannot be read, or a failure with no status and no mark of a refusal), the one code the workflow retries, and 28 an issue listing that fills its page, so the stall's issue could lie past it. Every verdict sits above the codes Node exits with by itself, and no path of the mode exits 3. A fault of the mode, one inside the reminder included, is left uncaught, so Node exits 1 with its stack, and the workflow gives each code a branch that names its state, ends each in an exit, and reads 1, 3, or any code it does not know, as the alarm itself failing. `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/errorSitesGate.test.ts` runs the gate as a process once per meaning and once per fault, and pins the workflow's retry and its exits. The lines the record below cites are those of `c4317fc20`. In the stall mode of the error sites gate, which the workflow `error-sites-stall.yml` runs every day, two exit codes mean two things each. Exit 1 is the stall signal (`.github/workflows/error-sites-stall.yml:46-48`), and it is also Node's own exit code for a script that fails to load or throws before it decides, so such a failure takes the workflow's stalled branch, which reports a stalled list (`:64-66`), while no reminder issue is opened. Exit 3 covers a GitHub call that failed for a moment and also two states of the data that persist, a list above zero whose history holds no row and a newest row dated after today (`scripts/testing/error-sites-gate.mjs:110-113`), and the workflow retries every exit 3 twice more, 30 then 60 seconds apart (`.github/workflows/error-sites-stall.yml:49-50`, `:56-61`): 90 seconds spent on a state no retry changes. Options: A, the stall and the persistent states get exit codes of their own, with their workflow branches and cases, which needs a change of `.github/workflows/error-sites-stall.yml`; B, keep 1 and 3 as the workflow's comment documents them (`:46-54`). Dormant until the script fails to load or the history reaches one of those states. Where: the lines above. Done: the Maintainer chooses A or B, and A is built with its cases. Found by: the native reviews of the central error system's first commits (`ef7` and `ef9`, 2026-11-20).
- **LEDGER-SUBJECT-HEURISTIC-TAKES-A-REGISTRY-DOMAIN-FOR-A-TEST-SUBJECT**: The test ledger names a test's subject by the test's file name before its first dot: a module of that name the test imports, else the one module of that name in the test's own workspace, else the one in a workspace its manifest declares (`subjectOf`, `scripts/testing/lib/ledger-imports.mjs:335-349`). When the error registry gained `packages/shared/src/error-registry/domains/auth.ts`, the ledger took it for the subject of `apps/api/tests/auth.integration.test.ts`, which never imports it, and that file's machine verdict turned from `KEEP` (basis `behavioural`, on `main` at `e20efb6a7`) to `ADJUDICATE` with the basis `subject-not-imported` (`ledger.json:3856-3863`; rule 8 of `verdictOf`, `scripts/testing/lib/ledger-core.mjs:373`). A module added anywhere a test's workspace depends on can turn a test's verdict with no change to the test. Live. Where: the lines above. Done: the subject rule takes no module that the test neither imports nor names by its path, or a recorded decision keeps the heuristic and this file is adjudicated, with a case of the ledger's suite either way. Found by: the native review of the central error system's registry commit (`ef5`, 2026-11-20).
- **COVERAGE-FLOOR-GATE-COMPARES-WITH-THE-TIP-OF-MAIN**: Fitness #37 compares the coverage floors of `HEAD` with the commit its base names, never with the merge base of the two: the base is `FITNESS_BASE_REF`, `origin/main` when unset, resolved with `git rev-parse` and nothing more (`scripts/testing/coverage-floor-gate.mjs:497`, `:506`). The local battery runs the gate with no base (`scripts/testing/battery.sh:216`), so on a branch that lags `main`, a floor `main` raised after the branch point reads as a descent of the branch, and the battery fails for a change the branch never made. In CI the step is given the pull request's base commit (`.github/workflows/fitness.yml:986`). The record-coverage gate resolves its base to the merge base (`scripts/testing/record-coverage-gate.mjs:210`), and the battery hands the errors step the merge base (`scripts/testing/battery.sh:206-207`). Live in the local battery. Where: the lines above. Done: the floors gate compares with the merge base of `HEAD` and its base, or the battery passes it one, with a case of a branch that lags its base. Found by: the correction of the battery's base for the errors step, in the central error system's first slice (2026-11-20).
- **ERRORS-RULE-MISSES-A-REPLY-HELPER-WITH-A-PRIVATE-NAME**: The lint rule `errors/no-inline-error` reads a reply helper's name in an identifier and in a member read under a computed key (`packages/eslint-plugin-errors/src/rules/no-inline-error.js:890-905`), so a helper declared with a private name such as `#sendError`, whose name is a `PrivateIdentifier`, is reported neither where it is declared nor where it is called as `this.#sendError()`; the rule's header states the gap (`:98-99`). A helper's name written as a string is seen only as the key of a member read (`this["sendError"]`): as a quoted or bracketed key of an object's property or method, of a destructured property, of a class member or of a signature it is not seen (the header, `:99-102`); a copy of the rule widened to report those keys gave the same number of reports over the tree's 3,407 tracked TypeScript files as the rule itself, so it can see them with no count of the list moving. No reply helper of the tree has a private name: a search for `#` followed by a name of the rule's `replyHelpers` option (`scripts/testing/lib/error-scope.mjs:146-153`) over `apps`, `packages` and `infra` found none on 2026-11-20. Dormant. Where: the lines above. Done: the rule reads a private name and a string key as it reads any other, with a case for a private helper's declaration, one for its call and one for a string key. Found by: the correction of the rule's exemption of a helper's declaration, in the central error system's first slice (2026-11-20).
- **DESTROYED-QUEUED-RESPONSE-HOLDS-ITS-CONNECTION**: A response destroyed while it waits behind an earlier response of the same connection holds that connection for as long as its client stays connected, whether a failure follows it or not. As a writer of the central error system's first slice reported, `destroy()` on a response that has no socket yet only emits `close`; when the response later gets the socket, its buffered bytes are written and it never finishes, so Node neither reuses nor closes the connection. Fastify destroys a response when the stream a route returned fails after the head was sent (`node_modules/.pnpm/fastify@5.12.6/node_modules/fastify/lib/reply.js:849-857`, and `:826-833` for a web stream), and a route that hijacks its response may destroy it itself; either does it behind a slower pipelined request. Measured by the independent verification on 2026-11-20 (its probe 14 held the connection 20 seconds, with a failure behind the response and without one), and not run here. When a refusal of the error system follows, the error system closes the connection after writing it; with no failure the error system is never called. It is Node's and Fastify's behaviour, not a change of the slice. Live, on a connection that pipelines requests. Where: the lines above. Done: the API ends a connection whose queued response was destroyed, or a recorded decision of the Maintainer leaves it to the client, with a case either way. Found by: the central error system's first slice (a writer's report and the independent verifier's sixth pass, 2026-11-20).
- **ABSENT-PEER-ADDRESS-IS-LABELLED-PEER**: The failure line's `ipSource` says where its `ip` comes from: `forwarded` when a trusted proxy reported an address other than its own, `peer` otherwise. When the connection's peer cannot be read, the resolver returns the placeholder `"unknown"` with the source `peer` (`apps/api/src/security/resolveClientIp.ts:47`, `:133-142`), which decision 2.9 of ADR-0047 states as the design; the independent verification of the slice read the pair as misleading, since no peer was read. Dormant: a connection with no readable peer address is not expected on a TCP listener. Where: the lines above. Done: the Maintainer decides whether an absent peer keeps the source `peer` or takes one of its own, and the resolver, its suite and decision 2.9 say the same. Found by: the independent verification of the error system's server slice (2026-11-20).
- **TWO-OPEN-PLAN-ROWS-CITE-WHAT-MOVED-OR-LEFT**: Two open rows of the plan give evidence the tree no longer holds, and both were so on the main branch before the central error system's first slice. DEF-25 (`docs/product/plan/areas.md:598`) cites `apps/api/src/index.ts:358-367`, `:351`, `:368-375` and `:1353-1354` for the publish queue the root builds from `new URL(getRedisUrl())`; that code is in `apps/api/src/infrastructure/container/registerBootstrapConnections.ts:53-56` and the root only calls `createAdminPublishQueue` (`apps/api/src/index.ts:395`), so the defect may stand while its citations do not. API-TESTS-TYPECHECK-BASELINE-STALE (`docs/product/plan/cross-cutting.md:382`) cites three entries of `tests/unit/auditService.stats.test.ts` at `apps/api/tests-typecheck-baseline.json:203-205`; the baseline holds two other codes for that file (`:200-201`) since `4592d53d3` lowered it, so the row's example is gone while the ratchet it describes still exits 0 on a count that falls. Live (documentation). Where: the lines above. Done: each row is read against the code again and rewritten with evidence that holds, or closed. Found by: the citation sync of the central error system's first slice (2026-11-20).
- **REGISTRY-GATE-READS-A-FAULT-OF-THE-HISTORY-READER-AS-AN-UNREADABLE-HISTORY**: The registry gate reads the history of existing sites through the sites gate's own reader, and one `catch` around the whole reading reports whatever it throws as `scripts/testing/error-sites-history: could not be read, so the page's existing sites were not read` (`scripts/testing/error-registry-gate.mjs:671-676`), so a programming error inside the reader's `stateOf` (`:667-668`) is printed as an input that could not be read, with the first line of its reason and no stack. The exit is the fail-closed one either way, so nothing passes that should not; what is lost is the difference between a bad history and a fault of the gate. The same gate's entry now prints the stack of a failure it does not name (`:80-81` states what stays outside it: a failure to load the gate's own imports, which exits with Node's code 1, the question the row STALL-MODE-EXIT-CODES-SHARE-TWO-MEANINGS settled for the sites gate's stall mode). Dormant: measured with a reader made to fail on purpose by the independent verification of the gates' corrections. Where: the lines above. Done: a fault of the reader that is not an unreadable input is printed with its stack, with a case; or a recorded decision leaves it. Found by: the independent verification of the corrections the native reviews asked of the central error system's first slice (2026-11-20).
- **CVE-FLOORS-HELD-BY-THE-LOCKFILE-ALONE-HAVE-NO-CHECK**: 23 rows of §"CVE-floor pins" in `docs/security/DEPENDENCY_AUDIT_RECORD.md` hold their floor as "lockfile only (natural resolution, NO override)" (measured 2026-11-24 with `rg -c 'lockfile only \(natural resolution, NO override\)'` on that file, every hit a table row, `brace-expansion` to `follow-redirects`), while `docs/security/dependency-audit-policy.md:9-11` says an advisory with an available fix is fixed "by upgrading the direct dependency or pinning the patched transitive version in the `overrides` block". These floors are in neither, and no gate reads them: the override-bands gate reads only `overrides:`, the holds gate measures only the testing and quality toolchain and states that it reads no CVE floor (`scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs:57-62`), and `pnpm audit` sees only the advisories npm's bulk endpoint lists, which leaves out the repository advisories some of these rows answer. So a re-resolution that moves a copy below its floor fails no gate where a consumer's range admits it: the record's own rows name two, `dompurify` (`^3.4.17` under the floor 3.4.18, GHSA-qm7q-q8h9-jg8q, which GitHub's global endpoint answers 404 for and OSV does not list; its return to 3.4.17 would fail one case of `apps/api/tests/unit/security/sanitizerOutputs.test.ts`, which pins an output that release changed and not the floor) and `undici` (`^8.12.3` under 8.12.4, the `qs` shape). Where: `docs/security/DEPENDENCY_AUDIT_RECORD.md` §"CVE-floor pins", `docs/security/dependency-audit-policy.md:9-11`, `scripts/testing/override-bands-gate.mjs`, `scripts/testing/holds-gate.mjs:57-62`. Done: each such floor is held by a check that fails when the tree resolves below it, or a recorded decision of the Maintainer keeps the convention. Found by: the independent verification of the dompurify floor (2026-11-24).
- **INSTRUCTIONS-NAME-TOOLS-THE-WORK-DOES-NOT-USE**: Closed by #988. Found by the maturity audit of 2026-11-25, which measured what each tool gave. Two sets of tooling were named by the instruction files and not used by the work. (1) 15 specialist agent definitions under `.claude/agents/`, with their handoff config `.claude/agents/config/agents_config.json`, which `CLAUDE.md` §Multi-Agent Strategy told a session to use: over the 14 days measured (325 session transcripts, 857 delegations) no delegation used one, since the orchestrator delegates to general work agents under the model table. (2) The graphify knowledge graphs, which `CLAUDE.md` §Knowledge Graph said reflect HEAD through `.husky/post-commit`: `graphify-out/` is git-ignored and existed only in the main checkout, which never commits, so the hook never fired there and the graphs were between 10 and 61 days old on 2026-11-25; rebuilt at `8f72ce32b`, they gave 9 of the 601 pieces of evidence (1.5%) that eight audited features counted by tool, against 510 from text search and direct reading. The Product Owner decided on 2026-11-25 to remove both. This pull request deletes the 16 agent files, the two sections of `CLAUDE.md`, the hook, `.graphifyignore`, the ignore lines of `.gitignore` and `.dockerignore`, and the `graphify-out` token of four scripts under `scripts/`. Kept: the allowlist entry of `.gitleaks.toml` for one deleted agent file, because the scan on push reads the whole history; `.claude/commands/canon-check.md`, which the tripwire hook names as its way out.
- **HOOK-GUARDS-CRASH-AND-MISS-WRITES**: Closed by #993. Found by the review of the hook layer the Product Owner asked for on 2026-11-25. Three defects of the session guards. (1) The record guard crashed on an argument longer than a file name can be: `repository_of` (`.claude/hooks-py/_common.py`) probed every argument with `exists()`, which raised, and the guard refused the command; 13 of its 64 refusals since 2026-11-18 were that crash. (2) `.husky/` was not a guarded path, so a session could edit the gate every commit passes through with no sensitive-edit window, and that gate called lint-staged through the package runner pre-bash forbids. (3) The patterns of `.claude/hooks-py/pre_bash.py` missed a git write reached past a global option (`git -C <dir> checkout -- <guarded path>`), an in-place edit called through a path, and a forbidden package tool inside a subshell or a substitution. This pull request: `repository_of` skips a name too long to be a file and raises any other failure; `/.husky/` joins the guarded patterns and the gate runs `pnpm exec lint-staged`; pre-bash keeps its old reading for every line and adds a reading by words on top, where a script it cannot tokenize is judged by mention on its own text. Over the 28,754 commands the hook judged in 14 days, none moves from refused to allowed and seven move from allowed to refused. The rule the Maintainer set on 2026-11-25 for the whole layer: the hooks are improved or made to work, never relaxed.
- **HOOK-LAYER-ITEMS-OF-THE-2026-11-25-REVIEW**: The same review left items that make the hooks do what they claim; none of them relaxes one. (a) Real writes neither reading of pre-bash sees, measured by an independent verifier with both the old and the new code: `perl -pi`, `sed --in-place`, `git reset --hard`, `git mv`, `git stash pop`, `cp -t`, `install -t`, `patch`, `curl -o`, `tar -x -C`, `unzip -d`. (b) pre-bash exits without the refusing code when it crashes or times out, so the command runs unjudged; failing closed there stops every command on a fault of the hook itself, which is the trade to decide. (c) `CLAUDE.md` §Tripwires says rows 4 and 5 are blocked automatically, and no hook implements either. (d) `.claude/hooks-py/stop.py` audits the main checkout, which no session edits, and has refused nothing since 2026-09-24. (e) The status line of `.claude/hooks-py/user_prompt_submit.py` reads the main checkout and not the session's worktree, and has printed a stale-index notice at every prompt since 2026-11-19. (f) `.claude/hooks.log` holds 154 MB and is never rotated. (g) The hook tests run in the local battery (`scripts/testing/battery.sh`) and in no CI job. (h) The lint-staged tasks live in `package.json`, which a guard by path cannot isolate. (i) The plan-mode guard is satisfied by reading any plan file, and its core has no test. (j) The scans the hooks share in `.claude/hooks-py/_common.py` to read heredocs and nested shells grow faster than the command: measured as real processes on 2026-11-25, pre-bash takes 28 seconds on 8 KB of matched heredoc operators on one line, more than 150 seconds on 16 KB of the same, and 53 seconds on 64 KB of repeated shell words, where an ordinary call takes 38 milliseconds; a hook that runs out of time does not refuse, so such a command would run unjudged. (k) What follows the document-read budget: a check of a delegation's brief (a document above the budget named with no extract and no range), and a report at a unit's close that flags an agent above a token ceiling. Two items the guard's own review left: a `sed -n` script with a quit command is not counted, where clipping the printed range at the quit line would count it; and no test rewrites a document with the same size and another modification time. Where: `.claude/hooks-py/`, `.husky/pre-commit`, `CLAUDE.md` §Tripwires. Done: each item is built, or closed by a decision of the Maintainer. Found by: the review of the hook layer of 2026-11-25 and the independent verification of the pull request that closes HOOK-GUARDS-CRASH-AND-MISS-WRITES.
- **DOCUMENT-READS-HAVE-NO-BUDGET**: Closed by #994. Found by the Product Owner on 2026-11-25, who asked why a documentation step was estimated at 3.5 to 5 million tokens and then called the method inefficient: whole documents were handed to agents and read again on every pass. Measured the same day over 14 days of transcripts: 4,642 reads of a document above 40,000 bytes, which put 4.39 million tokens in a context once and cost up to 9% of all tokens counting every later turn that carried them. The rule he set for all work: a document is indexed and queried, and a model receives only the rows it must judge. This pull request adds the guard he asked for so the rule cannot be forgotten: `.claude/hooks-py/pre_read_document_budget.py`, a PreToolUse hook on `Read` and `Bash` that keeps, per session and per agent, the line ranges read of each document above 40,000 bytes and refuses the read that would take them past that budget; a search or a computation is never counted; on a fault of its own it allows the call ([ADR-0048](../../technical/ADR-0048-document-read-budget.md)). A Bash command is judged only when it is plain (no subshell, group, substitution, heredoc or nested shell); any other is allowed. Replayed over the 29,252 Bash commands the hooks logged in 14 days, it would have refused 5 to 16, on six documents: it stops the whole read, which was rare, and leaves the small ranged reads that make most of that 9%. What follows it is in HOOK-LAYER-ITEMS-OF-THE-2026-11-25-REVIEW, items (j) and (k).

- **AREA-DOCUMENTS-HOLDING-CANON-RULES-HAVE-NO-ANTI-DELETION-GATE**: Fitness #24 holds the four canon documents and their four companions (`.github/workflows/fitness.yml:584-622`: it lists `docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md`, `docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md`, `docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md` and `docs/observability/LOGGING_CANON.md` at `.github/workflows/fitness.yml:600-603` and each one's `_REFERENCE.md`), and the rules of one area now live in other documents that the canon reaches by a pointer line: `docs/architecture/SAGA_CANON.md` (`docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE_CANON.md:117`), `docs/frontend/REACT_STANDARDS.md` (`docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md:183`), `docs/standards/frontend-standards.md` (`docs/development/CODING_STANDARDS.md:188`), `docs/architecture/schema-conventions.md` (`CLAUDE.md:33`) and `docs/security/dependency-audit-policy.md` (`docs/security/SECURITY_CANON.md:136`). So deleting one of the five documents fails that job while a pointer to it stands, and passes when the pointer goes with it, and emptying a document or deleting a rule inside it fails nothing. Done: a fitness step asserts that each of the five documents exists with its `**Owner:**` line and a named heading per rule family it holds, its red path proven, or the Maintainer decides the link check is enough. Found by: the context diet, T3, the independent verifier (2026-11-26).
- **INLINE-FITNESS-STEPS-HAVE-NO-TEST-OF-THEIR-RUN-BODY**: The red paths of an inline fitness step are proven once by hand and pinned by no test, except fitness #24's. Its step's body, `.github/workflows/fitness.yml:587-622`, runs under `apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/canonChildDocsStep.test.ts`: the suite reads the `run: |` body of the one step whose name starts `#24 ` from the workflow when it runs, refuses any other form of the step, a `shell:` on it, an `env:` or a `container:` of its job, a `runs-on:` that is not a GitHub-hosted Ubuntu runner, an `env:` of the workflow and a `defaults:` in it, runs the body with `bash -e`, the shell Actions gives a step that names none, in a temporary copy of the eight files, and expects exit 0 with no annotation on the clean copy, exit 1 with the named `::error` line for each clause (a missing child, a child with no `**Owner:**` line or with `**Owner:` unclosed, a missing companion, a companion with no `## How to extend` or with text after it, a child and its companion missing together), a case for each limit of block `# 24.` of `docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`, and two for limit (5), whose CRLF companion fails and whose CRLF child passes. That suite is the pattern, and FP-049 of `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md` states it. The inventory marks 41 checks `shell` (`rg -c '^- \*\*#[0-9]+\*\* .* — shell$' docs/development/FITNESS_FUNCTIONS.md`), so 40 inline steps still have no test of their body. The suite's reader of a step lives in that file alone: the reader of the inventory gate (`scripts/testing/fitness-inventory-gate.mjs`, #44) keeps of a step its name and the lines of its block scalars, not its keys, so it cannot refuse a `shell:`. Where: the lines above. Done: every inline step's body runs under a test of that form, or the step becomes a script with its own suite (FP-004); the second such test moves the reader of a step and the runner of its body into one test module that both suites import, never a copy, and gives a `runs-on:` whose value is not on the key's line (a block list or a mapping) its own reason and its own case: the reader refuses that form today as a job that runs on an empty label (`apps/api/tests/unit/scripts/canonChildDocsStep.test.ts:232-235`; finding R3-001 of the suite's native review, SB-928 of `docs/reports/review-suggestions-book.md`). Found by: the context diet, T3, the independent verifier (2026-11-26).
- **FP-046-AND-FP-047-ARE-LISTED-UNDER-CI-AND-GATES-AND-FILED-UNDER-DOCUMENTATION**: The summary table of `docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md` puts FP-046 and FP-047 in the area "CI and gates" (`docs/development/FOUNDATION_PRACTICES.md:52-53`), while their entries sit under `## Documentation`, which starts at `:404` (`### FP-046` at `:448`, `### FP-047` at `:455`); `## CI and gates` runs from `:224` to `:343`. A reader who follows the table to the area's section does not find them. Where: the lines above. Done: the table and the sections agree, by moving the two entries to `## CI and gates` or the two rows to the Documentation area, and a check holds the table's area against the section of each entry. Found by: the context diet, T3, the writer of the gates, T3b (2026-11-26).
- **PLAN-IS-NOT-YET-THE-ONLY-SOURCE**: The plan's files still lean on the older planning documents. Measured on 2026-11-26: 568 tags of the form `MP:<n>` point at lines of the earlier plan (246 in `areas.md`, 146 in this file, 131 in `chain.md`, 45 in `off-the-map.md`); 192 lines of 45 files outside the plan cite an older document, and 41 of the ids they cite have no row in the plan; no feature carries its level of need. Its first step is pull request 999: the plan is read by a parser (`scripts/plan/`), so that what follows is worked by query and not by reading the files again. Where: `docs/product/plan/areas.md`, `docs/product/plan/chain.md`, `docs/product/plan/cross-cutting.md`, `docs/product/plan/off-the-map.md`. Done: no tag, heading, mention or citation of an older planning document remains in the plan's files or in the rest of the tree; every feature carries its level of need; the older documents sit in an archive folder that nothing references. Found by: the Product Owner (2026-11-25).
- **INVOKEDASSCRIPT-IS-COPIED-IN-ELEVEN-SCRIPTS**: The function that tells whether a script runs as a command or is imported, `invokedAsScript`, is written again in each script that needs it: eleven copies. Where: `scripts/plan/plan-query.mjs:327`, `scripts/testing/battery-static.mjs:118`, `scripts/testing/battery-state.mjs:275`, `scripts/testing/coverage-floor-gate.mjs:545`, `scripts/testing/error-registry-gate.mjs:1548`, `scripts/testing/error-sites-gate.mjs:2661`, `scripts/testing/fitness-inventory-gate.mjs:1029`, `scripts/testing/node-runtime-advisory-gate.mjs:403`, `scripts/testing/plan-closure-gate.mjs:397`, `scripts/testing/record-coverage-gate.mjs:381`, `scripts/testing/sentry-init-probe.mjs:359`. - **SCHEDULING-FEATURES-CARRY-NO-WRITTEN-ID**: The fifteen features of the Scheduling area are written with no id, where the other areas write one, in four shapes (`A-TEAM-24`, `C-11`, `AP-33`, a bare ordinal). The id a query gives a Scheduling feature, `A-SCHEDULING-<n>`, is therefore its position in the table: a row inserted above moves every id below it, and whatever cites one of them then points at another feature. Where: `docs/product/plan/areas.md:217`. Done: every feature of the plan carries a written id of one shape, and a query returns that id. Found by: the writer and the verifier of the plan's parser (2026-11-26).
- **REFOUNDATION-UNITS-IN-PROSE-BELONG-TO-NO-RECORD**: Two areas attach units of the testing refoundation as a sentence of prose ("Testing refoundation units: …") and not as tags of a row, so a query of the plan by unit id does not find them. Where: `docs/product/plan/areas.md:328`, `docs/product/plan/areas.md:544`. Done: each unit named there is the tag of a row, or the sentence is gone because its units have a home.
